# Killer560's Mod - Full Feature Descriptions

The detailed version of the feature list in the README. Keep this file up to date whenever a feature is added or changed - the public features Doc is generated from it.

> **Menu:** the mod menu (`/killer560`) has a header bar (the mod's version and build, a search box, and buttons for the Discord, the GitHub page and this document), a sidebar of categories - Home, Favorites, Dungeons, HUD, Chat, Items, Social, Cosmetics, Misc and Profiles - and a grid of feature cards. Every feature is its own card: its name, one line about it, and its on/off switch (a feature that is off has a dimmed card); only Secrets and Puzzle Solvers are grouped, as titled runs of cards inside Dungeons. Inside a category the cards are in alphabetical order by name (ignoring case), with the cheat build's red Cheat block after them in the same order; Secrets and Puzzle Solvers are placed by their own names and list their sections alphabetically too (Puzzle Solvers lists its solvers alphabetically). Inside a drawer the feature's on/off switch is the first row. A feature that is really several features side by side (Dungeon Queue, Dungeon Alerts, Dungeon ESP, Wither Dragons, Leap Menu, Croesus, Experiments, Inventory Theme, F7 Spots, P3 Nav, Maxor's Crystals, Thorn and, in the cheat build, Auto Blood Camp, Auto Terminals, Interactive Map and Sharp Shooter) has a switch for each, and its card and drawer switch are ON while any of them is on and OFF only when all are; with everything off, pressing the card turns on only the first one, and with something on it turns every one off. Clicking a card opens that feature's settings in a drawer on the right, with its own switch, a star and a close button (the close button closes the drawer; Escape closes the menu, and the menu opens again on the same drawer, scrolled where you left it); every setting is a row with its label on the left and its switch, value or slider on the right, and Health and Mana Bars gets Basic and Advanced tabs for its Advanced section. The star on a card or in a drawer adds the feature to Favorites, which lists them as cards and, on Home, as a column of live switches; favorites are kept across restarts and in profiles. Ctrl F (or clicking the search box) searches every feature and setting, short names included ("etable" finds the Experimentation Table features): the arrow keys pick a result, Enter opens its drawer scrolled to the setting, and a feature result's switch turns it on or off in place. Home shows the version, the update channel and the last update check, buttons for Edit HUD, Edit Bars, Check Updates, Discord, GitHub and this document, the Menu Theme row, and cards for its own settings (Profiles is in the sidebar, and Favorites has its own page). Menu Theme (on Home as a row of buttons whose Customize... opens its settings, and as the round button at the right of the header: left click the next, right click the previous) picks the menu's colors: Amber (the orange look, the default), Dark (neutral grays), Light (light panels and dark text), Ocean (teal on navy), Viridian (leaf green on a deep forest green, with a gradient panel background that fades from forest green to near black under a soft glow from the top center; its name is a placeholder and will be renamed) or Custom, where the accent, panel background, card background, text and on-switch colors are each set in the color picker; changing a color starts Custom from the preset you were on, and Reset puts that preset's colors back. Save as New Theme keeps the colors on screen as a saved theme (Custom 1, Custom 2...); each saved theme has Use, a name box to rename it and Delete (a second click confirms), and while one is in use, changing a color changes that theme. Saved themes are kept across restarts and in profiles. Everything in the menu follows the theme - header, sidebar, cards, drawer, switches, value pills, sliders, the search and the tooltips - and it is kept across restarts and in profiles. Animations (in Menu Theme's settings): Smooth (the default) eases the whole menu - it fades in and grows slightly as it opens and fades out as it closes, a card lights up and lifts a little under the mouse, switches slide, a pressed row or card flashes, the drawer slides in from the right and back out, a new category's cards rise into place, cards glide to their new places when the drawer narrows the grid, scrolling glides, the selected category's highlight slides down the sidebar, tooltips and search results fade in, a slider's knob glides to a clicked spot and a stepped value fades in from the side it was stepped to. Every animation lasts 100-200 ms and runs on time, not frames, so it looks the same at 30 FPS as at 300; a click always acts at once, on where things end up. Reduced keeps only the short color fades (nothing slides, lifts or grows) and Off changes everything at once. Kept across restarts and in profiles. Animation Speed (the slider under it) picks how fast they run: Slow, Normal, Fast (the default, the timing above) or Extra Fast. Every slider in the menu that stops on values (whole numbers, steps of 5 or 50, tenths, a list of choices) only ever rests on one: while dragging, the knob jumps from value to value (gliding between them under Smooth) and always sits exactly where the number it shows belongs; the arrow keys move a slider one value at a time. The mod's own screens follow it too, with the same bevelled panels, wells and buttons: /profit and its trackers, Dungeon Profit (/croesus), Storage Search, the Run Log, the Inventory Sorter layouts, Custom Items, and the Bazaar and Auction House (their browsers and their reskins of Hypixel's menus). So do the Dungeon Sim's menu, Design a Map, its Filters, Sim Items and loading screen, Leap Order, the Interactive Map's frame and legend, the HUD editor, the scoreboard editor, Termism, and in the cheat build the AP3 and Breaker Aura config pickers and the AP3 and Auto Routes node editors. Each of those has a Theme setting in its feature's settings: Theme Default (follow the menu theme, the default) or Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Viridian or Custom for that screen alone; the Bazaar and Auction House also offer Inventory Theme, the flat look they had before, which follows the Inventory Theme setting. Every Theme setting lists the presets, Custom and every theme you have saved (read when you press it, so one saved a second ago is already there, under the name you gave it; deleting a saved theme puts the screens using it back on Default). The overlays that draw over the game with a look of their own have a Menu Theme setting too, which starts on Default - the overlay exactly as it always looked - and can be set to Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Viridian, Custom or a saved theme: the Storage Overlay (and the Profile Viewer panels that copy it), the Inventory Theme, the Inventory HUD, the Croesus chest profit panel and the Croesus and Experiments Start buttons, the custom Leap Menu (Croesus, Experiments and the Leap Menu each have ONE Theme setting in their tab that their screens and overlays all follow), the Item Browser and its recipe panels, and the Terminal Solver panels (which on a menu theme use its panel and accent instead of the Panel Background / Border colors). The Party Finder overlay, the Custom Scoreboard, the Health and Mana Bars, the class chips and the Themed Main Menu keep their own colors. In the cheat build every cheat-only feature sits in one red Cheat block at the bottom of its category, with cheat-only settings at the bottom of their drawer under a red header. The old New staging tab was removed on 2026-10-07. A setting that steps through several values (a mode, style, order, color preset) shows its value between a ‹ and a › arrow: a click on the right half of the value goes forward, on the left half back, and a right click on it goes back, wrapping round at both ends; a click on the setting's name does nothing. Every color setting opens the same color picker (a saturation/brightness square, a hue bar and an alpha slider), which also has a hex box showing the color that you can type into, Copy (puts the color on the clipboard as #RRGGBB, or #AARRGGBB when it is see-through, and says "Copied"), Paste (takes #RRGGBB, RRGGBB, #RGB, 0xRRGGBB or #AARRGGBB from the clipboard, spaces ignored; anything else gets a red "not a color" line and changes nothing; a color without alpha keeps the setting's own see-through amount), Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V for the same while the hex box is not being typed in, and a Recent row of the last eight colors picked, copied or pasted in any picker, newest first: click one to use it, so a color moves from one menu to another without the clipboard. Recent is kept across restarts and in profiles. The DVD text color uses this picker too.

Click Sounds (Home) picks the sound every button click makes: Default (Minecraft's own click), one of six short original presets (Soft Tap, Tick, Pop, Wood Knock, Chime, Bleep; synthesized for the mod, CC0, listed in docs/SOUND-LICENSES.md) or a sound of your own (.ogg, .wav or .mp3, or anything ffmpeg can read, a video included). Left click on Click Sound steps to the next and plays it, right click goes back; Click Volume sets how loud, from 100% to 500% (above 100% the click plays in layers, so 300% is three copies together; it is also scaled by Minecraft's master volume), and Preview plays it once. The click plays on every screen, Minecraft's own included, and also when you click an item in a chest-style Skyblock menu (Default stays silent there, as in the game). To import, press Open Sound Folder and drop a short Ogg Vorbis file in (under 1 MB; it is listed the next time you step Click Sound), or copy a sound file's path and press Import From Clipboard: an .ogg, .wav, .mp3, .aiff or .au of at most ten seconds and 1 MB is copied into the folder as it is and chosen. Anything else (m4a, flac, a longer sound, or a video such as mp4, where only the audio is kept) is cut to its first three seconds and saved as an .ogg by ffmpeg, which must be installed (without it the import says so and nothing is copied); a file with no sound, or not a sound at all, is refused with a message saying which. Imported sounds ride in profiles like the GIFs do. Sound Effects (Misc since 2026-10-20, out of Home's mod settings; added 2026-10-18) replaces five game sounds, each its own row with a switch and a Sound choice (Default, a preset or an imported file, the same list as the click; all off by default): Arrow Hit (an arrow hitting a mob or a player), Attack Hit (every left click that hits a mob), Block Hit (every left click on a block, the sound while you mine it), Mob Death (a mob dying, not a player) and Block Break (a block breaking). The chosen sound plays where the game's one would, from the same place with the same pitch and volume. Its switch is on the card and drawer as the tab's master switch, and Open Sound Folder and Import From Clipboard work as they do for the click (the imported file is then picked in a row's Sound). Descriptions (Home) has its own switch first (Descriptions, on by default, also the card's switch: off hides every card description and every HUD editor hint, and the two rows below keep their values for when it is back on), then two switches, both on by default: Show Descriptions hides the one-line description under every feature card's name (hover a card for its longer text), and Show Editor Descriptions hides the hint line at the top of the HUD editor (the line saying why the editor is empty stays). Every card description is short enough for its card at the narrowest the grid lays one out, with no "..."; the hint line in the HUD editor is swapped for a shorter wording on a narrow window rather than cut. The mod's own screens follow it too, with the same bevelled panels, wells and buttons: /profit and its trackers, Dungeon Profit (/croesus), Storage Search, the Run Log, the Inventory Sorter layouts, Custom Items, and the Bazaar and Auction House (their browsers and their reskins of Hypixel's menus). The old New staging tab was removed on 2026-10-07. **Chat**
- Translate — auto-translates your chat messages into another language before sending
- Auto Correct — fixes common typos before you send
- Command Auto Correct — optional: fixes typos in command names you type ("/wardorbe" → "/wardrobe") against the server's real command list, never touching arguments
- Chat Emotes — shortcuts that turn into fun chat emotes
- Click Translate — click a chat message to see it translated
- Copy Chat — with chat open, hold Shift or Ctrl and Left-Click a message to copy the whole message (every wrapped line of it), or Right-Click to copy only the line you clicked. Text is copied without color codes, a message stacked by Chat Hider is copied without its (xN), and a chat line confirms the copy. A plain click does exactly what it does without the mod
- Peek Chat — a key (or mouse button) that opens the chat history read-only: no text box, nothing you press types. Scroll it with the mouse wheel, Up/Down or Page Up/Page Down (Home goes back to the newest line); hover text and clickable links work as in normal chat. Escape or the Peek Chat key again closes it (Peek Mode: Toggle Peek); Peek Mode: Hold To Peek keeps it open only while you hold the key. The mouse wheel always scrolls the chat, never your hotbar. Only opens with no menu open, and never touches a message you had half typed in the real chat. Off and unbound until you set it in Chat > Peek Chat
- Chat History — one switch, **Unlimited Chat History**: vanilla forgets all but the last 100 chat messages; on, the whole session stays in chat and can be scrolled back to (more memory the longer you play). Off by default. Chat > Chat History
- Chat Hider — one switch over every chat tidier and hider (it replaced Hide Chat Messages and Chat Tidy on 2026-10-08; settings carry over). **Stack Duplicate Messages**: when a line arrives that repeats one of the last 50 messages in chat from the last 60 seconds (same text, same colors and formatting, same click action), the earlier copy is removed and one line ending in a gray (x2), then (x3) and so on, is added at the bottom, keeping the first copy's click and hover. Other messages in between do not stop it; the window keeps an old line from being pulled down from far up the chat. Blank lines and separator lines (----- or ▬▬▬) never stack, so back-to-back message boxes keep their spacing; signed player chat is left alone. **Hide Damage Messages**: hides Hypixel's combat spam, both families with one switch: "Your <ability> hit N enemy/enemies for N damage." (Implosion, Guided Sheep, Spirit Sceptre, Witherborn, Explosive Shot, Spirit Pet, Thunderstorm, Molten Wave and any other ability with that shape), and the hits you take: "A Crypt Wither Skull / Spirit Sheep / Chicken Mine exploded, hitting you for N damage.", "<boss, trap or mob> hit you for N (true) damage" (Maxor's Frenzy, Storm's Giga Lightning, The Arrow Trap, Wither TNT, Bonzo's Balloon...), "... struck you for N damage" (Stormy mobs, Spirit Chicken lightning), "The Lost/Frozen Adventurer used <ability> on you!" and "Your bone plating reduced the damage you took by N!". Only whole server lines are hidden: the same words typed by a player in any chat, or with anything else on the line, always show. Hiding is display-only: every other feature still reads the line, and the game log still records it. Heal lines (Wish, tether, fairy) are not touched. **Hide Useless Messages** ("There are blocks in the way!", "Profile ID: ..."), **Hide Discord Warnings**, **Hide Microsoft Warnings**, **Hide Empty Chat Messages**, **Hide Actionbar** and **Hide Non-Rank Invites** (party invites from players without a rank), **Hide Ban Announcements** (the Watchdog broadcast: "Watchdog has banned N players in the last 7 days." and "Staff have banned an additional N")
- Auto Meow — replies with a random cat noise ("purr~", "*slow blink*", ...) when another player says meow, purr, nya or mew, and replies in the same chat the message came from: party chat with /pc, guild with /gc, officer with /oc, co-op with /cc, a private message with /msg back to that player, all chat with /ac, and Mod Chat in Mod Chat (never through Hypixel). The channel is read from the whole chat line, so someone typing "Party > ..." in all chat is answered in all chat. Your own messages never trigger it, and it waits 3 seconds after each reply. Optionally plays a cat sound with its own volume
- Cringe — random one-liner messages for fun; `/cringe <chat>` picks the chat (pc, gc, oc, cc, ac, r, or kc for Mod Chat). Its first switch turns the whole feature on or off (on by default; it is also the switch on its menu card): off, `/cringe` and Leap Message's cringe line send nothing
- Spotify Lyrics — posts the lyrics of whatever you're playing on Spotify into chat, synced to the song. **No setup and nothing to configure**: open the Spotify desktop app and play something. It reads the app on your own PC directly — no account, no API key, no website, no login, and no source to pick. Only the artist and title ever leave the machine, to lrclib.net for the lyrics. There is no timing offset either: the app reports a track change instantly and the lyric clock stops while you are paused. Scrubbing back or forward, pausing, and starting the game part-way through a song are followed too: the mod reads Spotify's real position, length, play/pause state and cover from Windows' media controls (a small hidden PowerShell reader that runs only while one of the two switches is on and closes with the game), and snaps the lyric clock to Spotify's position whenever they differ by more than a second. Without that reader the clock is a stopwatch from the track change and a scrub is not seen. Lyrics are only used when they are for the same recording Spotify is playing: lrclib lists remixes and other versions too, so a version must have the same title, include Spotify's artist and be within two seconds of Spotify's length; when none is, no lyric lines are used (the HUD says the lyrics found are for another version). While an ad plays it says you are listening to an ad rather than leaving the previous song's last line up. The tab has two switches at the top, one per feature, and the card is on while either is. **Send Lyrics To Chat** (off by default; it was the "Mod Enabled" row, and keeps its saved value) turns chat posting on or off; under the Chat Lyrics header pick the chat it posts to (party, all, guild, plain, or Mod Chat), full lyrics or just the song title, and a profanity filter. A line sent to chat never starts with a slash, carries no section signs or control characters, and is cut to fit the 256-character chat limit with its prefix. Windows only. **Spotify HUD** (the second switch, off by default; it works with Send Lyrics To Chat off): a panel in the mod menu's own bevelled frame and theme colors, moved and scaled in Edit HUD, showing the cover, the song title and artist, elapsed and total time with a progress bar in the accent color, and the lyric line being sung with the current word in bright accent on a tinted chip, the words already sung in white and the rest dimmed. While paused the line is held and nothing moves. The cover and the total time are Spotify's own when the media reader runs (otherwise a note and lrclib's length). The highlighted word is an estimate unless the lyrics have per-word timing (rare on lrclib): the line's time is shared out by each word's syllables plus a little per letter, no word over 0.75 s, so a held note or a pause inside a line still puts it ahead or behind. **HUD Lyrics**, **Word Highlight** and **Hide When Paused** toggle the rest
- Mod Chat — a genuinely private chat channel between mod users. Messages travel over the mod's own relay server rather than Hypixel's party or guild chat, so players who don't run the mod never see them. `/kc <message>` sends one line (like `/pc`, and Translate, Auto Correct and Chat Emotes apply to it the same way), and `/chat k` switches your typed chat into Mod Chat (like `/chat p`) until you pick another channel with `/chat a`, `/chat p`, `/chat g` and so on, which still go to Hypixel unchanged; leaving the server switches it back too. Received messages show in your chat by default (Log To Chat). The tab shows who else from your party or lobby is connected. Falls back to nothing if the relay is unreachable — it will never quietly post your message in public chat
- Voice To Text — speak and the transcription is sent to chat, by Push To Talk (hold a key, release to send) or Open Mic (always listening; what you said is sent once you stop talking for the Silence Before Send time, 1.0 s by default, 0.3 to 3.0 s). Open Mic tells speech from room noise by how far it rises over the quietest moment of the last few seconds, so a fan or a hissing headset does not hold it open; a blip shorter than about a third of a second, an empty result, or one that is only "uh"/"um"/"the" is not sent, and it keeps listening while the last sentence is transcribed. Fully offline (Vosk); on first use it downloads the Vosk engine (~26MB, checksum-verified) and a small speech model (~40MB) into the config folder, so neither is in the mod jar. Pick which microphone it records from, and which chat it goes to: party, all chat, guild, plain, or Mod Chat. **Untested with a real microphone** While the speech engine or model downloads (first use only) the line above the hotbar shows the percent done, e.g. "[Voice] Downloading speech model: 37%". **Show Status On HUD** (on by default) turns off every status line above the hotbar - Listening, Transcribing, the download percent, the sent line; a failure (no microphone, set-up failed) still shows.

- Auto Inventory Sorter — save the exact slot each item should live in as a named layout, then put your inventory back into it with a key or `/invsort apply <name>`. `/invsort` (or Open Layouts in the tab) opens the layouts menu: it shows a copy of your inventory, you move items around in it (click to pick up and click to drop, or drag), type a name and save. The same menu lists your saved layouts to load, apply, rename, delete or bind to a key (a keyboard key or mouse button). Applying opens your inventory and moves each item with real clicks: an item going to a hotbar slot moves with one hotbar-number swap, anything else is picked up and put down. Ticks Between Moves sets the clicks' pace and Random Extra Ticks adds up to that many more at random. It stops on its own if your inventory changes under it, if the inventory closes, or if another menu is open, and it leaves items Item Protect has Locked In Place where they are. Layouts are one file each so you can hand one to a friend. Items are matched by their Skyblock item id rather than material. Your movement keys are held off while it's sorting. `/invsort list` and `/invsort stop` are there too. **Cheat build only**
- Price Tooltip — adds price lines to any Skyblock item's tooltip, in the style other Skyblock mods use (yellow labels, aqua values): NPC Sell Price, Bazaar Buy/Sell (Bazaar items), Lowest BIN Price, 1 or 3 Day Avg. Price, Est. Item Value, Obtained (the date the item was created, in red) and Museum (Combat): Donated / Not Donated. Each line has its own switch under the feature switch, which is off by default. Stack Price shows a whole stack's price while holding Shift (or always, or never), marked (64x). Est. Item Value is the item's own price plus what was applied to it - Recombobulator, Hot and Fuming Potato Books, enchantments (priced as Bazaar books), dungeon stars (their essence and items) and master stars, gemstones and the cost of each unlocked slot, Art of War and Peace, scrolls, enrichment, Polarvoid, Wood Singularity, Farming for Dummies, Jalapeno, Mana Disintegrator, Transmission Tuners, Wet Books, Etherwarp, drill and rod parts, dye, skin, rune, and a pet's item and skin; reforges are not counted. Value Mode Networth weighs applied upgrades the way networth calculators do (a Recombobulator counts 80%, an Art of War 60%), Full Price counts each at its market price; Value Breakdown lists every part while Shift is held (or always, or never). Number Format Exact (5,900,000.0) or Short (5.9M). Museum status is read for your selected profile from the API (your Profile Viewer key if set, else the keyless backend), every 10 minutes. Prices come from Hypixel (item list and Bazaar) and Skyblocker's public price lists (lowest BIN and averages, with Coflnet as a fallback), refetched every 1-30 minutes (Refresh Every, default 5) only while you are using the tooltips, kept on disk so they show at once after a restart, and never fetched on the game's render thread. Replaces the Item Browser's old Show Item Value line, whose setting it inherits once

**Cosmetics**

- Player Cosmetics (Cosmetics category) — one tab for how players look on your screen: Supporter Names and Supporter Sizes switches for other supporters' shared looks (each on its own; both off hides everything), Share if Supporter (one toggle that shares your display name, with its fade or letter colors exactly as you see it, and your own size once your account is linked as a supporter; players on an older version of the mod see the name in its first color), Share Ribbon Trail and Show Others' Trails (both on by default, also on the Ribbon Trail tab: share your Ribbon Trail with other mod users - supporters only, grayed out with the reason otherwise; the squares Trail is never shared - and draw the ribbons other supporters share), and three dropdowns. Custom Names is the in-game name changer: your display name with a color, a smooth per-letter fade between two colors, or a color of its own for each letter, Name Animation for a faded or letter-colored name (Off by default; Wave flows the colors along the name to the right in a smooth loop, Pulse dims and brightens the whole name; both are left click forward, right click back, with an Animation Speed from 0.25x to 3x - shared with the name, so other players on this version of the mod see it animate on your nametag, in chat and in the tab list, and older versions show it still), a Rank row for the tag in front of your name (Keep Original, None, VIP, VIP+, MVP, MVP+, MVP++ or Custom, left click forward, right click back; Rank Color steps the tag's color through the 16 chat colors, and Custom takes your own text such as KING++ with its own color for the + signs - it replaces a Hypixel rank tag directly in front of your name in chat, the tab list and everywhere else the name shows, and adds nothing where there was no tag), a live preview of the name as it will be painted (animated too), a reset back to your real name, renames for other players, and Per-Profile Names (off by default: your custom names are one set shared by every profile, kept outside the profiles, so loading or exporting a profile never changes them; on, they are saved inside each profile like any other setting, and the switch itself is the same in every profile). A name inside a command is never restyled: typing /killer560, a command with your name as its argument, and the chat box's command suggestions all show the name as it is; My Name in F5 (off by default) shows your own nametag above you in both third-person views, never in first person. Supporters' shared names show on nametags, in chat and in the tab list, Skyblock's text rows included, and every player's nametag follows their size so it stays just above the head from 0.05x to 2x. Player Size has My Size and Others' Size sliders from 0.05x to 2x, each with its own reset, plus your model's width/height/thickness. Play as Mob draws your player as any in-game mob: My Mob (Player by default; a button that opens a menu listing every living mob, Player first, with a search box that narrows the list as you type, and clicking a mob picks it; the bosses and giants - ender dragon, wither, giant, elder guardian, warden - are not on it) shows the mob in your own F5 view, walking, looking and swinging the way you do, with your nametag over its head, and My Size and Width/Height/Thickness apply to it as they do to your player; with Share if Supporter on and your account linked as a supporter, other players with the mod see you as that mob too (older versions see the player). Show Others' Mobs (on by default) draws other supporters as the mob they share, except inside a dungeon, where everyone always shows as a player so a teammate is never mistaken for an enemy. It is a picture only: your hitbox and everything the mod's features read stay a player. Held Item is the held-item transform. The name changer never recolors or renames anything inside this mod's own menus. Everything here is local rendering only — it never touches your hitbox, collision or reach, and anything *sent* to Hypixel (party invites, friend adds) always uses your real IGN, so renaming yourself can't break being partied or friended. If a nickname collides with a real account's name, chat asks which one you meant instead of guessing. There's a reset at the bottom
- Custom Items (Cosmetics category; was Armor Recolor) — client-side only: open the menu with Open Custom Items or `/customitems`, click any item in your inventory, armor or hotbar and change how it looks: a dye color (dyeable items, which is most Skyblock armor), an armor skin and trim (armor), or a Look - any item model by id, or Copy Look and click another item (a player head's texture comes with it). Applies To picks this exact item (by its UUID) or every copy of it (by its Skyblock id). Saved looks are listed in the menu to pick or Remove; the tab has the switch and Reset All. Nothing is sent to the server and the real item never changes.
- Held Item Outline (Cosmetics category, off by default) — an outline, a fill, or a filled outline around the item you are holding, drawn on the first-person item model itself: not on the hotbar or the HUD (they draw on top of it), and nothing in third person, with F1, or with an empty hand. Style picks Outline, Fill or Filled Outline; Outline Color and Fill Color each have their own color and alpha; Outline Side picks which face of the item the outline follows: Near (the default) hugs the face nearest you, Far hugs the whole shape including the item's thin sides (the original look), and Both draws around the near and the far face with a line joining them at every corner. A flat item (sword, bow, most items) and a block get all three; a model with no flat front and back (a shield) always uses Far. Outline Width is 1 to 10 screen pixels in half-pixel steps; Main Hand (on) and Off Hand (off) pick which hands get it. It shows whenever it is on, on any server: Skyblock Only does not hide it. It follows the item exactly through swings, eating and the held-item transform, since it is the item drawn a second time into its own mask
- Name Changer — client-side only: change how your own name shows (one color, a fade, or a color per letter, which can Wave or Pulse) and rename specific players (chat, nametags, tab list, GUIs), each with its own color picker. Bold Name draws your own display name in bold, and with Share if Supporter other mod users see it bold too (shared as an &l, which the relay already accepts). Lives in Player Cosmetics under Custom Names
- Ribbon Trail (Cosmetics category, off by default) — one smooth band streaming behind you like a Roblox trail: it stands upright behind you (never flat, whatever way the camera looks), starts just behind your back, and is colored from the head (at you) to the tail, fading out at the end. Set its height, how long it lasts, its opacity and its colors (two or three of your own, your Name Changer fade colors, or a flowing chroma); it shrinks back into you when you stand still and can be hidden in first person. **On Teleport** (Break, Connect or Arc; left click forward, right click back; Connect by default) says what the ribbon does when you etherwarp or otherwise teleport (a jump of more than 4 blocks in one frame): Break leaves a gap between where you left and where you landed, Connect draws one straight band between the two points, Arc draws a curved band that rises between them. It works alongside the squares Trail. A supporter can share it: with Share Ribbon Trail on (on this tab and in Player Cosmetics, on by default, grayed out with the reason for anyone who is not a linked supporter) and Share if Supporter on, your ribbon's colors, height, length and opacity go up with your shared name, and other players on this version of the mod see it behind you, drawn exactly as you see your own (Name Fade uses your shared name's fade colors). Without a display name of your own, only the ribbon goes up, and a name staff set for you is never replaced. Supporter status is checked once at startup, so your name, size and ribbon are shared without opening the menu. Show Others' Trails (on by default) hides everyone else's. At most 16 players' ribbons are drawn, the nearest within 96 blocks; older versions of the mod ignore it. Only ribbons are shared or drawn for others: a squares trail an older version shares is ignored Since 2026-10-18 it has no card of its own: its settings are the Ribbon Trail section of Player Cosmetics, the only trail that is shared.
- Trail (Cosmetics category, off by default) — a square dropped behind you each tick, green on the ground and blue in the air, with adjustable length, size, opacity and fade, and a Show In First Person switch. **On Teleport** (Break, Connect or Arc, Connect by default) says what the squares do when you etherwarp or otherwise teleport (a jump of more than 4 blocks in one tick): Break lays no squares between where you left and where you landed, Connect lays a line of squares between the two points, Arc lays them along a curve that rises between them. It is for configging and only you see it: it is never shared
- Name Changer — client-side only: change how your own name shows (one color, a fade, or a color per letter, which can Wave or Pulse) and rename specific players (chat, nametags, tab list, GUIs), each with its own color picker. Lives in Player Cosmetics under Custom Names
- Nickhider — its own tab in the Cosmetics category: Randomize Others gives every other player a stable fake name for the session. It works on its own, without the Name Changer toggle. Randomized Name (shown while Randomize Others is on) shows everyone as the base name you type plus a number instead - no two players share a number, each keeps theirs for the session, and changing the base name keeps the numbers; an empty base name falls back to made-up names

**Hud Elements**
- GIF Player — plays a GIF (with optional audio) as a HUD overlay. Ships with a built-in dancing cat girl GIF in every install, off until you switch it on; it is listed as "Dancing Cat Girl" and toggled like any other GIF, and DVD can bounce it too; your own GIFs in the folder are untouched and still show by default. GIFs are hidden while an inventory, chest, storage or any other menu is open
- DVD — the classic bouncing DVD logo screensaver on your screen; hidden while an inventory, chest, storage or any other menu is open. Its first switch turns the whole feature on or off (on by default; it is also the switch on its menu card): off, no box is drawn whatever each box's own switch says, and your boxes are kept
- Video Browser — watch YouTube Shorts in a small 9:16 window pinned over Minecraft (Windows only; uses your installed Edge or Chrome in its own profile, log into YouTube once). Keybinds for show/hide, next, previous, play/pause and mute work without leaving the game. Dark / Light / System / Amber theme option. Also plays normal YouTube, TikTok, Instagram Reels, Twitch or any URL; an edit mode to drag-move and resize the window; a zoom setting; a DVD-style bouncing placement; and a YouTube comments guard so next/previous does not fire while comments are open. **Untested**
- Health and Mana Bars — reads Hypixel's action bar (health, mana, defense, overflow mana, vitality, and any other current/max stat it shows with its own icon) plus the game's own experience bar, and draws your own bars and text from them. Turn on Stat Bars and choose a **Layout**: Predefined (the default for a new setup) puts the bars and texts in areas round the hotbar, the way Skyblocker's fancy bars work - two rows above the hotbar where every readout in a row gets an equal share of the hotbar's width, a column left of the hotbar and one right of it, and Hidden - stacked clear of whichever vanilla hearts, hunger, armor, air and XP rows are still showing, and following the window, GUI scale and HUD scale; in Edit Bars you drag a readout onto another area (the target lights up and the others make room) or onto the Hidden tray. Custom puts each one exactly where you drag it in Edit Bars. Both arrangements are kept, so switching back finds everything where it was; a setup from before the setting that already shows a readout stays Custom. The page itself is short (simplified 2026-10-12): Stat Bars, Edit Bars, an on/off for each bar, Style and Text Size. Everything else is under **Advanced**, closed until you open it and left as you leave it (the menu search still finds the settings inside): the layout, the bars' look, every color in one grid, the text readouts and the vanilla hides. The bars: Health (absorption - health past max on the action bar, or the game's own absorption - is its own segment in the Absorption Color straight after your health, like golden hearts, and the health fill stays your health out of max), Mana, Defense (shown as damage reduction, defense / (defense + 100)), Vitality (the resource healing abilities, Wither Shield and Creeper Veil spend; Hypixel shows it on the action bar only while it is in use), Other Resource (anything else current/max with its own icon) and XP (the level and progress the game has - on Hypixel that bar is sometimes a countdown instead, and then that is what it shows). Advanced holds the shared bar length and thickness, the on/off number on the bar, and the colors: each bar that is on, Absorption, Overflow Mana and the background, in one grid. **Bar Style** sets their look: Bold (the default) has a black and orange double rim, a glossy two-band fill and notches at every quarter; Sleek has a thin dark frame, a sunken track with a soft inner shadow and a gradient fill with a light top edge. Both draw the stat's own item as an icon at the bar's left (a golden apple for health, an enchanted book for mana, an iron chestplate for defense, Hypixel's ♨ Vitality symbol, outlined in the Vitality Bar's color, for vitality, a bottle o' enchanting for XP, a clock for the other resource). You also choose Rounded (a two-step curve) or Square corners, Icons on or off, whether the number sits Inside the bar or Beside it (after its end, in the bar's color, the bar shortened to fit in the same box; bars stacked in one column keep room for the widest number among them, so they end at the same length), Text Size (Small, the default, or Normal, both with a dark outline all round the digits), Smooth Fill (the bar slides to a new value instead of jumping) and Damage Ghost (after a drop a pale strip shows where the bar was, holds for a moment and drains down). Health's absorption and the mana bar's overflow mana show as their own layer in their own color (Absorption Color, Overflow Mana Color, a deep blue by default) after the value, ending at the bar's end at most. The icon and a Beside number take their room inside the bar's own box, so placing and resizing in Edit Bars works the same in every style. A bar showing its number is always at least the number's height plus one unit above and below it (12 units, a little thinner than the 14 it was before 2026-10-13; a Bar Height above that is drawn as set), and the number keeps four units clear of each end - when the full "current/max" does not fit, only the current value shows, and on a very short bar none. In the Predefined layout every bar and text shares one size (scrolling any of them in Edit Bars resizes them all together; it is saved like any setting) and every bar is the Bar Height thick, so a row never mixes sizes; Custom keeps each one's own scale and size. The bars and texts stay drawn while an inventory, chest or any other screen is open, behind it the way vanilla's hearts and hotbar are **Text readouts** (in Advanced): the same stats as text in the same places, plus Overflow Mana, Intelligence (worked out from max mana, which is 100 + Intelligence) and Effective Health (health x (1 + defense / 100)), and a text shadow. **Hide** (in Advanced): the hearts/hunger/armor/air set that only acts while Stat Bars is on (with Unhide Hearts In Rift), and the always-on hides for the vanilla health, absorption, mount health, regeneration bounce, armor and hunger bars, the XP bar with its level, and Hypixel's own stat text on the action bar (only the stat numbers go - "5/7 Secrets" or an ability name on the same line stays). Held Item Name sets vanilla's item name that pops up over the hotbar when you switch slot: Hidden Over Bars (the default) hides it only where it would land on one of these bars or texts, Hidden always hides it, Shown never does. The number on a bar is centered on it, to the pixel, in both layouts. Every bar and text is its own HUD element, moved and scaled in Edit Bars, where in Custom a bar's edges also drag its length and thickness on their own (the Bar Width / Bar Height sliders set every bar at once). True Defense is not offered: Hypixel never puts it on the action bar, only in the /stats menu. The old one-line Classic Display is gone; a saved setup that used it and no other readout comes back as the matching Health/Mana bars and Health/Mana/Defense texts where it was

**Helpers**
- Performance HUD — your real ping to the server (a measured round trip, once a second, the way Odin and Skyblocker do it; Hypixel's tab list always says 1 ms) and the server's TPS. Previously called Lag Display; the "server lag" readout was replaced with a real TPS figure and the text bar at the bottom removed **Colors**: Good, Okay, Bad and Awful colors for Color By Value (TPS, ping and FPS pick one by how good the number is) and a Text color for every line when Color By Value is off and for the CPS line; each opens the color picker.
- Quiver Display — shows the real "Arrows Remaining" count from any real arrow/quiver item anywhere in your inventory or off hand, in a text color you pick, with an optional Low Arrow Alert (an on-screen title and a sound, text editable with & codes and {count}) when you drop below a threshold of 20 to 200 arrows
- Experimentation Table Solver — solves Chronomatron, Ultrasequencer, and Superpairs by highlighting the answer: you click, it just shows you where. It is its own setting rather than a mode, so you can run it on its own. The **cheat** build adds a separate Auto E-Table section that clicks for you, independent of the solver - either, both or neither can be on; its auto-swap to your best Guardian pet before a run is on by default. In the cheat build the Superpairs highlights follow Auto E-Table's Auto-Solve Superpairs switch: with it off, Superpairs shows no highlights. Its defaults are killer560's own delays: Stop At Max Clicks, Click Delay 400 ms, First Click Delay 600 ms, Random Delay up to 50 ms, no auto-renews (an older settings file moves to these for every delay it never changed); the Superpairs skips and buying Titanic bottles are off until you turn them on. Click Delay is the least time between any two of its clicks, whatever the random delay draws, and neither delay goes below 150 ms. In Superpairs it plays in turns, counts what is left on the board to deduce hidden tiles, and takes money before XP; Skip Grand XP Bottles, Skip Guardian Pets and Skip Metaphysical Serums (three switches) leave those for the very end, only if clicks remain; Explore Before Claiming (off by default) also holds back every reward worth up to Hold Back Up To (0-3,000,000 coins in 100,000 steps, 1,000,000 by default, priced like the profit tracker) until the board is known, then goes back for them most valuable first, while anything worth more is claimed at once (held-back rewards are still money: their partners are looked for before XP, and once the clicks left would no longer cover them they are taken first); the Nadeshiko Dye, Superpairs' mega-rare drop, always comes first whatever the settings: once the board shows it, nothing else is claimed until the dye is, an Instant Find included (it is never mistaken for an XP tile); T7 Drops First (off by default) does the same for a T7 book (an enchant at its true max of VII, the level Enchant Colors paints Perfect), after the dye; powerups work as on Hypixel (a powerup never takes a turn's click, +Clicks is a free space, Instant Finds stack); when the board's clicks run out it presses Escape once, which takes you to the rewards to claim. Any key, mouse click or scroll of yours stops a run (moving the mouse does not), as does opening another screen such as chat; the click on Start ETable that began it does not count, and chat says what stopped it - see [Which jar do I download?](#installing) below.
- Experimentation Table Profit Tracker — logs every claimed experiment (game, clicks, rewards, XP, bits) with coin values and running totals, plus a max-clicks chat notice whether you are using the solver, the Auto E-Table, or both. Its tab has the on/off switch and an Open /profit button; the totals and the reset are on the /profit screen. On by default
- Profit Trackers (/profit) — one command for every profit tracker. `/profit` opens a hub with a card per tracker (Dungeon/Croesus, Experimentation Table), each with a live one-line summary; click a card to open that tracker, and Back or Escape returns to the hub. `/profit <name>` opens one directly and tab-completes: `croesus` (or `dungeon`), `etable` (or `experiments`); an unknown name lists the valid ones in chat. `/croesus` and `/croesus profit` still open the dungeon tracker. The Experimentation Table tracker has its own screen (drawn with the same panels as the dungeon one) and an Open /profit button in its tab. Every claim is recorded against the Minecraft account (by its UUID, so a renamed account keeps its profit), Skyblock profile and mod profile it was made on. Profit is kept separate per Minecraft account: **Show Profit** (in the Croesus and Experiments settings) is This Account by default, which counts only what the account you are logged in on recorded, or All Accounts, which adds every account together. **Profit Data** (beside it) is Shared Across Profiles by default, so switching mod profile never hides your profit, or Per Mod Profile, where each mod profile shows only the profit recorded while it was the active one (renaming a profile keeps its profit); switching either way moves and loses nothing. Both settings are the same in every mod profile and are not part of one. Each tracker screen has switches for the account (This Account, All Accounts, then each other account with profit, by name), the Skyblock profile and the mod profile (left click steps forward, right click back; All on each; under Per Mod Profile the mod profile switch shows the current one and does not step) plus All Profit, which shows everything ever tracked (every account; under Per Mod Profile, everything in this profile). Reset Totals turns red and asks once more ("Reset <what is selected>?"), and resets only what the switches select; the dungeon log keeps every claim in its file. Claims from before accounts were kept go to the account that recorded them when its name is known (the account you are on, or one of Prism's saved accounts), and otherwise to the account you are on the first time this version reads the tracker; a claim with no mod profile goes to the current one. Claims with no Skyblock profile show as ? on that switch. Client-side only, so it works the same on Hypixel
- Storage Overlay — see the contents of every Ender Chest page and Backpack live, right alongside your open inventory. Always centered across the top of the screen at any window size or GUI scale (you can still drag its height in the HUD editor). Three themes: Amber (the original dark look), Dark (neutral grays, no orange) and Light. A Scan All button beside the grid (under Search) opens every real Ender Chest page and backpack in turn, at an unhurried, slightly varied pace, so the grid holds all of them - it reads Hypixel's Storage menu first and never opens a locked page or an empty backpack slot. While it runs the button shows its progress ("Stop 3/8"); press it again, or Escape, to stop
- Bug Report — `/killer560 bugreport` builds one zip you can drag straight into Discord: your latest log, every one of the mod's config files, and a report of your mod/Minecraft/loader versions, which jar you are running, your OS, Java and memory, and every mod you have loaded. **Credentials are stripped before anything goes in.** Your session-login and account-proxy files are left out entirely rather than redacted, and in everything else any value whose name looks like a token, secret, password, auth, session or cookie is replaced with [redacted] - verified against a file containing six different fake credentials, all six removed, with keybinds and normal settings left untouched. Log lines are scrubbed the same way. It tells you where the zip is and opens the folder
- Dungeon Sim — **work in progress, not part of a release yet.** A clear-practice dungeon you join from a Dungeon Sim button on the Multiplayer screen. It connects to nothing: it opens a flat local world this mod owns, and everything in it is client side. The button opens one wide menu titled DUNGEON SIM with its pages in a column down the left - **Create a New Map** (the Map Designer), **Previous Runs**, **Load a Room** and **Route Practice** (see Dungeon Sim Room Filters), the open one lit in the accent - with **Back** at the bottom of the column, and the page you pick fills the rest; there is no in-between "pick what to practice" page. The first time you open it in a game session it opens on Create a New Map; after that it reopens on the page you were last on (remembered until the game closes, not across restarts), and a drawing on the designer is kept while you look at the other pages. The column stays on the designer too, so the other pages are always one click away. The header bar carries only the title (no page name), and the column has no room count under it. The generator takes the floor, puzzle count and rooms-to-blood, and each floor fills its own real size like a Hypixel floor - Entrance 4x4 rooms, Floor 1 4x5, Floors 2-3 5x5, Floor 4 6x5, Floors 5-7 6x6 - as one compact rectangle, never scattered over the 6x6. **Load a Room** lists every room as a table - Name, Type, Size, Secrets, Crypts and Routes, each column as wide as its widest heading (sort arrow included) and cell so no two ever run together, Name taking the rest and cutting a long name with "..." (a narrow window drops Type, then Crypts, Size, Secrets and Routes as it must) - with a search box, a **Sort** button (Name, Type, Size, Secrets, Crypts, Routes; left click the next, right click the previous; counts sort biggest first) and column headings you can click to sort by that column, again to turn the order round; the sort is kept across restarts. Under the search are the Size chips (1x1, 1x2, 1x3, 1x4, 2x2, L) and the Secrets and Kind settings, the same filter as its Filters button (see Dungeon Sim Room Filters). A chosen chip is lit - filled in the theme's accent with bright text - and one that is not is dark with dim text; Secrets and Kind are lit when they are not on Any, and the Filters button is lit while any filter is on, its count being the number of chips chosen (two Size chips read "Filters (2)"). Sorted by Routes, the rooms with "-" (no route possible) come first, then "no routes", then rooms with route nodes by their count; the other direction turns all of it round. A room loads only when you click on its row - a click beside the list does nothing. **`/reset`** (or **`/reload`**) rebuilds the room you loaded on its own (from Load a Room, Change Room or All Rooms) exactly as it was loaded - every block, so a blown or broken wall is back, the secrets (chests shut, items, bats, essences, levers), the puzzle, the mobs and the doors - and puts you back where the room first put you, facing the same way; the map, Secret Waypoints' found marks and Auto Routes start over as on a fresh load, and chat says "Room reset". On a whole floor it resets nothing and says it is for solo rooms. Like the other sim commands it only exists inside the sim: anywhere else it is not suggested or completed, and a server's own `/reset` or `/reload` runs untouched. **Previous Runs** shows every saved run that has a map as a card: a small picture of the run's dungeon map beside the floor, score, time, date, room count, party and secrets, crypts and deaths, sorted by Newest, Oldest, Best Score, Fastest or Floor and filtered by Floor (both kept across restarts). Clicking a card opens the run's own page: the map large on the left and beside it everything the run recorded - time, score, the phases and device times, then the party (each player's class, rooms, secrets and deaths), which rooms each of them cleared alone and with others, and what killed them - with **Load This Run** to rebuild it and **< Runs** to go back. A rebuilt run puts every room down facing the way it did in the real run, so its doors meet: since 2026-10-15 a saved run records each room's rotation as the live map read it; a run saved before that, or a room whose rotation was never read, is turned to whichever way its doorways meet the doors on the run's map. Inside: etherwarp, Wither Impact, Tactical Insertion, Spirit Sceptre, Superboom, Dungeon Breaker and the Terminator (three arrows, and Salvation piercing five after three hits), all reimplemented locally, all swinging at Hypixel's 100 Attack Speed baseline. A refused ability says only what Hypixel says: "You cannot use abilities in this room!" in a trap room (Superboom and ender pearls still work there) or the Teleport Maze (only Superboom), "You cannot do that in this room!" for an etherwarp in Boulder, and "You don't have enough charges to break this block right now!" for an empty Dungeon Breaker (once a second while you keep trying, as on Hypixel); an etherwarp with no target, a Superboom aimed at nothing, a Dungeon Breaker in a puzzle room and Salvation's ready and hit counts say nothing in chat (each is in the log). You always have the Magnetic Talisman: items are picked up from three times vanilla's reach. One Superboom opens every crypt, crypt wall and prince with any block within 3 blocks of where it lands (measured to the block, not its center), each with its own undead, and takes the whole of each: a prince goes with its entire tomb, the player head on it and the layer under it (Big Red Flag's tomb is a prince too), and a cracked brick in reach takes its whole connected chain of cracked and chiseled brick, however far it runs. Ordinary stone brick beside them stays. Secret bats appear once you have been within 8 blocks of them for 5 ticks (a quarter of a second), and count for the room's secret total when killed. The lever-and-door rooms (Mines, Pressure Plates, Bridges, Raccoon, Logs, Arrow Trap, Redstone Warrior, Hallway, Knight, Atlas, Long Hall, Deathmite, Doors, Buttons, Dome, Cobble Wall Pillar, Golden Oasis) drop their door or gate when you flick the lever; the levers missing from the captures (Logs, Arrow Trap, Cobble Wall Pillar, Golden Oasis) are put back. The Redstone Key works as on Hypixel: the key is the real key skull, clicking it takes it (that is not a secret), and clicking the room's redstone block afterwards puts the key in, which counts one secret for the room; in Golden Oasis that also drops the three redstone blocks by the lever. The Dungeon Breaker holds 20 charges, spends one per block and regains two a second, and every secret you get (chest, item, bat, essence) gives charges back, up to the 20: how many is the **Echoes of the Lost** slider in the mod menu's **Sim Settings** tab (the Wither Essence Shop perk of that name, whose five levels restore 1 to 5 charges per secret on hypixelskyblock.minecraft.wiki; 0 is the perk off, and it defaults to 5, the maxed perk) and it is saved across restarts. Mage is the only class, with the left-click beam, which has ZERO pierce - it kills the nearest thing you are looking at and nothing else. Mobs have 1 HP and never move, so a route is timed against the route rather than against where a mob wandered; starred ones carry the same name-tag stand Hypixel gives them, so Mob ESP and everything like it works unchanged. Fels sit as motionless skulls and wake into an upside-down enderman when you approach. Wither doors take a key, become barriers, then clear. Until the sim has real mob fights, every mob room counts as cleared (map checkmark, score, Auto Clear) the moment you walk into it, without killing anything; puzzle rooms still clear by their puzzle: a solved puzzle turns its tab-list line to done and its room's map checkmark green, as on Hypixel (Ice Fill once you reach the end; Boulder the moment its chest is opened, whether or not the boxes were pushed). One mimic per map. The sim used to outline every chest that could have been it, and that outline is gone: because every placed secret chest is a mimic candidate, it amounted to highlighting every secret on the floor whether or not Secret Waypoints was switched on. The sim has no highlighter of its own now - Secret Waypoints is the only thing that draws secrets in there, under exactly the setting that draws them on Hypixel. Score follows Catacombs: one point per crypt to five (counted when the crypt's undead dies, as Hypixel's "Crypts" line does; a prince's death also says "A Prince falls. +1 Bonus Score"), two for the mimic (a trapped chest, as on Hypixel: opening it puts a baby zombie there, and the point comes when that dies; no chat line for either, as on Hypixel), Paul's EZPZ, and bats counting as secrets. `/speed <n>` sets speed on the Hypixel scale. `/simpuzzle <name>` builds a puzzle to drill. A five-second countdown holds you at the start of a full run. **State, 2026-10-01:** secrets come from the room database and land inside their rooms (asserted by a scenario that builds a floor and checks every chest). A Catacombs sidebar is put up on the sim's own integrated server, so the floor, the secret count, the keys and the room you are standing in read as they do on Hypixel. Rooms are rotated now: each capture's own quarter turn is recovered from its roof marker and its secrets, so a rotated room still puts its secrets in the right corner. Braziers burn as they do on Hypixel (a capture that recorded one with its fire knocked out has it put back), and a doorway between two rooms keeps its own trim - the stairs, slabs or iron bars along the top of the opening - rather than being cut to a bare 3x4 hole. Rooms are joined only through their real doorways, which sit at the same height on every floor, as on Hypixel; a gap higher up a wall (Mage, Quad Lava) is never taken for a door and cut through. **The eleven puzzle rooms are bound to their real geometry rather than to invented arenas** - the room's own levers, buttons, pads, lanterns, ice and boulders, found by scoring each puzzle's coordinates against the pasted blocks at all four rotations and refusing to arm a room they are not in. That means your own puzzle solvers and auto-puzzles work in there against the same data they use on Hypixel; `docs/SIM.md` records what each one was measured against. **Not done:** mobs (the captures hold no mob positions); a handful of rooms have imperfect captures (Balcony, Catwalk, Purple Flags); and none of it is tuned against a real floor yet
- Dungeon Sim Teleports — **work in progress, sim only.** Two ways of getting around inside the dungeon sim, and neither command exists anywhere else: both are gated so that outside the sim they do not run, do not tab-complete and do not shadow whatever Hypixel does with those names. `/goto <room>` (also `/tpto <room>`) teleports you into a room by name, and the name tab-completes against the rooms on the floor you are standing in rather than against the whole 135-room library - so it can only ever offer you somewhere you can actually go. The argument is greedy, because room names have spaces in them ("Higher Blaze", "Lava Ravine"), and completion matches a prefix first and then any substring, so "blaze" offers both halves of the blaze pair; a name that matches two different rooms is refused rather than guessed. Where you land is the same place a room loaded on its own puts you (the picker, All Rooms, `/next`, `/back`): on the room's main floor, two blocks in from one of its doorways and facing into the room - the floor height most of its doorways share, on a solid block with nothing at your feet or head. On a generated floor a one-tile room uses the doorway a run walks in by. A few rooms keep the spot you asked for: the trap rooms, Teleport Maze and Boulder one block in from the entrance, Ice Fill two in, and the blaze rooms at their own level. `/c <blocks>` clips you that many blocks along the direction you are looking, and a negative number goes backwards; it goes through whatever is in the way, which is the point of a clip in a practice world, and the height is clamped into the world so a clip straight down cannot drop you under the void. Both write your position directly, which this mod never does anywhere else and must never do on Hypixel - the sim's "server" is the client's own integrated server, no packet leaves the machine, and the same gate that guards every other sim ability (a singleplayer world AND no connected server) is what makes that true rather than hoped for
- Dungeon Sim Room Capture — **dev builds only (not in a release jar), either variant.** `/mage` (also `/killer560 mage`) re-captures the Mage room for the sim from a real dungeon, because the shipped Mage capture has Altar stitched in as its second tile and so has only one real doorway in the sim. Stand inside Mage on Hypixel and type it: it reads the room the dungeon map says you are in, refuses if that is not Mage, if the map has not grouped both of its tiles yet, or if any chunk of the room is not loaded (walk round the room and run it again), then reads every block of the whole room - both tiles, the wall around them, every height - from what your client already has loaded. Nothing is sent to the server. Before saving it checks the result: each tile's center column must be Mage in the room database (a stitched-in tile is named and refused) and there must be a doorway at the doorway height (y 69..72). A good capture is written to the sim's room library in your config (`config/killer560/dungeons/sim/killer560smod-rooms/Mage.json`), which wins over the copy shipped in the jar; a file already there is kept as `Mage.json.bak-<date>-<time>`. The file is read back and compared block for block, the library reloads, and chat says where it saved and that the sim uses it from the next floor or room it loads. Internally it can capture any room by name; only `/mage` is exposed.
- Dungeon Sim Room Filters — **work in progress, sim only.** One Filters panel, the same everywhere the sim picks rooms: the Map Designer, Load a Room (also what Change Room opens) and All Rooms each have a **Filters** button that opens it on their own filter, so the three never look or behave differently, and each is saved in its own file so it survives a restart (`killer560smod-sim-designer-filters.json`, `-picker-filters.json`, `-allrooms-filters.json`). It is drawn in the mod's own style, like Design a Map: a black header bar titled FILTERS with "N of M rooms" on the right (amber once anything is filtered), one row per category with its label on the left and the mod's buttons as chips on the right, a chosen chip in amber with an amber border, and **Clear all** and **Done** at the bottom. **Size** (1x1, 1x2, 1x3, 1x4, 2x2, L shape) and **Kind** (Normal, Puzzle, Trap, Champion, Blood, Entrance, Fairy) choose any number - nothing chosen means any; **Rare room** (Any / Yes / No), **Secrets** (Any / None / 1 to 3 / 4 or more) and **Your routes** (Any / Has routes / No routes) pick one; **Puzzles** (each puzzle room by name) and **Crypts** (Any / None / 1 to 3 / 4 or more) are the designer's older filters kept in the same style. Everything comes from the room database: Kind is its room type (a rare room counts as Normal, Old and New Trap as Trap), Rare room is its rare type, Champion its four miniboss rooms, Fairy the fairy room. "Has routes" means at least one node in your Auto Routes file for that room - the same file and the same room names Auto Routes plays from, and one route covers every rotation - and "No routes" lists only rooms worth routing (not puzzles, Blood, Entrance, Fairy or 0-secret rooms). Chips wrap onto more lines when the window is narrow, the labels go above their chips when it is narrower still, and the rows scroll when they do not fit, so nothing ever overlaps. Every Load a Room row also shows its Auto Routes at a glance in its Routes column: a green node count when it has a route, a gray "no routes" when it has none. **Route Practice** on the Dungeon Sim menu is a page with three sets - **All Rooms**, **Rooms Without Routes** and **Rooms With Auto Routes**, each showing how many rooms it holds after the filters - and loads the first room of the one you pick, on its own. **`/next`** and **`/back`** then load the next or previous room of that set, one room at a time, with a chat line like "Room 3/41: Atlas (5 secrets, routes: no)". That is the only line a room load says (a room loaded by itself says "Built <room>"); the secrets placed and the lever doors opening are in the log. With no filter on, the sets leave out puzzles, Blood, Entrance, Fairy and 0-secret rooms, as they always did; choosing a Kind or a Secrets bucket replaces that part, so the filters can bring those rooms back (Kind: Puzzle steps through the puzzle rooms). The list is fixed when you pick the set, so a room you route while walking "Rooms Without Routes" keeps its place; pick the set again to refresh it. The walk stops at either end rather than wrapping - `/next` on the last room says so and loads nothing. While All Rooms is running the pause menu has **Next room (All Rooms)** under Change Room, and `/simbuild noroutes` jumps to the next room of the set with no route. **All of that only works in All Rooms**: on a room you loaded by itself (from Load a Room or Change Room), `/next` and `/back` load nothing and say "/next and /back only work in All Rooms", their keys do nothing, `/simbuild noroutes` says the same, and the pause menu has no next-room button. The Route Practice page has a **Keybinds** section with **Next Room** and **Previous Room** (unbound by default, a mouse button works too, Esc clears), which do the same as the commands; they are also listed in their own **Sim Keybinds** tab. Both commands and both keys only exist inside the sim, so on Hypixel `/next` and `/back` are untouched. (This replaced the old All Rooms preset, which pasted every captured room in one line, and the picker's Routes / shape / Puzzles buttons and the pause menu's "Next room with no routes" on a single room.)
- Map Designer — draw a floor by hand instead of generating one, on the Dungeon Sim menu's Create a New Map page (the page the menu opens on first in a game session; the menu's column of pages and its Back stay on the left). Under the grid and the room list are two rows: the floor, Rooms to blood, Puzzles and Generate, then Clear, Load and Play, each a third of the row. The Rooms to blood and Puzzles sliders stop at what the floor can really hold - a long path and many puzzles compete for a small floor's few cells - so Entrance goes up to 6 rooms to blood and its Puzzles top drops from 4 to 2 as Rooms to blood rises, Floor 1 drops from 5 to 2 puzzles between 5 and 8 rooms to blood, Floors 2-3 allow 4 puzzles at 8, and Floors 4-7 allow everything; changing the floor brings both values down to the new floor's tops, Puzzles follows Rooms to blood as you drag it, Random draws only inside those tops, and if a floor ever still comes out short the status line says so first. The grid on the left is the floor button's floor at its real size (Entrance 4x4 room slots up to 6x6 on Floors 5-7, scaled to fill the same square), with the floor and its size written above it; changing the floor button changes the grid, and when a floor is already generated it generates a new one of the new size at once (rooms you placed that no longer fit are taken off, and the status line says how many); the list on the right is every room in the library that can actually be pasted, searchable, with its kind, shape and secret count, and colored by room type the way the live map colors it. Click a room then a cell to place it, right-click a cell to take it out. The grid is drawn on whole screen pixels at every window size and GUI scale: evenly spaced lines, every cell the same size, and each placed room filling exactly its own cells (an L room its three cells) with the same small gutter inside the lines on every side, its name centered in it. Only the doors the build will really cut are drawn - a gap between two rooms that stays walled shows as a wall - and they are colored the way the dungeon map colors them: a narrow opening for an ordinary doorway, the blood color for the blood door, the entrance color for the entrance gate, and the wither color with an amber outline for a wither door. The sim builds no wither doors, so the doors a real Hypixel floor would have as wither doors - every door on the room path from the entrance to blood, except the entrance gate and the blood door - are drawn that way too, here and on the sim's live map (with a single outline there, and "A wither door on a real floor" in the interactive map's tooltip); never on a real Hypixel map. Room names are fitted to the room exactly as the dungeon map fits them, one word a line and scaled down until they fit, so a 1x1 shows its whole name rather than a truncated one. Every generated floor, from Generate here or from the random generator, holds one trap room (Old Trap or New Trap). A room whose footprint would run off the grid or over a neighbor is refused rather than squeezed in, so a drawing always builds the floor it shows. There is no Save: every floor you Play from here goes into the **Load** list with the date and time it was made, newest first, marked Generated or Drawn ("Floor 1 · Generated", "Entrance · Drawn"), with its room and puzzle counts (right-click a row to delete it), and clicking one puts that exact floor back on the grid at its own floor size, ready to play again - a generated floor comes back with the same rooms, rotations and doors. Playing a floor that is already listed does not list it twice. Floors from real runs are not in it (they are in the saved-runs screen). The list survives a restart (`killer560smod-sim-map-history.json`, kept out of profiles as history); a drawn floor naming a room you no longer have loads the rest and says how many it dropped. Each list row has a little space between its two lines. Designs kept by the old Save button (`killer560smod-simmaps.json`) were brought into the list once, as "Name · Saved design" dated by when that file was last changed; the old file is left as it was, and deleting one from the list keeps it deleted. Play hands the placements to exactly the code path the random generator ends in, so a floor you drew and a floor the mod invented are built identically and differ only in which rooms went where. The Filters button beside the search box opens the sim's Filters panel on the designer's own filter (see Dungeon Sim Room Filters for the rows). Here size, rare room, secrets, crypts and routes do not apply to puzzle rooms, which follow only the Kind and Puzzles rows. The filters narrow the room list and Generate. Generate always keeps Entrance, Blood, Fairy and a trap, and any room you pinned on the grid, whatever the filters say, so a filter can narrow a floor but never break it: if the rooms left cannot make a whole floor (no Normal rooms, say) it generates from every room instead and says so in chat and on the status line ("filters too strict"); if fewer puzzles are allowed than the Puzzles slider asks for, the floor gets the ones allowed and the status line says how many ("only 1 puzzle allowed (slider 3)"). The Filters button is lit while any filter is on and shows how many chips are chosen, Clear all clears them, and they are saved in `killer560smod-sim-designer-filters.json` so they survive closing the screen and a restart (filters saved by the first, slider-based version of this panel are not carried over). The room labels on the grid follow the live dungeon map's Room Labels setting and are drawn with the same fitting code and text scale: Secrets writes each room's secret count, Room Name writes the name, and Room Name + Secrets writes the count under the name, in the way the live map writes a room nobody has opened ("0/6", or a bare "0" for a room with no secrets, from the room database), in the unopened-room gray. Names are split one word a line and scaled down to fit small rooms. Off and Checkmarks have no text to draw, so the designer shows names for them. Entrance, Fairy and Blood keep their names here, which the live map omits
- Map Logger — **dev builds only (cheat and legit; not in a release jar), always on, no setting.** Every dungeon room he stands in has its skull blocks written to `config/killer560/dungeons/maplog/killer560smod-maplog-<time>.log`, one line per skull with its position counted from the room's origin and its height in the capture's own heights, so the line can be compared with a room capture directly. Only skull blocks are read (decorative ones too, which is the point: etherwarp cannot land on them, so a sim room missing one sends the pathfinder through a wall). A skull is written once, the first time it is seen in a room; one that disappears later is written as "gone" and never added to the room, which is how a skull the Dungeon Breaker or Breaker Aura broke stays out of the record. A skull already broken when the room is first seen is never written. Chat stays silent; the log is a file, so profiles leave it out.
- Auto Debuff — **cheat build only.** The M7 dragon debuff, on the dragon the split says is yours: prefire Last Breath at the spawn box, stop in time to get the Ice Spray Wand in hand, jump ~300ms out, spray on the spawn tick, then swap and hit it. Healer and Tank use Flay (Soul Whip or Flaming Flay, either tier) at its real 0.5s cooldown; Mage melees instead, preferring Dark Claymore, then Midas' Sword, then a Hyperion with Chimera, then one with Ultimate Wise, with its own Mage Melee After toggle. Separate on/off per class for Mage, Healer and Tank - Archer and Berserker are never touched and have no setting. Stops once the dragon leaves the killbox. M7 only, since F7 has no Phase 5. The jump and spray have no slack between them, so the lead is ping-aware by default. **Walking is not implemented yet**: it does not path to the split, does not chase the dragon for the melee classes, and does not walk back on a death - so today you position yourself and it does the timed sequence
- Auto Update — keeps the mod up to date by itself, in Home's Auto Update section. Its Auto Update switch turns it on or off (on by default; off, it never updates), and its Channel picks what it follows: Full Release (the default) follows the newest numbered release, Beta follows the rolling Latest Dev Build that is rebuilt on every change (newer, less tested). On launch it asks GitHub, in the background, for the channel's release; if that release was built from a different commit than the jar you are running, it downloads the jar for your Minecraft version and your variant (it never switches you between cheat and legit), checks its SHA-256 against the release's checksum file, and refuses anything that does not match or does not come from the mod's own GitHub releases. A download that stops sending gives up after 60 seconds with no data (and says so); a slow one that keeps moving is never cut off. A chat line then says the update is ready. Nothing changes while you play: when you close the game a small helper waits for Minecraft to exit, removes the old jar and puts the new one in your mods folder, so there is never a second copy of the mod there. The next launch shows an "Updated to" popup with the release notes. Check Now runs the check on demand. Beta builds are release builds (no developer tools, quiet logs). Testing builds never auto-update, and neither do instances set up by the developer's deploy script
- Reboot — `/reboot` (the menu's Home page no longer has a Reboot button, since 2026-10-15) closes the game and starts it again once it has fully closed. It only works when Prism Launcher (or PolyMC / MultiMC) started the game: the instance is found from the game folder, and anything else (the official launcher, CurseForge, a dev run) gets a chat line saying it cannot reboot and nothing happens. Connected to a server it asks first: click the chat line or type `/reboot confirm` within 10 seconds. Singleplayer and the title screen reboot straight away. The game closes the normal way, so settings save and an Auto Update waiting to install goes in before the game starts again. A small helper process waits for the game to exit (at most 2 minutes, then it gives up and starts nothing) and then asks Prism to launch the same instance; it logs to `config/killer560/system/reboot/killer560smod-reboot/reboot.log`. To put it on a key, bind `/reboot` in Command Keybinds.
- Update Notice — tells you once, the first time you join a world each launch, if a newer release of the mod exists, with the version you are on and a link. It is a single check on startup, not constant background checking, and it stays silent if it cannot reach GitHub - so playing offline or behind a filter gets you nothing rather than an error you can't act on. Only full releases count; pre-releases and the Latest Dev Build are ignored. On by default (the one exception to new features shipping off, because the notice exists to catch people running a months-old jar), and turned off with Notify Me Of Updates on the Home tab, where Check For Updates still works on demand
- Screenshot Copy — automatically copies a screenshot to your clipboard the moment you take it
- No Fire — removes the on-screen fire overlay so burning doesn't block your view mid-fight
- Discord Rich Presence — shows "Playing Killer560's Mod" on your Discord profile, with your island/area, dungeon floor and boss phase, and an elapsed timer. Off by default; a Hide Details mode shows only the mod name
- Object Hider — client-side render suppressors for M7/F7: healer fairy, power orbs (with a keep-nearby radius), soulweaver skulls, archer bone meal, sheep, wither cloak creepers, shield hearts, grounded arrows, blindness, death animations, block-break/explosion/smoke particles, dying P5 dragons, the Wither King model, boss damage splashes, and a Clean End mode. Nothing is removed from the world — it only stops rendering. The vanilla health/armor/hunger bar hides live under Health and Mana Bars, the chat-line hides under Chat Hider in the Chat category, and Hide Players has its own section.
