Keys belong to the whole team on Hypixel, so it counts the team's keys from the sidebar's Keys line, else from the "has obtained Wither Key!" / "opened a WITHER door!" chat lines - a teammate's pickup counts the same. **Trap rooms** only through **Auto Trap** (on, with a route for that trap; otherwise trap rooms are left out): Party plays his selected mode, Solo picks Full Trap only while the S+ still needs secrets and the trap has some, else Just Cleared. It etherwarps onto the trap route's start node from outside - the only warp into a trap room - and never plans or fires a warp while he is inside one; his route walks him out, and if a step ever leaves him inside it stops and says to walk out. **Boulder and Teleport Maze** are puzzles like the rest: once its step in a Maze or Boulder room is over (or the room shows cleared, or the puzzle done or failed) the Interactive Map may path back out, so the maze's end pad no longer ends the run there. **Crypts** come from routes: a route's crypt nodes add a bonus point each, up to the five less the crypts already blown. The mimic and the prince count only when something else got them. Like Auto Secret, it never goes back for the secrets of a room whose route has run to its end this run, and it holds while an auto puzzle is working, so a puzzle's wait no longer times out under the auto and it never warps away mid-puzzle (since 2026-10-14).
- Auto Routes — **cheat build only, work in progress — not part of the current release.** Record a route through a dungeon room (`/ar start record`) and play it back when you etherwarp onto its start node. Recording captures your actual path; playback follows it rather than replaying key presses blind, so a lag spike doesn't desync the rest of the route, and every etherwarp / item use / block break waits for real confirmation before moving on. `/ar add` takes exactly ten types - `boom`, `breaker` (or `dungeonbreaker`), `ew` (or `etherwarp`), `use`, `walk`, `path`, `crypt`, `bat`, `jump <number>` and `align w<width> l<length>` - and after the type only `start` and `await:<number>` (wait for that many secrets first - or, on a crypt or bat node, kills; a bare `await` or `await:` waits for 1; `await:x` is refused, and so is anything else). While a route runs, Secret Aura clicks secrets only on a node waiting for them (killer560, 2026-10-16): a chest, lever or skull you pass mid-route is left alone, so a route that needs one taken needs an `await` node in reach of it; items you walk over are still picked up. A node goes on the center of the block you stand in - whole blocks, not AP3's half-block grid - with the height kept at your feet; recording places its nodes the same way. Nodes saved on the old half-block grid keep working where they are, and land on a block center the next time the editor moves them. A node you add fires straight away, as if you had just walked onto it: `/ar add ew` warps you the moment the chat closes (the first tick no screen is open), the rest of the route carries on from it as it would from any node - including the landing re-fire, so adding the second of two etherwarps aimed at each other starts the bounce - and it fires even with Start From Start Node Only on. If you step off it before the chat closes it does not fire, and while a route is already running it only latches as before. It is still one `/ar undo` step. On Hypixel it sends exactly what walking onto the node sends. A `start` node goes off the moment you step onto it, even mid-stride with W held - keys you were already holding are overridden while a node runs and only a fresh press counts as taking over - and it is the node the Interactive Map warps you to. Start nodes draw filled in, in every render style. On a route built with `/ar add` a walk node sprints the way you were facing when you placed it, until the next node fires, you take the keys or the mouse back, or you run into something. Mouse clicks never stop a route. A right click never skips an await (it is how you click the chest the await waits for), but a **left click while a node waits on its await fires the node now** - the etherwarp goes even though the secret has not been got. That left click is taken by the route: no swing and no block hit goes out with it, so the warp is the only thing it sends, and it is only taken while a node is actually waiting, or while the route is warping you onto the next node (anywhere else a left click is a normal left click). When an await is met by a chest, lever or skull click (yours or Secret Aura's), the etherwarp goes the next tick, already facing its block: Hypixel's server takes an item use right after a click on a block as that click's second half when the look it last heard (the chest's) points at a block, and then nothing happens - so after a click every automatic item use lets the server hear its own look first (the body turns that tick, the camera stays), and when its target is within reach it sends the click on that block first, as your own right click does. Nothing waits on a timer: Auto Routes' warps, use nodes and Kill Mimic, the Interactive Map's hops (so Auto Clear, Auto Secret, the Dungeon Autopilot and Blood Rush trips too) and Auto Puzzles' shots and warps turn first only when it matters, and AP3's plain use, Fast Leap, Auto Fairy Souls' pearl, Mask Swapper's rod, Auto I4 and the Goldor Triggerbot's shots send what your own right click would. A left click made while the warp or path onto a waiting node is still in flight is held for half a second and skips that node's await as it arrives, and a left click while Kill Mimic is using its item for the waiting node skips it too (Kill Mimic stops). It skips whatever the node waits for - secrets, a secret bat, or a crypt node's wait for its crypt/prince kill (the node lets go of the held click and the route moves on) - whatever is under your crosshair. Only your own mouse click counts: attacks and digs the mod makes itself (Breaker Aura, Secret Aura, Auto Clear, the route's own nodes) never skip anything. Tab completion offers `await:` on its own, so you type the number straight after it. Command nodes in a routes file always run (the Allow Command Nodes switch is gone). Each `/ar` command has an assignable keybind; Start and Stop Recording have theirs right under their buttons, the rest are in a Keybinds dropdown. The settings tab runs: the Auto Routes switch (full width), Start From Start Node Only (full width), Run While Map Open beside Kill Mimic, Mimic Timeout under Kill Mimic, Crypt Weapon beside Crypt Attack Time, Etherwarps Per Second (full width), Open Routes Folder beside Reload Routes, then Render Style (style, node numbers, thickness, height, Breaker Block Display and Breaker Block Style), Recording (Start / Stop with their keys under them), and the Colors and Keybinds dropdowns last, Colors directly above Keybinds - every row in even half-width columns, as AP3's tab is. The per-room node list is gone from the menu; `/ar list`, `/ar delete`, `/ar undo` and `/ar clear` do all of it. In the Filled render style each node is drawn one block lower, inside the block it was placed on top of (only the drawing moves - where it triggers is unchanged; Box and Cylinder draw where they always did). In the Cylinder style a start node's fill is a cylinder between its two rings, not a box poking out past them. `/ar edit db` (or the short `/ar db` / `/ar editdb`) still picks a breaker's blocks, and right after `/ar add breaker` it picks them for the breaker you just added, wherever you stand; otherwise for the nearest breaker. While breaker edit mode is on (`/ar edit db`, `/ar db`, or Pick Blocks in the node editor) and you hold your Dungeon Breaker, a left click is a normal left click - you can break blocks while picking. Right click adds the block you look at and shift-right-click removes it, as before; picking sends nothing of its own - only your own left clicks break blocks. While edit mode is on no route runs and no breaker node fires, even one you stand in. Any block your crosshair can pick is accepted: the reach is measured from your eyes to the block (4.5 blocks, the Dungeon Breaker's normal dig reach), not from the node, and if the block is further than that from the node's own spot the chat line says so, since the node breaks from wherever the route has you when it fires. Edit mode only answers to the Dungeon Breaker in your hand: with your AOTV, Hyperion or anything else held, both mouse buttons work normally in edit mode, so you can etherwarp, use abilities and hit things while picking, and no block is picked. **Breaker Block Display** (Highlight / Waypoint, in Render Style) chooses how those picked blocks are drawn in edit mode - Highlight like any block outline, hidden behind walls; Waypoint through walls anywhere within your render distance - and **Breaker Block Style** makes them Outline, Fill or Filled Outline (the default, the old look); still-standing blocks are white and already-broken ones red. They draw with Breaker Aura's own Display and Box Style code. `/autoroutes` is the same command as `/ar` in every form, with the same tab completion. `/ar edit <n>` opens a node editor, AP3's `/ap3 edit` for routes: the node's position in world coordinates (X, Y and Z on one row with whole-block nudge buttons under them, each landing on a block center like a placed node; a box you leave alone keeps the stored value exactly), yaw and pitch for etherwarp, use and walk nodes, the use item, the type (the five `/ar add` types), Start Node on or off (only one per room; saving moves it off the old one), an Await Secrets dropdown from None to 4, and for a breaker Pick Blocks (saves, then breaker edit mode on that node) and Clear Blocks, plus Look from me and Item from hand, with Save, a two-press Delete and Cancel in one row at the bottom; each section (Position, Aim, Item, Node, Breaker) sits under a small heading, and Position from me is gone. Go To is the full-width amber button at the top of the editor. Go To saves, closes the editor and etherwarps you to the node with the Interactive Map's own pathfinder (the same call the map uses, so it plans with whatever the map plans with); when you arrive, edit mode turns on and the node is latched, so it does not fire under you, even after edit mode goes off, until you step off it. If there is no etherwarpable block at or near the node, or the pathfinder finds no path, it says so in chat and edit mode stays off. Go To is refused while recording or while another Interactive Map warp is running. On Hypixel Go To sends exactly what an Interactive Map warp sends - the same etherwarps, rotations and sneaks - and nothing else; the editor itself sends nothing. Every save from the editor is one undo step, and `/ar redo` (and its keybind) re-applies whatever `/ar undo` last took back, including a delete; any new change clears the redo history. Routes live in one shareable, hand-editable JSON with an Open Routes Folder button and `/ar reload`. A `USE_ITEM` node matches on the item, not the exact id: stars, fragging and reforges were already ignored, and the four wither blades (Hyperion, Astraea, Scylla, Valkyrie), Spirit Leap/Infinileap and Superboom/Infinityboom now count as one item each. Aspect of the End, Aspect of the Void and the Etherwarp Conduit count as one too: they share the same 8-block Instant Transmission and the range comes from the Transmission Tuners on the individual item, up to four, so a fully tuned one of any of them goes 12. Nodes are numbered per room the way AP3 numbers its chains: 1, 2, 3 in the order they were added, drawn over each node (Show Node Numbers, on by default; nodes on the same tile lift their labels apart), and renumbered the moment one goes, so the old #3 reads #2. `/ar delete <n>` and `/ar remove <n>` are the same command and take that number; with no number they delete the node you stand clearly nearest (within 3 blocks, and every other node at least a block further), and say so instead when it is ambiguous. `/ar undo` takes back the last add, delete, breaker-block edit or `/ar clear`, whichever room it was in, as many steps back as you like (the history lasts until the game closes or `/ar reload`); with nothing left to undo it deletes the last node of the room you stand in, as AP3's does. `/ar stop` ends a running route. On a route built with `/ar add`, a teleport that lands you in a node fires that node, even one earlier in the route or the one you just left: two etherwarps aimed at each other bounce you back and forth until you press a movement key or `/ar stop` (or its key). Each bounce still waits for the server to confirm the landing. **Etherwarp chains run at one warp per tick**: once the server's position packet confirms a landing, an etherwarp (or path) node that fires next goes on that same tick, with no interact delay in between. A chain of etherwarp nodes that each land in the next one, with no awaits, therefore warps as fast as the server answers: one warp per tick at zero ping, so up to 20 a second, and one per round trip on a real connection. **Etherwarps Per Second** (a slider in the tab, 1-20, default 20) sets how fast they may come: consecutive etherwarps - chained nodes and the hops of a path alike - are at least 20/rate ticks apart, so 10 a second is one every 2 ticks and 3 a second one every 6.67 ticks on average (7, 7, 6). It is a pace, not a per-second budget: it never makes three quick warps stop for the rest of the second, and time a warp already spent waiting for something else (its landing, an await, a boom on the tile) counts toward it, so nothing waits twice. It never lets a warp go before the server has confirmed the one before. At 20 it is exactly as fast as before the slider existed. Each warp still waits for the server to have moved you before it aims from where you now are. Only an await, a node's own action (a boom waiting for its blocks to change, a crypt node's attack) and the other nodes on the same tile add waiting, and those still fire one after another with the interact delay between them; any node other than an etherwarp that you land on (a boom, a breaker, a use, a crypt) also still waits the interact delay after the landing. Several nodes on the same tile (the same snapped spot, the way `/ar add` places them when you add more than one without moving, with the feet in the same block) all fire, one after another, each waiting for the one before it to finish: dungeon breaker, then superboom, crypt, use, command, rotate, unsneak, etherwarp and walk last, so whatever moves you goes after everything that needs you standing still. Nodes of the same type fire in the order they were added (by number), whichever of them is the start node: a boom, a crypt and then another boom on one tile fire boom, boom, crypt. A superboom node is done when any block within three of where it hit changes - a Superboom blows the crypt or wall near the block it lands on, not that block - and one that changes nothing within a server round trip (or has no block in reach to aim at) has nothing left to blow and completes instead of stopping the route, so a stack of booms always reaches the crypt node after it. The block a superboom node aims at is the one its recorded look meets from the node's own standing eye (where you stood for `/ar add boom`), aimed at from wherever your eye is when it fires (since 2026-10-20: before, the look went from your eye as it was, and with the warp before it still holding sneak a shallow look met the floor a block short and the second boom of a stack blew nothing). A node's `await:<n>` waits before its own turn. The stack runs as one unit: where its last node puts you, the landing re-fire above applies to whatever node or stack you land in; a movement key, mouse turn or `/ar stop` ends the rest of it and the tile stays latched until you step off; if one node fails (a breaker item with no Charges line at all) the rest of that stack does not run and the usual stop message says why; an etherwarp that does not land is not a stop - it is reported like a server correction and the route carries on from where you are. A node that already went off as part of a stack is not fired again when the route reaches its own number. Stacked labels lift apart with the lowest one firing first. Numbering, delete and undo are unchanged. On Hypixel nothing new is sent: each node in a stack sends exactly the packets it sends on its own, with the usual interact delay between them. Every node acts on the tick it fires: the hotbar swap, the sneak and the aim are asked for together, so an etherwarp that needs to start sneaking uses one tick later (its sneak goes out in the firing tick's input packet, ahead of the use), an etherwarp you are already sneaking for, a use, a superboom, a command, a rotate, an unsneak and a walk all go on the firing tick itself, and a dungeon breaker breaks on the firing tick, right behind the swap: with Breaker Aura's Multi Break on (its default) every one of its blocks that is loaded, solid and within reach goes out on that tick, as many as the breaker has charges, followed by one swing - the same burst Breaker Aura sends; with Multi Break off it breaks one block per interact-delay tick as before. **Not enough charges**: when a dungeon breaker node fires and the item shows fewer charges than it has picked blocks still standing and in reach (a block that is already air needs none), it sends nothing - not even the swap - and waits, sneak, position and look held as the node holds them, logs "Waiting for N breaker charges (have M)." once (no chat line), reads the item's Charges line every tick (Hypixel gives back two a second, and so does the sim) and breaks them all in one burst on the tick there are enough, then the route carries on. A node with more standing blocks than the breaker can hold waits for a full breaker and breaks that many. A fresh movement key or `/ar stop` ends the wait, and after 30 seconds it stops with a message. Only a breaker whose item has no Charges line at all still fails at once. A swap no longer costs a tick, and no longer sends the same held-item packet twice. Legit mode (testing builds only, see below) still waits for its camera turn, an await still waits for its secrets, and every teleport still waits for the server to confirm the landing. Auto Routes runs in obvious mode only for now: the Legit/Obvious Mode setting is gone from every released jar while legit mode is finished, and a config file that still says legit is run as obvious (the saved choice is kept for when it comes back). In obvious mode the camera never moves, but the rotation the server hears does: every etherwarp, use, superboom and dungeon breaker turns your body to the look it needs on the tick it acts (your view held where it was), and your body is turned back to your view a tick after the route stops acting - the next movement packet always reports the rotation the use or dig carried, as a real client's does. Measured 2026-10-05 against GrimAC on a dedicated server (testkit 62-argrim): before this every obvious-mode etherwarp and path warp drew BadPacketsJ. A breaker's digs now carry the face your eye actually sees (they were always "top", which GrimAC refused for any block above your feet), and a superboom's click ends with the face vanilla sends. A walk node's camera ease no longer carries on into the next node, and turning your camera while a node waits on its await does not stop the route. An empty-hand use node, a crypt node and Kill Mimic turn your body the same way (camera held), and the route never holds its etherwarp sneak while a chest or any other blocking screen is open - your keys are all up there, as in vanilla. Each node writes an `[AutoRoutes] Node #n TYPE acted N tick(s) after firing` line to the log. Sneak is held between etherwarps: after every node the route looks at the node that fires next (the rest of the stack, the node a teleport landed you in, or the next in order) and, if it is an etherwarp, keeps sneak down - or starts it, when that etherwarp fires where you stand - so the server already has the sneak and the etherwarp uses on its firing tick (0 ticks instead of 1; two etherwarps aimed at each other went from 2 ticks per warp from firing to the landing down to 1). A walk node lets go (the walk sprints), and so do an unsneak node, the route ending or stopping however it stops, and a use-item node, which waits for the server to have the sneak up first because sneaking changes what a right click does (an AOTV would etherwarp, a chest would not open). A superboom, breaker, rotate or command node in between keeps it held. On a route built with `/ar add` the sneak stays down while you walk to the next etherwarp yourself, so you walk there at sneak speed; on a recorded route the recording's own sneak drives the walk between nodes. A `[AutoRoutes] Sneak held/released after node #n` line says when it changes. On Hypixel holding sneak is just a held key: one input packet with shift down when it starts and one with shift up when it ends, nothing in between. **Path nodes** (`/ar add path`, also with `start` and `await:<n>`, and in `/ar edit <n>`): path nodes pair up in number order - the room's 1st path node with its 2nd, the 3rd with the 4th - and the first of a pair etherwarps him to the second along a chain of warps planned by the Interactive Map's own floor planner (the same call a map press makes). The chain is planned ONCE - when the second node of the pair is added, or the first time the pair fires if it had no plan yet - and saved on the first node in the routes file, room-relative like every node, so it replays the same at any rotation and on Hypixel; playback flies the saved warps and never plans again. It is planned again only when either node of the pair moves or goes (an edit, a delete, an undo or redo), or when Re-plan Path is pressed in that node's editor, which also shows how many warps are saved. The plan is the fewest warps there are, proved: the floor planner's chain is only the starting point, and an exact search (every landing block, every point of every face of a block you could line up by hand to a sixteenth of a block, breadth first from both ends) either proves no chain with fewer warps exists or finds the shorter one - always: it gets a second and a half on the Interactive Map's planner, and if that is not enough the proof carries on in the background with no time limit, chat saying "proving it the fewest in the background..." and then "fewest possible, proved in N ms"; until then the pair does not fly (a route that reaches it waits there and says so; any key still stops it). Every block of the route's own Dungeon Breaker nodes numbered before the pair (or stacked on its first node's tile) is planned as already broken - a warp may go through it or land under it - because the breaker node breaks it first; Breaker Aura's pick list is not used, since Breaker Aura only works in a boss fight. If one is still standing when the warp is due - in its line or over its landing - the route waits there with sneak held and sends nothing, logs which block once, and warps on the first tick it is gone (no time limit; any key or `/ar stop` ends it). Re-planning after you change a breaker node's blocks is manual: `/ar replan`, and a pair planned before the change says so in chat when it fires. **Bat node** (`/ar add bat`): a crypt node for secret bats - it swaps to the Bat Weapon setting (Hyperion or Spirit Sceptre), uses it straight down, holding right click, until a secret bat dies within 10 blocks of you within three seconds of one of your uses (or `await:N` of them), then swaps back; Bat Attack Time without a kill moves the route on. With the Spirit Sceptre only the first two uses go straight down; after that it lets go of the held click and uses the sceptre AT the nearest secret bat (every 4 ticks, a plain item use aimed at the bat's center, never a click on a block) until the kill or Bat Attack Time, and looks down again whenever no secret bat is in sight. With the Hyperion it keeps looking straight down. **A breaker node keeps mining after it fires**: any of its blocks that were out of reach (or waiting for charges) go as soon as they come into reach - falling through the hole it made included - until another node fires, you leave the room, or ten seconds pass. A breaker node whose blocks have grown back fires again on its own only while you stand on it and at least one of its blocks is there - just that node, one dig per standing block, once until they are gone again; nothing fires when they are all gone or you are elsewhere. No warp of a chain ever lands on a block without a completely flat, full top face - no bottom slab, low half of a stair, chest, soul sand, farmland, path, bed, trapdoor, carpet, pressure plate, pane or bars; full blocks, top slabs and double slabs are fine - and a path node you place ON one of those is still reached. A chain always ends ON the second node's block: if no warp can land on that block at all (a walled-in nook only reachable on foot), the pair is not planned and chat says so, rather than saving a chain that stops on a block next to it. `/ar replan` throws away every saved chain in every room and plans this room's again at once; every other room's is planned again the next time its route fires the pair or is edited. Each warp is an etherwarp exactly like an `ew` node's - the same sneak, held between warps, and the same landing check, with the next warp on the tick the landing is confirmed - and a path fires in the etherwarp slot of a stacked tile. The second node of a pair is the arrival: landing in it fires it (it does nothing itself), its await and whatever is stacked on its tile. A first path node with no partner yet says so in chat and does nothing. **A new block in the way**: an `ew` node remembers the line it was placed along, which was clear then. If a block now stands on that line - one that was not there when the node was placed - nothing is sent: the route waits with sneak held, logs once which block it is waiting on, and warps on the first tick that block is gone (no time limit; `/ar stop` ends the wait). A block that was already there when the node was placed changes nothing, and nodes placed before this existed never wait. **Jump node**: `/ar add jump <number>` (1-20) presses jump that many times in a row - the jump key down for one tick each, waiting before each press until you are on the ground - then the route carries on. Once a jump node has done its jumps, it (and anything on its tile) does not fire again for 3 seconds, so landing back on it does not start it over; after that the usual rules apply. The number comes straight after `jump` and is required (`/ar add jump 3 await:1` works); `/ar add jump` on its own adds nothing and says in red "Please add a number of jumps: /ar add jump <number>". The Add Jump Node key opens chat with `/ar add jump ` already typed, so you type the number and press Enter (it sends nothing by itself). The node editor has a Jumps button for the count, and a jump node saved before the count existed jumps once. **Align node**: `/ar add align w2 l2` is AP3's align in a route. Its box is the width x length you give it in blocks (AP3's spellings: `w2 l2`, `width:2 length:2` or `2x2`, 0.5 to 64; a side you leave out is 1), across the room's x and z and turned with the room, and every other node keeps its usual ring. Walking into the box fires it, and AP3's own align (the Align Tolerance, Align Timeout and reach from the AP3 settings) brings you to rest on the center of the block it was placed on, with movement keys only, then the route carries on. It ends only when you are on the point; past AP3's reach (4 blocks plus half the box) or out of time it stops the route and says why. A server correction is reported and the align carries on from where you were put. The node editor has Width and Length boxes for it, and the Add Align Node key opens chat with `/ar add align ` typed. **Missing landing block**: before any etherwarp the route makes (an `ew` node or one warp of a path), if the block it lands on is not there in his world - air, broken, not yet placed - nothing is sent: the route holds where it is with sneak still down, logs once which block it is waiting for, and warps on the tick the block is back; after 60 seconds it stops with a message. **Awaits and chests**: a node waiting on `await:<n>` keeps waiting while a screen is open - a secret chest's own window is usually the secret it is waiting for - and fires on the first tick the screen is closed. The click itself meets the await, so the route can warp before the chest's window arrives (Hypixel credits the chest on the click; the window is only its view, a round trip behind); a chest window that opens within two seconds of your click is waited under the same way. Any other screen still stops the route. While a node waits on its await the held etherwarp sneak is let go (a sneaking right click with an item in hand uses the item instead of opening the chest - with the etherwarp item that is a warp), and it comes back when the node fires, so an etherwarp after an await uses one tick after it is met. If the chest window arrives a tick after the secret count, the node starts over once it closes. Breaker edit mode (/ar edit db) now takes its right clicks ahead of every other click handler, so the Dungeon Breaker's own ability can no longer fire on a pick. The routes file keeps six decimals for every position, look, landing and saved path warp, and a node is rounded to exactly that the moment it is placed, recorded, edited or planned, so it behaves the same before and after /ar reload. **Run While Map Open** (off by default, under Mode): with it on, the Interactive Map's screen no longer holds Auto Routes back - nodes fire when you walk or land on them and a running route keeps running, sneak and stacks included, while the map is merely open. A warp the map itself is making still stops a route and takes over, as before, and every other screen (a chest, your inventory, chat, the node editor) still pauses it. **What an await counts** (since 2026-10-05): only things YOU do, never the room's "x/y Secrets" bar, which a teammate's secret raises too and a lever never touches. A secret is: your own right click on a chest, a lever or a wither-essence skull (by hand or by a route node; the same block counts once per await, so clicking a chest twice is one; a button or any other block is not a secret); the Redstone Key twice - picking the key up counts one for the await (as your routes were recorded; it is not one of the room's secrets, so Auto Secret's room count leaves it out), and putting it on the room's redstone block is one, whether you click the block yourself or Secret Aura puts it in for you (counted on the click when the key is known found and the face you click is open to air, otherwise once the server shows the key in, which covers a key a teammate found); an item you pick up yourself (item secrets have no click); a secret bat that first appears within 10 blocks of you (the spawn, not the kill, so a later node can go and kill it; bats already there when the count began do not count); and a mimic dying after you clicked its trapped chest - the trapped chest's click itself is not the secret. An await counts from the moment the node before it finished (the route's start, for the first node), so a secret you get on the way to the node counts - and so does one you got in the last half second while the node before it was still acting, within 6 blocks of the node: on Hypixel the item you warp onto is picked up in the same moment you land, before the route has seen the landing, and Secret Aura can click a chest on the landing tick. A lever or bat a teammate makes happen next to you at the same moment can still count; nothing else of theirs can. Each counted event is logged (`await secret 1: lever at x,y,z (clicked by you)`). **Empty-hand use nodes**: a `use` node with no item clicks the block it looks at by hand - a chest, a lever, a skull. `/ar add use` with nothing in your hand makes one (chat says `click block (empty hand)`), and so does an empty-hand right click on a block while recording (a click into the air still records nothing); in `/ar edit` its Item box reads `(empty hand - clicks the block)` and Item from hand with an empty hand clears it. Playback selects an empty hotbar slot if there is one (otherwise it clicks with what you hold and says so in the log), lets go of sneak, aims as recorded, and right-clicks the block in sight within 4.5 blocks exactly as vanilla does - no use packet into the air - on the firing tick; with no block in sight it stops with "nothing to click". It works with `start` and `await:<n>` like every node. **Kill Mimic** (Off by default; Off / Hyperion / Spirit Sceptre, beside Run While Map Open): after you click a trapped chest - by hand or a route node, in a dungeon with Auto Routes on - it waits for the route to be between nodes, swaps to the item, looks straight down and uses it: a wither blade once (Wither Impact implodes where you stand), a Spirit Sceptre again every interact delay until the mimic is seen dying, for the Mimic Timeout at most, until you press a movement key, until you are 16 blocks from the chest (nothing from there reaches its mimic), or until the chest has opened as a plain chest with no mimic by it a few ticks later (not every trapped chest is the mimic); Auto Secret waits for it before leaving the room - its first two uses straight down, the rest aimed at the mimic itself while its bats can reach it (so a mimic out of a floor blast's six blocks, as in Chains where the chest is six blocks over you, still dies; straight down again if it is out of reach); then your held slot goes back. A route starts no node and moves nothing while it works. No wither blade (or sceptre) in the hotbar: one chat line and nothing else. **Crypt nodes** (`/ar add crypt`, also with `start` and `await:<n>`, and in `/ar edit <n>`): placed at the crypt, it swaps to the **Crypt Weapon** setting's item (Hyperion or Spirit Sceptre), looks STRAIGHT DOWN so the item only explodes and never teleports you, and holds right click until one crypt undead or prince of yours dies, or `await:<n>` of them. It holds the use key down exactly as you would, so the game itself sends a use every 4 ticks (5 a second), the same packets as holding the button, and lets go when the node is done. It does not click on its own timer any more. In obvious mode your camera stays where it was and the held click acts on the floor under you, not on whatever your crosshair is on. The **Mimic Timeout** slider (0.5-10 s, default 5) is how long Kill Mimic keeps using the Spirit Sceptre on one mimic before giving up. The **Crypt Attack Time** slider (0.5-10 s, default 5) is how long it keeps attacking without a kill before the route moves on to the next node, in case an undead walked out of reach; no weapon in the hotbar stops the route. A kill is yours when the tab list's "Crypts: N" rises, or "A Prince falls. +1 Bonus Score" arrives, within three seconds of a use or hit of yours - a teammate's kill inside that window would count. Crypt kills only ever count for a crypt node, and secrets never do: on one tile, `boom` + `crypt await:1` + `ew await:1` blows the crypt, kills its undead, then waits for one secret of yours and warps. **Server corrections never stop a route** (since 2026-10-06): a chat line and the Correction Alarm, then it carries on from where the server put you - a warp the correction took you off is flown again from there (a path from the hop that starts nearest you), and a recorded route rejoins its path at the nearest point. **Run memory** (since 2026-10-14): for the rest of a run Auto Routes remembers every secret you got - chests, levers and skulls you clicked, items you picked up, secret bats that appeared beside you, mimics you killed, whoever took it (a route node, Secret Aura, Auto Clear's run or your own hand), with where and in which room - and which nodes' awaits were met. A node whose await was met earlier this run (the room's route run again, or the node reached a second time) does not wait again: it goes at once. A node reached for the first time also counts a secret you already got within 6 blocks of it before the route started that no other node's await has used (taken by hand, or left by a stopped route). A bat node whose bat was killed earlier this run moves on without attacking. A route that runs to its end marks its room as done for Auto Secret and Dungeon Autopilot. A new run (a new dungeon or a new sim floor) forgets all of it. **Progress HUD** (Auto Routes tab, off by default, with a Progress Rooms slider from 1 to 15; a movable and resizable element in the HUD editor): with no planner running it is one line, the room you are in, how much of its route is done and the node count (`Auto Routes: Mushroom 43%  (3/7 nodes)`, 100% once the route ran to its end). While Auto Clear, Auto Secret or Dungeon Autopilot runs it shows the run instead: the planner's name, the total percent done (rooms done over rooms done plus rooms left; for Autopilot, actions) and the estimated time left, counting down (`~1:23 left`, the same estimate as the Auto Clear Status HUD's) at the top, the room under way marked with `>` and its own route percent once that route has started, then the next Progress Rooms rooms in the order the planner chose them, and a `+N more` line for the rest. The queue is the planner's real one: Auto Clear's order after each pick, Auto Secret's rooms with a route most-unfound first, Autopilot's ranked candidates with what it will do in each (Secret, Clear, Puzzle, Explore). **Auto puzzles**: while an auto puzzle is working (turning you, walking, warping or clicking for its puzzle, and for two seconds after) no node arms and a running route stops, so a node the puzzle takes you through can no longer warp you off the puzzle; nodes arm as usual once it is done. **Server lag**: while the server lags (Hypixel's once-a-tick pings stop arriving) every wait for its answer - an etherwarp's landing, a broken block, a boom, a kill - waits that much longer, so a lag spike is never reported as a warp that did not land; with no lag the timing is unchanged. A route is complete on the tick its last node finishes (since 2026-10-15): the interact delay is only ever a wait before a NEXT node, so nothing waits after the last one. A map warp onto a start node (Go + Secret, Auto Secret, Dungeon Autopilot) also ends on the tick the server confirms its last landing (since 2026-10-15), so the start node arms then instead of about 9 ticks later; a start node that opens with a breaker, boom, use or other block or item action still waits the interact delay first. A Shadow Assassin's leap never freezes a route (killer560, 2026-10-20): Hypixel shows the leap by moving your world border off you, and the game refuses every block click outside it, so a chest clicked during the leap never opened and an await waiting on it sat at 0 for ever. Secret Aura now holds that click and makes it the tick the border comes back; and if a secret's click never registers (Secret Aura gives it up after its refused tries and has nothing else in reach, or three of your own clicks are refused) the await stops waiting, says so in chat, and the route goes on with that secret left uncollected and uncounted. An empty-hand use node holds its click for the border the same way.
- Auto Secret — **cheat build only.** Secrets the whole floor with your own Auto Routes. Start it with its key or the Auto Secret button in the new **Auto Clear** tab (next to Auto Routes; off at every start, needs Auto Routes on, refuses outside a dungeon or in the boss). It takes the rooms you can reach in the order that gets the most secrets for the least travel (since 2026-10-15): every room is worth its unfound secrets, every trip between two rooms costs the etherwarps the Interactive Map's own planner needs for it, and of all the orders it takes the one where the secrets wait least, counted to the tick each room is done - so it no longer crosses the map for two secrets while two rooms beside you have one each, but a big enough room across the map still goes first; the order is logged beside what "most unfound first" would have cost. A room behind a closed wither door or the blood door is left for later (it never opens the blood door; a wither door only when nothing else is left - below), and trap rooms and every puzzle are ignored. It warps to the room's route START node exactly as the Interactive Map's Go + Secret does (the map's own planner and executor), lets the route play to its end, and takes the next room. While you travel to a room and its route plays, it already plans the trip from where that route will leave you to the next room, so the first warp out goes the tick after the route's last node. If the room's secrets do not all count yet at that point (the action bar, the map, or your own clicks and pickups in the room), the warp waits for them at most 6 ticks, sneaking with the Aspect of the Void in hand, and goes the tick they all count. A room with no recorded route is skipped with a chat line, once a run; a route that fires no node for Route Stall seconds (default 20 - a route waiting for a walk nobody will make) is stopped and the next room taken. **Ice Fill First** (on): when the floor has Ice Fill and Auto Ice Fill is on, it goes there first and lets Auto Ice Fill do it. **Quiz First** (on, needs Auto Quiz and the Quiz Solver): the Quiz is its first goal, ahead of Blood First, and under Dungeon Autopilot too (since 2026-10-20); when the Quiz is on the map behind a wither door it opens that door first (with a key in hand, or a key lying in reach; with neither the run goes on and asks again every decision, `[AutoSecret] Quiz first: opening the wither door at cell N`); a standalone Auto Clear run goes into the Quiz before any room's mobs and waits there while Auto Quiz answers (`[AutoClear] Quiz first: ...`, `[AutoClear] Quiz first over: every question answered`). Auto Secret steps into the room, which starts Oruo's countdown, and leaves at once - and then, while each question's cooldown runs (11 s before the first, then about 7 s from an answer to the next question opening), it does Ice Fill during the first and, between the later ones, secret rooms whose whole round trip (the trip there, the route, the trip back to the answer spot, by the Interactive Map's trip estimates) fits in the time left; with nothing that fits it waits at the Quiz, and it is back for each answer. **Insta Clear First** (on): before secreting, it warps into uncleared rooms only through entries the Insta Clear tracker already knows clear the room (never a guessed one). Never a room in front of a shut wither door or the blood door (a key room, below): its key drops at its last starred death, so an insta clear would lose it. When no secret room is left: each unfinished puzzle nobody else is standing in, waiting Puzzle Wait seconds (default 30) for it to be done - or not at all once that puzzle's own auto has ended its last step there (not the Quiz, whose auto ends after every answer); then uncleared rooms are always handed to Auto Clear (it carries on secreting once Auto Clear has cleared it); when it is done it stops quietly, with no chat line. It never waits at a door while anything else is useful, but when the only way on is a closed wither door it opens it itself: it goes to the door that leads to the most unfound secrets and rooms still to explore or clear (the Interactive Map's Locked Door pathing) and right-clicks it with the team's wither key, read off the sidebar; with no key it waits there and says so, and if a teammate (or Auto Clear) opens it first it simply carries on. Only after that does it warp to the nearest uncleared room and hand control back. It stops for good when every secret is found (the tab list's 100%) and no uncleared mob room or unfinished puzzle is left for the endgame, the boss starts, the run ends, you die, or you press a movement key (a key already held when it started does not count until you let go). A server correction never stops it: it says what happened and where in chat, sounds the correction alarm, and plans again from where the server put you. Chat says when it starts and always why it stopped, plus a real failure (a room with no route, a trip given up, a key or door it is stuck at); each room it goes to, finishes or waits on is logged, not said (killer560, 2026-10-16: "only have a message for started auto clear or stopped auto clear type things"). The tab and the Status HUD show what it is doing. On Hypixel it sends nothing of its own - every warp is an Interactive Map warp and every node a route node, with their packets. A room whose route has run to its end this run - by Auto Secret, Autopilot or by hand - counts as fully secreted and is never gone back to, even if the route leaves a secret behind (since 2026-10-14). While an auto puzzle is working, Auto Secret starts no trip, route or door and none of its waits time out; it logs "Waiting for Auto <puzzle> to finish" and carries on when the puzzle is done.
- Auto Clear — **cheat build only**, off by default. While it kills, no secret is clicked: Secret Aura and Secret Triggerbot hold back (unless an Auto Routes route is running, which decides as always), and its held weapon is used in the air instead of on a chest, lever or skull its look meets, so the room's route still finds them. Kills the STARRED mobs of the dungeon's mob rooms (normal, rare and champion rooms - a starred miniboss included; never puzzle, trap, fairy, blood or entrance rooms) with your Hyperion or Spirit Sceptre, never by melee. Switch it on in the **Auto Clear** tab (its section is below Auto Secret's), then start and stop it with its Clear Toggle Key or `/autoclear`; it refuses outside a dungeon and in the boss. **Clear Mode**: Any Mob Room clears every uncleared mob room - first the rooms off the blood rush, the branch (split) with the most rooms first and nearest first inside it, then the rooms on the blood rush from the Entrance toward the Blood door; Blood Rush Split clears only the uncleared mob rooms on the shortest room path from the Entrance to the room in front of the Blood door (locked doors counted as open) - that reading of "the rooms on the blood rush" is a guess and says so in its tooltip. It finds the mobs the way Mob ESP does (the "✯ ... ❤" name stand resolved down to its mob). **Clear Weapon** Hyperion: Wither Impact, a 10-block dash and a 6-block blast where you land - aimed straight down once the mob is in reach; Spirit Sceptre: a bat that explodes (6-block blast) on the first block or mob it hits, aimed straight at the mob or at a block beside it (numbers from hypixelskyblock.minecraft.wiki). Because both are blasts, a mob standing inside a wall still dies when the blast goes off within reach of it. The use key is held, as the Auto Routes crypt node holds it, with your body turned and the camera left alone. Where it stands is planned for the whole room at once (since 2026-10-15): of the blocks it can etherwarp onto, the fewest that between them put every starred mob of the room in reach of the weapon, nearest first - so three mobs nine blocks apart in a line take two repositions (one block between the first two), not three; the plan is logged beside what taking the nearest mob first would have needed, and planned again when a mob dies or moves. Getting there is decided per hop by estimated time: a near mob with an open line gets Wither Impact hops toward it (**Hyperion Hops**, on; needs a Hyperion in the hotbar even with the sceptre as the weapon) - a hop that lands in reach kills on the way, and is only used where it reaches every mob the planned block for that mob would (so it never costs a reposition the plan does not) - and a far or walled-off one an Interactive Map etherwarp chain onto the planned block. A Spirit Sceptre bat is priced in too (since 2026-10-19): it needs no move but has to fly there, about 1 block a tick (the sim's bat speed; Hypixel's is unmeasured) plus a tick to launch and one to swap to it. With the Hyperion as the weapon and a sceptre in the hotbar, a mob out of the blast that a bat from where you stand reaches is shot with the bat when that is quicker than the hops and the etherwarp; with the sceptre as the weapon, a bat that would fly further than an etherwarp onto the mob's block and a shot down would take is replaced by the etherwarp. **Walk When Faster** (on, since 2026-10-19): walking is priced in the same way - sprinting, stepping up and jumping ledges your jump clears (Jump Boost included) over the blocks around you, at your real speed, ending the tick the weapon reaches the mob - against the etherwarp chain to the same block (about 5 ticks plus 2 a warp), and taken only when it is strictly quicker, so it walks to a mob a few blocks off across open floor or a short hop up a ledge and etherwarps anywhere further; roam trips choose the same way. A walk goes by your real position, presses keys only, turns your body with the camera left alone, and one that stops getting closer (or is knocked off its way) etherwarps to the same block instead; goals over 20 blocks away are never walked. Each choice is logged once with both estimates. It never waits at a door while anything else is left to do; when the only way on is a shut wither door it goes to the door with the Interactive Map's door pathing and right-clicks it open with the Wither Key, or, with no key yet, waits there and says so in chat (it never opens the blood door). A mob that will not die within Mob Timeout (8 s) is skipped, and a room with no starred mob in sight that never shows cleared is first walked (since 2026-10-19): a starred mob of the room that went out of the client's view alive (20 blocks or more away when last seen), or one skipped as out of reach, is gone for first and at once (a quarter second with nothing in sight): an etherwarp onto a block that reaches where it was last seen, nearest first, each lost mob once and at most 6 such trips a room; with none known, after 2 s with nothing in sight it etherwarps to up to 4 spots across the room, one per quadrant of its tiles and each the farthest from where it has already looked, looking 1.5 s from each and trying skipped mobs again from there, for at most 25 s in all; only then is the room given up with a chat line. **King Midas** (since 2026-10-20): its mob is not there until the gold altar in the middle of the room (the tomb at the end of the red carpet, its 35-slab smooth stone lid) is blown open, so with no starred mob in sight and the altar standing it takes the Superboom from your hotbar, etherwarps to a block within reach of the lid if it has to, turns to it and taps it - the same packets as an Auto Routes boom node - then waits for the lid to break (no fixed delay; three Superbooms that change nothing give the room up) and clears the room as any other: the mob that comes out is killed and the room is done when it dies. No Superboom in the hotbar gives the room up with a chat line. Standing on its planned block for a mob that has walked out of reach, it etherwarps to a block that reaches the mob now before ever skipping it, and with the sceptre a bat straight down at its feet counts as an aim (the plan's own assumption). Its own trips (onto a mob's block, a roam spot, a key) end on the tick the server confirms the last landing, with no arrival settle (since 2026-10-19; about 9 ticks a trip sooner). With mana under a quarter of your maximum (the action bar's figure), the sceptre sends one bat at a time at a mob - the next once the last should have hit - so the bar is not run dry under etherwarps; above it the held use repeats every 4 ticks as before. It stops when you press a movement, jump or attack key (one already held when it started does not count), in the boss, when you die, or when nothing is left; a server correction never stops it - it says so in chat, sounds the correction alarm and plans again from where you were put. What it is doing shows in the shared Status HUD (below). Auto Secret always hands it the rooms it has to clear. While an auto puzzle is working it waits where it is, holding nothing, and starts no trip until the puzzle is done (since 2026-10-14). Its waits for the server (a Hyperion hop's landing, a mob dying, a room's mobs showing up) grow by any server lag, so lag never skips a mob or gives up a hop; with no lag they are unchanged. **Pick Up Wither Keys** (on, since 2026-10-19): a Wither Key lying within 40 blocks is picked up before anything else - an Interactive Map etherwarp onto the nearest block within pickup range (Key Base Range + 5), then it stands there until the team's key count goes up or the key goes; a key not taken within 2 s, or one the map cannot get closer to, is left for the rest of the run. Since 2026-10-20: a starred mob that walks out of its room by a hair (up to 1.5 blocks past the room's tiles) still counts as that room's and is still targeted where it stands, instead of the room being roamed for it; only one further out, out of view or dead stops counting. Every starred mob's last known spot is kept for the whole run (not just the current room's), so a room whose mobs left the view while you were elsewhere is gone to at the nearest one's last spot rather than its nearest tile, and a spot is dropped when the mob is seen elsewhere or dies. **Predictive Bats** (on): a bat at a moving mob is aimed where the mob will be when the bat gets there (its walking speed over the last ticks, the bat's ~1 block a tick), and the log says once per mob how far ahead it aimed. **One-Tap Warp** (At Once; also After Impact or Off): when one bat is sure to kill its mob, Auto Clear stops shooting it after the first bat and moves on to the next mob (At Once: the tick the bat leaves, so the next etherwarp overlaps its flight; After Impact: the tick it should land, keeping the aim on the mob meanwhile). Sure means the health on the mob's name tag is no more than a bat's proven damage - the second highest health of a mob seen dying to exactly one of your bats this session - or no more than 80% of 2,000 x (1 + Intelligence / 100 x 0.2), the wiki's base damage and scaling with Intelligence read as your maximum mana less 100. A mob still alive half a second after its bat should have hit is shot again and never trusted again, and the proven figure drops to below its health. The decision is logged once per mob. The wiki says the Guided Bat follows your aim, so At Once may miss on Hypixel when the etherwarp turns you away; After Impact never turns away while it flies. Wither doors open as fast as the server allows (since 2026-10-20): it walks to the door when that is quicker (Walk When Faster), else etherwarps with a trip that ends on the server's answer to the landing, clicks on the first tick the door is in reach (swapping to the key and turning on the same tick, clicking the next), and then waits only for the server: the key taken means the door is opening, an answer without it means the click was refused and it clicks again, up to 3 times, before waiting there for a teammate. **Secret chest = cleared** (killer560, 2026-10-20): once Auto Clear or Auto Secret has grabbed a mob room's secret chest, the room counts as cleared for Auto Clear, Auto Secret and Dungeon Autopilot 3 s later (its mobs get a moment to show or die first), so they may leave it; if the map still does not show it cleared 30 s after the chest it counts as uncleared again and they may come back to it, once per room per run. The come-back is taken while he is near (since 2026-10-20: "leaving behind a random room uncleared and going back at the very end way out of its way"): before Auto Clear or the Autopilot moves on to a room two or more rooms from such a room, still not cleared on the map, it is cleared first, and one already due that he is next to again goes ahead of the next pick (`[AutoClear] chest rule: ... coming back to it now`, `[AutoClear] next room X (come back while near - the chest rule)`, `[AutoSecret] autopilot: clearing X now, while it is near`). Auto Clear's time left counts the trip back to any room the rule still holds. Each decision is logged. **Bat logs and /test** (since 2026-10-20): every Spirit Sceptre bat is logged from its use to its hit (log `killer560smod-bats`, lines `[Bat] #N use / seen / fly / hit / result`: the tick, his position and the look the use carried, the target's id, name tag, distance, speed and health, the bat's position every tick, the hit tick, the damage and how far the look was off the target), and every bat, etherwarp and one-warp choice Auto Clear makes gets a `[Bat] decision` line with its numbers. **/test** (cheat build), typed in a dungeon's clear with a Spirit Sceptre in the hotbar and nothing else automated running, measures the bat: it picks a mob it can see (starred first), then fires five single bats - about 10, 20 and 30 blocks from the mob aimed straight at it, then 10 blocks aimed 15 degrees to the side and 15 degrees up - walking or etherwarping (as Auto Clear chooses) to a block that far away with a clear line for each. Each shot waits on its own bat's end, never repeats, and is skipped (logged) when it cannot be set up. It ends after the five, or at once on a movement or jump key, a screen, a server correction (said with the alarm as always), death, another automation, or two minutes; then `[BatTest] summary` lines give each shot's flight time and speed, the damage, and whether the bat flew straight along the look or curved. Typing /test again ends it. **Kill speed** (since 2026-10-20): each trip is priced by its real warps (one only when a sneaking look from where he stands lands on the block) and by what this run's own trips of that many warps took, so a bat from where he stands is taken whenever it is really faster; with the Spirit Sceptre the room's stand blocks are planned within the distance a bat beats a trip (about 10 blocks in the sim, about 17 at Hypixel's trip times) and every mob a bat from here reaches is shot first; the next block is the one the fewest warps reach, and a block one warp lands on is warped to directly with no search; the use goes as a fresh press every 2 ticks (10 a second) instead of vanilla's 4-tick held repeat; the swap after a trip goes with the turn; a far aim must still hit for a few degrees of spread and must meet something (no bursts "in the air"); a mob is left to enough bats for its health on the damage bats were seen doing; and a run he started leaves a room at its last starred death (or with every mob left to a trusted bat) instead of waiting for the map's checkmark, coming back if the map has not cleared it in 3 s. Every room logs `[AutoClear] room time:` (its ticks and where they went) and every trip `[AutoClear] trip time:`. **Key rooms** (killer560, 2026-10-20: "Any room before a wither door is insta cleared, then you can no longer get a wither key"): the room in front of a shut wither door or the blood door (the side nearer the Entrance), until it is cleared on the map or the team holds keys for every shut door, holds that door's key, which drops at its last starred death. While nobody has been in such a room (the map does not show it discovered and you have not stood in it), Auto Clear never etherwarps into it, nor through it on the way to another room: it etherwarps onto the near side of its door, four blocks back, and walks in, then clears it by its mobs as any room. The insta-clear evidence behind it: an etherwarp into an undiscovered room insta cleared it 13 times of 15, into an unopened one 15 of 74, into a discovered one 1 of 75, and no walk-in has a real one on record. Auto Secret hands such a room to Auto Clear instead of warping into it for a route, and its Insta Clear First skips it. Four failed tries to walk in give the room up (never an etherwarp instead). Log lines: `[AutoClear] key room: ...`, `[InstaClear] skipped ...`.
- Dungeon Autopilot — **cheat build only.** Auto Secret, Auto Clear and Auto Puzzles run as one hands-free dungeon runner: enter a run, press the **Autopilot Key** (or the Autopilot button at the top of the **Auto Clear** tab) and it plays the clear up to the blood camp. It owns no movement of its own - every warp is the Interactive Map's, every secret a route of yours, every clear Auto Clear's and every puzzle that puzzle's Auto Puzzles auto - it only decides what is next, again after every step, from the dungeon map and the Score Calculator's reading, so a door, room or secret a teammate did changes the next choice at once. Each step it weighs every reachable thing it could do by the score it should buy and the seconds it should take (travel by the Interactive Map's warps, the route's length, a clear's time learned during the run, a per-puzzle estimate): a room is worth 140 / rooms on the floor (room score and skill), a puzzle that plus the 10 skill an unfinished puzzle costs, and a secret 40 / the floor's required secrets - but only while the S+ still needs secrets (the Score Calculator's "secrets for S+" with every room done, plus one). A known insta clear is always taken first. Since 2026-10-15 the travel is each trip's real etherwarp count from the Interactive Map's planner (searched off the game thread, while a route plays), and it weighs the whole order rather than only the next step: the order that gets the most score soonest over everything it could do, so the best score per second next no longer leads it away from two rooms worth more together (logged as "tour" beside the old pick). In Solo a mob room is cleared before its secrets (killer560, 2026-10-20: secreting first scatters the mobs): when the pick would be a room's route while its clear is still to do, Auto Clear goes first and the route goes straight after (logged "clear first: mobs dead before its secrets", then "cleared first - its secrets now"); Party keeps routes first, and a room with no mobs is unchanged. **Run Mode** Solo (300 score): routes, clears, puzzles and unidentified rooms in that order; once the Score Calculator reads 300 it finishes. Party (secrets): your routes first - an auto router's secrets beat anyone's legit ones - and clears, puzzles and unexplored rooms only when no route is left, never in a room a teammate is standing in. **Blood First** (off): first the Blood Rush Split - the uncleared mob rooms on the Entrance-to-Blood path in order, then its doors: a wither door when the team has a key, then the blood door - and the rest of the floor by the Run Mode once the blood door is open; with no key for the next door it does the rest of the floor meanwhile and tries the door again each step. **Do Puzzles** (on): puzzles are planned like rooms, only those whose auto is switched on in Auto Puzzles; it stands in the room until the puzzle's own auto has ended its last step there (since 2026-10-20; the Quiz keeps the old wait), or the map or the tab list says done or failed (or three times the puzzle's estimate). Doors: it never waits at a door while anything else is useful; when nothing reachable is left it opens the wither door with the most behind it (no key: it waits there and says so). Doors are right-clicked with an empty hand (or one holding no ability item), so the click never fires an Aspect of the Void or a Hyperion instead. The blood door it opens only in Solo, as the very last thing (it starts the Watcher), or as Blood First's goal; in Party without Blood First it never touches it. Solo then stops with "blood door open, over to you for the blood camp". It stops when you press a movement key, enter the boss, die, or the run ends; a server correction never stops it (chat line and the correction alarm, then it plans from where you were put). Every decision is logged with the candidates it weighed and each finished action with its real time beside the estimate. What it is doing shows in the shared Status HUD (below); the score estimate and the reasons for each pick are in the log only. Needs Score Calculator on for the 300 check (without it, it does everything), Auto Routes for secrets and Auto Clear switched on for clears. **Keys**: a Wither Key or Blood Key lying in a room it can reach is something to do like any other (worth two rooms while the team has no key of that kind, half a room as a spare; Party takes it alongside the routes), and Blood First goes straight for the key its next door needs. It only goes as close as the key pickup range needs - the base range (**Key Base Range**, 1 block by default: vanilla's item reach, unverified for keys - the wiki gives no number; the Magnetic Talisman was confirmed in game NOT to apply to keys) plus 5 blocks from 0.27.2 ("Increased the pickup range of Wither Keys and Blood Keys in the Catacombs by 5 blocks", a permanent Minister Perk) - so 6 blocks by default: it warps to the nearest block within that, or not at all when it is already in range, waits for the pickup and logs how far away it was when the key went. Keys belong to the whole team on Hypixel, so it counts the team's keys from the sidebar's Keys line, else from the "has obtained Wither Key!" / "opened a WITHER door!" chat lines - a teammate's pickup counts the same. **Trap rooms** only through **Auto Trap** (on, with a route for that trap; otherwise trap rooms are left out): Party plays his selected mode, Solo picks Full Trap only while the S+ still needs secrets and the trap has some, else Just Cleared. It etherwarps onto the trap route's start node from outside - the only warp into a trap room - and never plans or fires a warp while he is inside one; his route walks him out, and if a step ever leaves him inside it stops and says to walk out. **Boulder and Teleport Maze** are puzzles like the rest: once its step in a Maze or Boulder room is over (or the room shows cleared, or the puzzle done or failed) the Interactive Map may path back out, so the maze's end pad no longer ends the run there. **Crypts** come from routes: a route's crypt nodes add a bonus point each, up to the five less the crypts already blown. The mimic and the prince count only when something else got them. Like Auto Secret, it never goes back for the secrets of a room whose route has run to its end this run, and it holds while an auto puzzle is working, so a puzzle's wait no longer times out under the auto and it never warps away mid-puzzle (since 2026-10-14).
- Auto Clear Status HUD — **cheat build only.** One small HUD for every auto clear: Auto Clear, Auto Secret and Dungeon Autopilot (since 2026-10-15; it replaced the separate Auto Clear Status and Autopilot Status lines, whose reasoning text ran across the screen and over the map). Two short columns, side by side when a run does both: **Clearing** - the room, what it is doing in a word or two ("traveling", "attacking", "finding mobs", "opening door", "waiting: puzzle", "waiting: key", "stuck - retrying") and how many mob rooms are left; **Secreting** - the room, its secrets found ("secrets 3/5"), what it is doing ("running route", "last secrets", "traveling") and the next room. Auto Clear alone shows only Clearing, Auto Secret only Secreting (both when it hands Auto Clear a room), Autopilot both; the column that is not working says "paused" and names its next room, and doors, keys and puzzles show under Clearing. Each line is cut short (".."), so the box stays narrow (at most about a quarter of a 1080p screen at GUI 2, usually far less). While it runs, a last line counts down the estimated time to finish ("Done in ~1:23"): Autopilot's from its own plan - the trips between the rooms it will do, each route priced by its node count, each clear and puzzle by its learned or guessed time; Auto Secret's from the trips and its routes' node counts; Auto Clear's from the trips plus 12 s a room until it has cleared one, then its own pace this run - counting every room left, the ones behind a shut wither door too (a room not identified yet, such as the gray one behind the door, at the same per-room figure), plus each shut wither door on the way (the stop at it, the click, and a key pickup for each door beyond the keys in hand), so the time goes up when a wither door section appears on the map (since 2026-10-20). Every new pick re-estimates it. Nothing shows while nothing runs, apart from a "Stopped: ..." line for six seconds after a run ends. It follows the menu theme's colors, sits at the top center by default, moves and scales in Edit HUD like every element, and in game steps out from under the Dungeon Map if the two would overlap. One switch: **Status HUD** in the Auto Clear tab's Dungeon Autopilot section (on; it takes over the old Autopilot HUD switch's value).
- Auto Trap — **cheat build only**, off by default, its own **Auto Trap** tab next to Auto Clear. Plays your trap-room routes for the two trap rooms, **New Trap** and **Old Trap**, through Auto Routes (switch Auto Routes on). Each trap has two route slots, **Full Trap** (the whole trap) and **Just Cleared** (only what gets the room cleared), and a **Mode** picking which plays; **Capture** copies your Auto Routes route for that room into the selected slot and **Clear** empties it, and the tab shows each slot's route (captured or bundled, node count, when saved). When on, the trap route is what Auto Routes plays in that room (never while you are recording one). Its start node is the entry: the Interactive Map etherwarps onto it from outside - the only etherwarp into a trap room; a route with an etherwarp node is refused, because no ability works inside a trap room, and the map itself never plans or fires a warp from inside one. A path node pair is kept (since 2026-10-19): in a trap room it walks from its first node to its second with AP3's path planner - real key presses, no etherwarp, re-planning when he drifts off it and after a server correction - and stops on the second node the way an AP3 align does (at rest on its point, no overshoot). The planned walk is saved with the route on the path node, like a path's warps, so a later run that starts from the same spot at about the same speed plays it without planning again (moving either node plans it afresh). If AP3 finds no walk (a wall, a gap or lava between them, or no ground under the second node) the route stops there and says why in chat. Every re-plan of a trap-room pair - a drift, a server correction, a changed start, the node editor's Replan or `/ar replan` (which drop the saved walk) - uses AP3's walk planner; nothing plans or flies an etherwarp for it (since 2026-10-20). Only New Trap and Old Trap count: Arrow Trap is an ordinary room and its path pairs warp. Your route walks you out, and once you are 2.5 blocks past the trap room's edge (since 2026-10-15 - right at the edge the server can still count you inside and refuse the next warp) everything trap-related stops - a trap route still playing is stopped - so whatever comes next starts from where you are. Dungeon Autopilot does trap rooms only through Auto Trap. Your captures are saved in `config/killer560/dungeons/autotrap/killer560smod-autotrap.json`; routes bundled with the mod go in `assets/killer560smod/autotrap/routes.json` (empty for now).
- Auto Secret Insta Clear Tracker — **cheat build only.** A passive recorder that measures which room entries make Hypixel count a room as cleared without its starred mobs being killed, so Auto Secret only tries an insta clear it has evidence for. Every time you enter a room in a dungeon - by etherwarp, any other teleport, or on foot, by hand or by any automation - it notes the room, the entry (which neighboring tile you came from and the block you landed on, both in the room's own rotation-independent frame, so the same entry matches across runs and rotations), how you got there, whether the room had starred mobs, and then watches the room for 15 seconds: if the dungeon map flips it to cleared while starred mobs are still standing it is an insta clear; if it flips only after every starred mob in it died, or you stopped in it and it never flips, it is a failure. A room you only pass through - you leave it within 3 seconds and it does not flip while you are in it or within 1.5 seconds of leaving, like every room an Interactive Map path lands in on the way - is recorded as a pass-through and never counts as a failure. When a room flips before any of its mobs could be seen (mid-path, a fraction of a second after you land), it keeps watching for up to a minute: Hypixel leaves an insta-cleared room's mobs standing, so starred mobs seen there later make it an insta clear, standing in it with none makes it a room without starred mobs, and never seeing it counts for nothing. A starred mob that disappears counts as a kill only while you are in its room. The Entrance is never recorded. Entries made by an Interactive Map path, its final landing included, are recorded as etherwarps by the Interactive Map. Rooms already cleared, rooms with no starred mobs, and flips it could not see (no dungeon map, or you were too far away) are kept but count for nothing. A file written before these rules (version 1) is migrated on first load: its failures are kept but no longer counted (most were pass-throughs), its Entrance entries are dropped, and the original file is first copied aside unchanged as `insta-clear.v1-<time>.json`. An entry is known to insta clear only after at least 2 insta clears and not one failure (`/autosecret instaclear threshold <n>` changes the 2; `window <seconds>` the 15). `/autosecret instaclear` lists every room with its entries, insta clears, failures and median time to clear; `room <name>` lists one room's entries, `last` shows the last entry in full, and `mark insta|notinsta|ignore|measured` overrides the last entry's outcome by hand. Evidence accumulates across runs in `config/killer560/dungeons/autosecret/insta-clear.json`. It sends nothing to the server.
- Scrollable Tooltips — the mouse wheel scrolls an item tooltip that is taller than the screen, with an optional hold-a-key modifier. Short tooltips are untouched and the wheel still works normally on them.
- Custom Enchant Colors — recolors enchantment names in item lore by SkyHanni-style tier (Poor/Good/Great/Perfect, plus a distinct Ultimate color), not per enchant — matches SkyHanni's own default colors and tier maths. Perfect defaults to an animated chroma rainbow like SkyHanni's real default, which flows diagonally like a waterfall, top left to bottom right in gentle waves, across every enchant line of the tooltip at once, with Chroma Speed and Chroma Size sliders; a static color and Bold are options. True Max Detection (on by default) colors a level that only exists above an enchant's normal cap — Giant Killer VII, Sharpness VII, Growth VII, Ender Slayer VII, and others reached only through Experiments/the Dark Auction, not the enchanting table or ordinary book combining — as Perfect instead of an ordinary high level; turning it off falls back to never calling anything above Good "Perfect". Only acts on items Hypixel actually tags with enchantment data.
- Terminal Aura — **cheat build only.** Opens P3 terminals by itself when one comes within range (range, delay, ground-only, an after-leap delay so you don't steal the terminal the person you leapt to was walking into, and **Pause On Movement Keys**, which holds off while you hold WASD or jump — it reads the keys rather than your velocity, so walking into a wall counts and being knocked about does not; sneak is not one of them), and an **Aura FOV** slider (Any by default) that limits it to terminals in front of you and **Turn To Terminal** (on by default): for a terminal your look does not hit, it turns your body - never your camera - to it for one tick, clicks on the next and turns back, because a terminal clicked behind you without it draws GrimAC's Hitboxes flag. It skips any terminal whose stand is above your eyes, since terminals no longer open from below - the rule QUOI added on 2026-10-07. Range is a slider from 0.5 to 4.5 blocks and turns red past 3.0, which is the furthest measured against a live GrimAC without drawing a Reach flag — a terminal is an armor stand, and an entity gets a tighter reach limit than a block's 4.5. Opening only — Auto Terminals is what solves them — and it never rotates your camera.
- Terminal Triggerbot — **cheat build only.** Same shape as Secret Triggerbot: opens the P3 terminal your crosshair is already on, after a configurable delay, with a cooldown and a reach limit. Delay and Cooldown count whole game ticks (rounded up to 50 ms); a Delay of 0 clicks on the tick the crosshair lands on the terminal. Never moves your camera.
- Goldor Triggerbot — **cheat build only.** While your crosshair is on Goldor in F7/M7 P3 (ray-traced out to its Range, so it works from across the arena), clicks him at a rate rolled inside a CPS band. **Aim Delay** (0-500 ms, 0 by default) is how long your crosshair must be on him before the first click, rounded up to whole 50 ms game ticks since clicks go out on the tick. Never moves your camera.
- Fast/Auto Leap — **cheat build only.** (its own tab, separate from Leap Menu) QUOI's Auto Leap: a leap to the right teammate for where you are (door opener, Crystal (P1), predev, pads, PY healer, Storm death, P3 sections, middle, P5, relic). Each leap is one row: a Fast switch (left-click a Spirit Leap or Infinileap there and it leaps) and an Auto switch (it leaps by itself when its trigger happens), each on or off by itself, and its Target button, which cycles through the five classes and your party members by name (left click forward, right click back); a class goes to whoever has it, with the class picks you made in Leap Order applied. P3 has a target per section (S1-S4). Target Mode Posmsg instead goes to whoever posted that position in party chat, with each leap's Target as the backup; block inputs / fast mode. An Auto leap takes one step a tick: if the leap is not held it swaps to it (hotbar only - with no leap in the hotbar it says so and does nothing), uses it, clicks the target's head, then swaps to Return To (your previous slot, Stay on Leap for no swap back, or a fixed slot 1-9); a step that fails (no leap, the menu did not open, the target is not in it) says which in chat. The leap menu never shows on screen during a fast/auto leap.
- Boss levers (Secret Aura tab, "In Boss"; this was the separate Lever Aura until 2026-10-15) — **cheat build only.** The Secret Aura tab holds two features side by side, Secret Aura and In Boss, each with its own switch (the card is on while either is). Under Levers, In Rooms (Secret Aura's lever clicking in rooms, only while Secret Aura is on) and In Boss. In Boss works with Secret Aura off; with Secret Aura on it also lets the aura click the other F7 P3 levers it knows (the old "F7 Boss Levers"). An old Lever Aura switch that was ON loads as In Boss ON; its Section Levers, Boss Lever Range, CPS, Swing Hand and Chat Feedback are unchanged. F7/M7 P3 Section 2: optional S2 section levers (once S2 opens, and optionally early), each clicked at most once, no camera turn (a lever behind you gets your body - never your camera - turned to it for one tick first, as Terminal Aura's Turn To Terminal does, because a click your look misses draws GrimAC's RotationPlace flag); paced by a CPS slider (1-20 flicks a second, each gap varying a little around the average). The Lights device is not part of it any more: see Lights Device
