a still for now) or an acid cave (blocky cave walls and stalactites, some dripping, framing a glowing acid lake with drifting ripples, bubbles that rise and pop and rings on its surface, an acid waterfall pouring from the ceiling with a foaming splash and rising mist, green fog drifting over it all, and on a ledge bottom left the silhouette of the theme designer's cat-eared character swinging a sword at a big Minecraft slime that hops, squishes and flashes when hit; the earlier forest at dusk and the simpler fireflies scene are kept in the code), Ocean Cavern Falls (killer560's own picture of a dark teal Minecraft cavern, animated through a shader - the whole curtain of water pouring down (every streak of the picture lit by dashes rushing down its own column, turbulent sheets and surges rolling down the falls, fine fast threads in front, spray churning up where it lands), the lake itself moving (waves rolling toward the viewer and rings spreading from where the falls land bend its reflections, the falls' reflection shimmers and stretches, current lines drift across, glints ride the crests) and the mist at the foot billowing, drawn from a 2560x1440 texture, bright soft streaks falling in front and fading into the mist, the small falls on the cliffs running with foam at their feet, mist billowing and drifting where it all lands, ripples spreading and glints coming and going on the dark lake, the glowing specks on the cliffs twinkling and motes of spray drifting up, the feet of the falls glowing in a slow pulse, the view itself still; the older drawn waterfall stays in the code), Dark a Minecraft-styled Milky Way (killer560's own picture graded almost black and white - a blocky galaxy band with dark dust lanes running down to a bright core low on the right, blocky pines and hills, and a small caped character sitting on a stepped grass-block hill at the lower left looking up - animated: the bright stars twinkle as crisp crosses and faint ones flicker, a blocky shooting star crosses every few seconds away from the title, the core's glow breathes, haze drifts along the hills and her cape flutters; the same picture without the character and the older drifting starfield stay in the code), Light soft drifting lights and slanted rays on a light backdrop, its one scene in both Fancy and Simple (drawn by the mod's own shaders at the screen's full resolution: the rays fade with soft sides, the glow and the vignette are smooth, every gradient is dithered with blue noise so no banding shows on any screen, and the drifting lights are perfectly antialiased discs gliding between pixels at any GUI scale), and Custom the Simple embers in the menu's own Custom colors, in both Fancy and Simple (killer560, 2026-10-16: it has no painted scene of its own). Every background moves on time, not frames, and while the themed background is on screen out of a world the game runs menus at your own Max Framerate (120 by default; a lower setting is kept, Unlimited stays unlimited, VSync still applies) instead of Minecraft's 60 FPS menu cap. In Menu Theme's settings, Main Menu Style picks Match Menu Theme (the main menu uses the mod menu's theme and its background, the default), Separate Theme (a theme of its own: Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Viridian or the menu's Custom colors) or Custom (a theme of its own plus Background, Particles Off/Low/Normal/High, Animation Speed 25-200% and Button Panel on/off). Right under it, Main Menu Scene picks Fancy (the default) or Simple for whichever theme the style gives (left click the next, right click back); the choice is saved and also sets the background behind Minecraft's other menus. Custom's Background picker lists every scene: Theme Default (the theme's scene in the Main Menu Scene mode), Amber Aurora, Cavern Falls, Milky Way, Aurora, Soft Light, Slime Boss, Plain, and the Simple Embers, Bubbles, Stardust and Fireflies. The yellow splash text Minecraft draws beside the logo is never shown on the themed main menu, in any theme or style; with the main menu theming off the title screen is vanilla's and keeps it. A Mod Settings button in the column opens the mod menu from the main menu (Escape returns to it); every setting works there, and the HUD editors, which need a world, say "Join a world to use Edit HUD" instead of opening. Themed Vanilla Menus (on by default, in the same section) draws every other menu - Singleplayer and Create New World, Multiplayer and its Add Server and Direct Connection pages, Options and every page under it, the pause menu (the sim's Change Room included), loading, disconnected and confirm screens, the mod list and other mods' screens - in the main menu's theme, following Main Menu Style: out of a world the main menu's own background behind a veil (world and server lists sit on an opaque panel so their text stays readable), and its buttons, text fields, sliders, checkboxes, tabs, lists, scrollbars and tooltips everywhere. A theme change shows at once on whatever menu is open. Nothing is moved or removed. One switch in Display Options turns it all off; chests and inventories stay normal. Title Screen Switcher (only in the Prism instance "Title Screen GUI", which is for looking at the main menus and never joins a server; it has no setting and every other instance never shows it): the Multiplayer button is replaced by Main Menu Looks, which opens a picker listing every main menu look by theme - Vanilla (the plain title screen), Amber Embers / New / Aurora / Simple, Ocean Waterfall / Cavern Falls / Simple, Dark Milky Way / Simple, Light Soft Light, Viridian Slime Boss / Simple, Custom Simple - with the one on screen lit; a click shows that look at once behind the picker, Done goes back to the title screen, and Saved Look goes back to the instance's own settings. A small button in the top right corner of the title screen names the look on screen and steps through the same list: left click the next, right click the previous. A look picked this way changes no setting, so the next launch starts on the saved one. It is turned on by the marker file `killer560smod-title-screen-gui` in the instance's game folder
- Fonts — Menu Theme's Font setting draws text in another font: Minecraft (the default), four bundled fonts (Lato, Space Mono, VT323 and Press Start 2P, all under the SIL Open Font License, listed in assets/killer560smod/fonts/LICENSES.txt), or any .ttf or .otf you import: Open Fonts Folder opens the folder, drop the file in, and it is in the Font list (Reload Fonts reads the folder again; a file that is not a font says so and Minecraft's font stays). Font Applies To picks Mod Menu Only (the mod's own screens) or Everything (every line of text: chat, the HUD, scoreboards, tooltips, Minecraft's menus and name tags). Font Size goes from 70% to 140%. Characters a font lacks (Hypixel's symbols, accents) still come from Minecraft's font. Kept across restarts and in profiles; imported fonts ride in profiles too
- Theme Extras — Menu Theme's Theme Extras section gives each theme (Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean, Green and Custom) things besides its colors; Extras For picks the theme being set up, starting on the menu's own. Theme Music (default ON; each theme plays its own track): while the main menu is open, the theme it shows plays its track on a loop, and leaving the main menu for a world or a server stops it at once (Options, Singleplayer, Multiplayer and the mod menu opened from the title screen keep it going). Each preset theme has its own bundled loop, original music by the mod's owner; a theme's Music row picks Theme Track, Off, or any bundled loop (Custom has no loop of its own). Play Preview, under it, plays that track once at Music Volume so it can be heard right there; a second press, picking another track or closing the menu stops it, and Minecraft's menu music and the main menu's own track wait while it plays. Music Volume runs 0-100% on its own slider, not the game's Music slider; while a theme track plays, Minecraft's own menu music waits. Import Music takes the sound or video whose path is on the clipboard (Shift + right click the file, Copy as path), as Click Sounds does, and makes it that theme's music; an .ogg is kept as it is, anything else is converted by ffmpeg (which must be installed), its first 10 minutes. Imported music is streamed, never held whole, and Open Music Folder opens where it is kept. Theme Font (default Keep Current): a theme can bring any font the Font setting offers (Minecraft's, the bundled ones or an imported one), used while the mod menu is in that theme; Font Applies To and Font Size stay the Font setting's, and Keep Current leaves the Font setting as it is. Theme Background (default Theme Scene): Import Background takes the video or GIF whose path is on the clipboard (Shift + right click the file, Copy as path), as Click Sounds does, and makes it that theme's main menu background, cropped to fill the screen and looping; Open Backgrounds Folder opens where they are kept. A GIF is used as it is; a video (and a GIF too big to hold in memory) is turned by ffmpeg, which must be installed, into frames at 20 fps, its first 60 seconds at up to 960x540. Decoding runs off the render thread; until the first frame is ready, and if a file is missing or broken, the theme's own scene shows.
- Arrow Align — F7/M7 third device solver: clicks-needed numbers on each frame, Prevent Misclicks (crouch to override); **cheat build:** Trigger Bot with a **CPS range** (1-20, a fresh random gap between the two ends for every click; an old fixed delay becomes its CPS as the low end of a band two wide) and Aura, which clicks every frame in range that still needs it at an **Aura CPS** you set from 1 to 20 (each gap varies a little, the average is what you set; 20 is one click a game tick). It clicks frames already under your crosshair first and only turns your body, never your camera, for the others, so a frame you are looking at goes at the full rate while each one it has to turn to costs an extra tick. An old Min/Max Delay setting becomes the CPS nearest its middle. Before stage S3 starts the Aura takes the lowest, leftmost arrow to one click from done and leaves it there (**Leave Last Arrow**, on by default), so the device completes on a single click once S3 starts. **Aura Clicks Per Tick** (1-4, default 1) sends several clicks on a frame within one tick; tested clean against GrimAC. The Trigger Bot, the Aura and Leave Last Arrow each have a **Disable While Crouching** switch (on by default) that stops them while you hold crouch.
- Secret Triggerbot — **cheat build only.** Clicks a secret (chest, lever, redstone key, wither essence) when you look at it, after a delay; never re-clicks a looted secret; optional slot swap
- Starred Mob Hitboxes (named Dungeon ESP on the cheat build) — boxes or glow on starred mobs and secret bats, each with its own color, line-of-sight only; Room Scoped highlights every starred mob in the room you are standing in (plus an adjustable margin for anything that wandered just outside) instead of using a flat range. **Blood Mobs** (off, red by default, its own color) highlights the Watcher's blood mobs the same way - the ones Blood Camp sees land, and any mob whose Hypixel name tag names one of the Watcher's mobs (Mr. Dead, Revoker, Tear, Skull, Walker, Cannibal, Frost, Putrid, Reaper, Vader, Flamer, Freak, Leech, Mute, Ooze, Parasite, Psycho, Wandering Soul, Bonzo, Scarf, Spirit Bear, Livid, and F7's Bigfoot, Jolly Pink Giant, L.A.S.R. and The Diamond Giant), so one that landed while you were away counts too - kept by identity for as long as they live, so one that walks out of the Blood Room stays highlighted; Range and Room Scoped never limit them. It tracks them itself, so Blood Camp does not have to be on. In the cheat build the ESP switch draws them through walls like the others
- Wither Boss Highlight — the real F7/M7 wither bosses (Maxor, Storm, Goldor, Necron) at any range with no range limit, as a glow hitbox or a filled hitbox, still line-of-sight only; the cheat build adds a red ESP section with ONE **ESP** switch for everything in it: starred mobs and bats through walls and the wither bosses through walls at any range, in the same wither color as the legit highlight (the ESP has no color of its own); the **Tracer** option (and its thickness) is in the Wither Boss Highlight section on both builds: the legit highlight draws the line only to a wither boss you can see (line of sight), the cheat ESP to every one through walls
- Auto Door Opener — **cheat build only.** Aura or triggerbot on locked wither/blood doors, firing only in a short window after the matching key is obtained rather than continuously. In Triggerbot mode, **Triggerbot Delay** (0-1000 ms, 0 by default) is how long your crosshair must stay on the door before it is clicked. A key message arms it only while it is switched on, and it finds the doors in the dungeon sim too. The old "Look At Door" setting is gone
- Interactive Map — **cheat build only.** The full-screen map: open it on a key, then aim at a room with the cursor and press your Start bind (a key or a mouse button, your choice) to act on it. No warp it plans lands on a block without a completely flat, full top face (no bottom slab, low stair, chest, soul sand...; a top slab is fine). A press starts flying the floor graph's plan at once, and while you fly the exact search Auto Routes path nodes use looks for the rest of the trip in fewer warps and swaps it in if you have not reached that warp yet. One press goes there in the fewest etherwarps, and of the landings those reach, the one nearest the middle of the clicked tile rather than just past its doorway - never an extra warp to get further in; a press while a path is already running cancels that path and re-plans to the new room or door instead of being ignored; a press on the room you are standing in runs that room's own secret route; two presses in quick succession path to that room's recorded route START node, so you land ready to secret it (rooms with no recorded route fall back to the room's own spot) - the **Double-Press Secrets** toggle, on by default, turns that off. A separate **Go + Secret Key** (unbound by default; a key or a mouse button) does the same thing on a single press - it goes to the room under the cursor, or the room you are in if the cursor is off the map, and runs its secrets - and works whether or not the double press is on. A press always beats a secret route that is already playing — the route is stopped and the map's goal takes over. Hover for type, secrets, crypts and who cleared it; scroll to zoom. The map never pans: dragging with any mouse button does not move it, and every press acts when you let go, however far the mouse moved - a left press goes (or retargets), and a right or middle press keeps its own action (Locked Door, Go + Secret, or reset the view on an unbound middle button); once the view is zoomed a Reset view line on the side panel puts it back. The side panel shows the room colors two to a row, the door colors, a Controls section that is collapsed to one line (Go) until you click its [+] header (it remembers), and Extra Info (crypts, bat/mimic/prince on one line, S+ secrets on a line of its own when it does not fit beside its label - shown whether or not Score Calculator is on, with a one-line "waiting" note before the run's first numbers); nothing on it overlaps at any window size or Auto Scale factor, and anything that cannot fit is clipped to the panel. Room labels (Off, Checkmarks, Secrets, Room Name, Room Name + Secrets) are ONE setting shared with the Dungeon Map - changing Room Labels in either tab changes both maps - and are drawn exactly as the Dungeon Map draws them, with the same marks (Custom Checkmark Sprite), Font Scale and Text Shadow, but it always draws the whole scanned floor whatever Funny Map says. Paths are searched leg by leg, each leg kept to the two rooms it joins, and a leg with a straight warp into the next doorway is taken without the full search - measured outside the game on a floor of real room captures, a click takes about half a millisecond on average and a few milliseconds at worst; every hop's aim is checked by casting the exact warp it will send, so it lands on the block it was planned for. **Ticks Between Etherwarps** (1-10, 1 by default) sets the pace: a map path sends its next etherwarp that many client ticks after the last, so 1 is a warp every tick. In the dungeon sim the hops chain at that pace, resolved from where the sim's server has you, the way Hypixel resolves them. When the server puts you somewhere the warp plan does not know about (a correction), it says so in chat, plays the Correction Alarm and plans the same goal again from where you are, every time - corrections do not count against its two re-plans. A server lag spike is not a missed warp: while Hypixel's once-a-tick pings are late, the 1-second "Warp N never landed" limit grows by the lag, so it neither warns nor re-plans while the server catches up (the timing with no lag is unchanged). **Trips planned ahead**: whenever the Interactive Map is on in a dungeon - the map screen open or not, with no setting of its own (off with the Interactive Map, and then none of it runs) - as soon as the floor is known and the floor graph can search in milliseconds (about half a second in), the map plans its trip to every room it knows from where you stand - each tile as a press goes there, the room's recorded spot, and its Auto Routes start node - nearest rooms first, then the rooms on the way to Blood and the rooms with routes; it does this again whenever you stand still somewhere new. Then, as rooms load in, it plans from the last node of every room's route to the start node of every other room's route (or to the room itself when it has no route), which Auto Secret and Dungeon Autopilot use for their room order and their next warp. A press whose trip is ready sends its first warp as soon as the server has the sneak, with no search; each trip is exactly the plan a press would have made, and is checked again the moment it is used (every warp cast in the world as it is then, the first one aimed again from exactly where you stand, the last landing where it was planned) - one that no longer holds is dropped and the trip planned fresh at once. A trip planned while only the quick part of the floor graph was ready is never flown once the whole graph is: for the second or so it takes to plan those again, a press there searches (a few milliseconds), so a precomputed trip never takes more warps than a fresh one. A block change drops every trip whose warps pass near it and plans the rest again soon (a door that opened may have made a shorter way). It runs a few milliseconds at a time on the map's own planner thread, giving way to any trip you ask for, and nothing of it runs outside a dungeon or in the boss.
- Terminal Solver — highlights the correct slot(s) to click on Floor 7 terminal puzzles, including Melody detection (with an optional "Send Mel Coords On Open" that types your position into party chat when the Melody terminal opens, and "Send Melody Progress" that posts "Melody 1/3 - 33%" as you finish each row). The **cheat** build additionally has an Auto Terminals mode (with a Min/Max click delay, an input-block safety toggle, and a Melody Skip Mode for how aggressively it clicks ahead) that clicks for you - see [Which jar do I download?](#installing) below. Every overlay color is customizable (per-terminal highlights, Numbers' next/after-next/3rd tiers, Rubix left- vs right-click, Melody's board palette, panel background/border and label text) with a one-click Reset Colors. With Custom GUI on, the terminal is redrawn as a compact panel of only the squares that matter, and the real chest slots behind it are fully gone: hovering where one sits shows no highlight or tooltip, and clicks and keys there (number keys, drop, pick block, shift-click) do nothing - only the panel's own squares click. Terminal titles are read with color codes and resource-pack glyphs removed, and a terminal the solver cannot read is written to the log once (its title and board), for reporting.
- Termism — a practice mode that generates fake terminal puzzles (every real type, including a real timed Melody puzzle) so you can drill them without a real dungeon run, with Auto Terminals also usable here on the cheat build to test it risk-free The menu has no explanatory line, and Random and Done have a little more room around the terminal buttons. Resizing the window keeps the current board and its timer.
- Full Block — **cheat build only.** Expands the clickable area of levers, buttons, chests, and Wither Essence blocks so real secrets are easier to click, with a master on/off toggle; each block type has a Shape setting (buttons: Flat / Full / Custom, the rest: Full / Custom), and Custom adds Width, Height and Length sliders from normal size to a full block - see [Which jar do I download?](#installing) below.
- Simon Says — highlights the correct button(s) on the F7/M7 boss-fight Simon Says device with numbered, colored boxes (next = green, then orange, then red), tracks party members' progress from their chat announcements (compatible with Odin/QUOI's own progress messages too), and can block a real click on the wrong button (Prevent Misclicks - hold Shift to override). The **cheat** build additionally has a Trigger Bot, Auto Start for the real "skip" trick (configurable clicks, exact tick spacing), and Auto Solve with Rotate / No Rotate modes (No Rotate never moves your camera, but turns your body to each button for a tick before clicking it, because a click your look misses is dropped by GrimAC as RotationPlace) and a Timer Target (11-13s from the first start-button click, same as Hypixel's own device timer, with up to ±250ms variance). Rotate mode adds Stim (between rounds the aim retraces the newest buttons of the round it just did - Stim Buttons, 1-5, default 2 - and comes back to the first button; look only, it never clicks) and a Momentum slider (Off to 100%, default Off: the click goes out once the crosshair is on the button face instead of fully settled, the camera carries part of its speed into the next turn and heads for the next button as soon as a click is sent; off, the aim is exactly as before), plus Auto Restart SS (restarts the device when it breaks), a Restart keybind (it does nothing while Auto Simon Says as a whole is off), and a Trigger Bot delay slider (ms from aiming at the right button to the click, rounded up to whole 50 ms game ticks). All of that lives in its own red Auto Simon Says section directly under Simon Says - see [Which jar do I download?](#installing) below.
- Tick Timers — real countdowns for Maxor's start, Necron's drop, Goldor's start and damage tick, and Storm's pad/lightning/purple-pillar/crush windows, driven off the real boss chat lines (timings follow SkyBlock 0.27.2's faster boss pacing, as measured by NoammAddons 1.2.9)
- Split Timers — per-segment run splits for every floor, announced in chat and shown on a HUD list in one fixed layout: **Blood Open** (run start until the blood door opens), **Watcher** (blood door until the last blood mob dies - the Watcher's "You have proven yourself" line), **Portal Entry** (from there until the boss's first line) and **Boss Entry** (the sum of those three); a line; on F7/M7 **Maxor**, **Storm**, **Terminals**, **Goldor**, **Necron**, **Dragons** (M7 only - Phase 5 starts on Necron's death line or the Wither King's first line, whichever comes first) and **Boss** (the whole boss fight); a line; then **Total** and **Lag** (how much of the run was server lag, in seconds). On other floors the boss is one row named after the boss (Bonzo, Scarf, The Professor, Thorn, Livid, Sadan), which is the boss time itself. Every time reads the same way, on the HUD and in chat: the real time, then the time without server lag in parentheses - "Blood Open: 22.39s (19.59s)" - while **Lagless Times** is on (default). If Mort's opening line is missed, the clock starts one second after "Starting in 1 second." The end-of-run chat summary lists the same rows. Watcher Move, Core Entry and the M7 Phase 5 dragon/relic lines are optional extras around the layout
- Terminal Timers — Odin-style F7/M7 P3 timing: "Panes solved in 3.21s!" after each terminal you complete, (section | phase) times added to every terminal/device/lever completion line with a per-section summary when the core opens, and the Simon Says whole-device time
- Mask Invincibility Timers — automatic active/cooldown timers for Spirit Mask, Bonzo's Mask, and Phoenix Pet, detected off the real "saved your life" chat lines. The **cheat** build additionally has Auto Mask, its own red card beside Mask Invincibility (which it needs on, for the procs and cooldowns). Mode picks Keybind or Full Auto (the default, and what an older Auto Swap setting loads as): in Keybind the Swap Key (unbound until you set it; a key or mouse button) puts on a mask when you wear none or swaps to your other mask when you do; Full Auto swaps once the mask you wear, or Phoenix, procs, and never without a proc. Swap Order picks what comes next: Mask First (the default) tries your other mask (Spirit before Bonzo when none is worn), then Phoenix; Phoenix First summons Phoenix first, then the masks. Nothing on cooldown, already worn, not owned (the Spirit / Bonzo / Phoenix switches) or already summoned (Hypixel's "You summoned your Phoenix!" and Autopet lines) is picked, and with nothing left it says so in chat once. Dungeons Only (on by default) keeps it out of everything but the Catacombs; Boss Only (off) lets it act only in the floor's boss fight, never the clear. A mask goes on through the /stats equipment menu, Phoenix by your rod's Autopet rule or the /pets menu (Phoenix Route), one interaction a tick, never under a screen except the menu the swap opened; a proc that lands while a screen is open swaps as soon as it closes (within 5 seconds). Swap Step Delay (3 to 20 ticks, default 7) is the pause between each step; an older millisecond value loads as ms / 50, rounded
- Sharp Shooter (i4) — a Solver that marks every hit target green and dots the between-column spots to aim at for predictions. The **cheat** build adds Auto i4: shoots each lit target (Rotate / No Rotate, adjustable rotation speed; No Rotate keeps your camera still and turns only your body to each shot for a tick), Terminator (with predictions) or Machine Gun Shortbow (aims each block, fires Rapid Fire at the start), auto swap to your bow, and Auto Mask with a Bonzo / Spirit / Phoenix order (in development)
- i4 Leap Out — **cheat build only**, in the Sharp Shooter (i4) tab. Leaps when your i4 device finishes (or on left-click at pre4) to a chosen class, player, or the Melody player with a backup target; Prevent Inputs blocks input during i4 and the leap
- 0 Ping Dungeon Breaker — **cheat build only.** While holding a real Dungeon Breaker item with charges left, insta-mines the exact block you're looking at the instant you start mining it, instead of waiting on your real connection's ping; obsidian is left to the server, which digs it over time, so clicking it never leaves a hole on your screen. **Don't break secrets** (off by default, on the Dungeon Breaker tab): the breaker - Zero Ping, Breaker Aura and Auto Routes breaker nodes - never breaks a secret block (chest, trapped chest, lever, Wither Essence or Redstone Key skull). Whatever the setting, the breaker never breaks anything inside a Fairy room; puzzle rooms are not affected
- Dungeon Map — a HUD copy of the real held dungeon map, on both builds showing only what the dungeon map in your hotbar shows: the rooms you have opened, and the gray "?" rooms beside them, unnamed - it never draws a room the map has not shown, even when the world has it loaded. A door the map shows gray (into a room nobody has opened) is drawn gray, as on the real map. The map item is read at the corner Hypixel draws each floor at (Entrance, Floors 1-3 with 18-pixel rooms; Floor 4 and 5-7 with 16), so a stray green run on the map cannot shift the whole reading. Rooms are colored by type (brown normal, magenta puzzle, orange trap, yellow miniboss, pink fairy, red blood, green entrance, gray unexplored), corridors flush against the rooms, multi-tile rooms merged, and the room marks (check, cross, "?") are Hypixel's own, copied pixel for pixel off the hotbar map where Hypixel drew them (with no map item to copy from - the sim, p3sim - a built-in copy of the same shapes). **Custom Checkmark Sprite** (off by default) swaps them for the mod's own outlined marks, or (Sprite: Your Own) for PNGs you draw - green.png, cleared.png, failed.png and unopened.png in the folder Open Sprite Folder opens, any size up to 64x64; a missing one uses the mod's mark. Rooms you have not opened use NoammAddons' colors: a gray question-mark room (and the door into one) is the Unopened gray, 65,65,65, and a room drawn in its own color before you have opened it (every such room on Funny Map, and the Blood Room behind its blood door) is that color darkened the way NoammAddons darkens it (darker twice, about half). **Darken Unexplored** (0 to 0.9, 0 by default) darkens all of them further; a saved 0.05, the old default, moves to 0 once. Room names and checkmarks sit inside the room's own box: an L-shaped room (Chambers, Altar...) is labeled on its row of two, never on the empty corner of its square. The room you are standing in is drawn opened, all of it, the moment you are inside its walls, without waiting for the hotbar map to redraw. The mimic's room turns a faint red once you have killed the mimic in it and stays red for the rest of the run. You are a green arrow and each teammate a white one (**Show Teammates**, on by default); a teammate out of render distance is placed from the map item's markers, matched to names in the order the tab list shows the party, and glides to each new marker instead of jumping; **Recolor by Class** colors teammates' arrows by class and puts a faint ring of their class color around their head with Player Heads; **Player Heads** (shown under Show Teammates while it is on, off by default) draws you and your teammates as your skins' faces instead, each head turned so its top points where that player faces, on this map and the Interactive Map (anyone whose skin is not known yet keeps the arrow); **Player Marker Size** (0.5x-3x) sizes arrows and heads on both maps; every arrow is inside a solid outline that contrasts with its own color so it stands out on any room. **Names Holding Leap** (off by default) writes every teammate's name under their marker while you hold a Spirit Leap or Infinileap. **Mark Reported Rooms** and **Color Rooms by Type** are on by default. The map is sized by scrolling over it in the HUD editor (there is no Room Size slider; a Room Size from an older config is folded into the map's HUD scale once). Its outline is the same square for the whole run and on every floor; inside it the grid is scaled from the floor you are on - Entrance 4x4, Floor 1 4x5, Floors 2-3 5x5, Floor 4 6x5, Floors 5-7 6x6, Master Mode the same as its floor number - so nothing re-fits or shrinks as rooms are found, and a small floor's boss arena is never drawn as rooms. In the dungeon sim, which has no hotbar map, the map shows the floor that was built (a sim holding a dungeon map in slot 9 is read like Hypixel's). **Map Theme**: Real Map (Hypixel's colors, the default), Amber, Dark, Light, Ocean and Viridian (worked out from the mod menu's presets, each room type keeping its own hue), Match Menu Theme (follows whatever theme the mod menu is in) or Custom. Room colors exist only under Custom: **New Custom Scheme** makes a scheme from the colors on the map now and opens the scheme editor - a sample floor drawn in the scheme's colors with every room type, an unexplored room, a wither door, the blood door and checkmarks, beside its name and its ten colors (each a picker), Reset to Real Map and Delete (click twice). Every scheme is saved; under Custom the Scheme setting picks one and Edit Scheme reopens it. Map Background and Border Color are pickers of their own. **Extra Info Overlay** (Map Extras) adds two centered rows under the map, laid out like NoammAddons': Secrets found / needed for S+ / the floor's total and Crypts, then Score, Deaths and the bonus kills as M (Mimic, Floor 6 and up), P (Prince) and B (the Murkbat bat), each green when done and red when not, B with a count when more than one player got theirs - at half the font size, so one screen pixel per font pixel at GUI scale 2, never wider than the map; it works with Score Calculator off. In game the Split Timers never draw on top of the map or its Extra Info. **Cheat build:** **Funny Map** (off by default, at the bottom of the tab) draws every room the mod has scanned, not just what the hotbar map shows, with names and real colors - the ones you have not been in darkened by Darken Unexplored; with it on, **Show Mimic Room** (on) tints the mimic's room from the start, from its trapped chest, in **Mimic Room Color** (20% red by default; its alpha is how strongly it blends). Without Funny Map the cheat map is the normal map. The Interactive Map always draws the whole scanned floor.
- Predictive Rooms (a setting on the Dungeon Map tab, with Special Room Guess under it, not a card of its own) — names a room on the Dungeon Map before you enter it, from what you have seen of it. Every other tick (every tick, with two rounds of extra rays round the doorway, while you can see into an unopened room) the mod casts rays from your eyes across your view (the game's own line-of-sight test) and records the first block each one hits inside an unopened room; it never reads any other block, so nothing behind a wall, round a corner or under a floor is ever used. Those blocks are compared with every room in the room library that ships with the mod (the sim's captured rooms), at all four turns and every placement that fits the floor and does not overlap an opened room; a room/turn/placement that disagrees with more than a few percent of what you saw is ruled out. Once a tile has 20 seen blocks, every placement still standing is the same room and each has matched at least 15 of them (a tenth of a second's look through a doorway, and named from up to 27 blocks back from it; the parts of a puzzle that change between runs, such as Water Board's board, never count toward those matches), the map shows that room's name and color on the tiles every remaining placement covers, and a gray "?" on the tiles only some of them cover (a room whose turn you cannot tell yet shows only the part you can be sure of, and fills in as you see more). Two different rooms still possible: nothing. Doors are never predicted; the map's doors stay the held map's. A room the map already names is left alone, so on the cheat build's full map it only adds rooms the world scan has not identified. **Special Room Guess** (same tab) splits an unopened room the held map shows is one tile across - every side the floor's edge or an opened room it is not joined to - into darkened stripes of the special rooms it can still be: Miniboss while no miniboss room is opened, Trap while no trap room is opened, Puzzle while fewer puzzle rooms are opened than the tab list's "Puzzles: (N)" (rooms Predictive Rooms has named count as found); when only one is left it is filled solid in that room's color, so the miniboss room turns yellow once the trap and every puzzle are accounted for. An unopened room whose only door is the blood door is filled as the Blood Room. On the legit map only (the cheat map already knows). Both on by default, both builds
- Secret Waypoints — real, preloaded per-room secret positions (chests, items, wither essence, bats, redstone keys) from the same public room database NoammAddons uses, shown for the room you're in only. NoammAddons' colors by default (chest magenta, item blue, bat green, essence black, key red), each box sized to its object (or a full block), drawn through walls (toggleable), with an optional name label above each one. A waypoint disappears as soon as you take that secret - click the chest/essence/key, pick up the item (or a teammate does; you can be well away from it by the time the pickup arrives) or kill the bat. Levers are waypoints too (found live in the room, in their own color), and a lever's goes as soon as you click that lever and stays gone for the run; clicking any other lever hides nothing else. Two extra toggles, Show Crypts and Show Princes (both off by default, each with its own color), box the crypt tombs and the prince's golden crypt in the room you're in, with the same style, names and through-walls as the secrets. The room database only knows how MANY crypts a room has, and a crypt's undead and the prince do not exist until the tomb is blown, so these are found from the room's own blocks: a crypt is the lid of smooth stone slabs (with the prince rule it gives the database's crypt count exactly in 118 of the 134 captured rooms, so expect the odd decorative slab panel to be boxed and the odd tomb to be missed; a slab staircase is never a crypt), a prince is gold set beside a smooth stone slab or a sea lantern walled in polished andesite, with nothing on top of it (so Pirate's carpeted lantern tables are not princes). Once a tomb is blown its waypoint goes at once, whether or not you kill what came out. A part of the room your client has not loaded (or has let go of) never counts as empty: a crypt there is not judged opened, and a lever there keeps the waypoint it had when it was last seen
- Blood Camp — tracks the Watcher and his blood mobs in the Blood Room on every floor by their real skull skins, predicting where each mob is about to land and counting down until it's vulnerable. It draws what NoammAddons' Blood Camp helper draws, using the same prediction: the trip starts at the skull's spot in the wall and the landing spot is 16.1 blocks along the skull's direction of travel for the Watcher's first wave, 11.9 after it. Over each landing spot there's a 1x1x1 outline box from 1.5 to 2.5 blocks above it (2.5 px, magenta, turning green once your ping is longer than the time left). A cyan **Spawn Line** (2 px by default, width 1-10) runs from the skull's position 2 blocks above the stand, drawn where it is that frame, to the landing spot. The seconds left show with a shadow at **Timer Text Scale** 2 by default, one decimal and no unit, in **Timer Text Color** (white by default, a color picker with a reset). All of it shows through walls from the skull's first move until it's removed. **Kill Sound** (on) plays once per mob, on the tick the Trigger Bot is to hit it - its kill timer moved by Trigger Offset (one tick after the box turns green by default), never before the Kill Popup's KILL once the Watcher has greeted the room - and only for a skull that really flew out (one shuffling on the wall makes no sound); mobs due together share one sound. Its sound is Arrow Hit by default (click to cycle) at **Sound Volume** (0.5). The old Countdown Start Sound, which played as each mob started to spawn, is gone. Mobs that reach the same moment together make one sound, not a burst. The cheat build's Trigger Bot and Aura live in their own **Auto Blood Camp** tab. A lag spike that stops the skull's movement for a moment no longer loses the line: the trip, its path and its countdown are kept and pick up again when the movement resumes; only a skull that already arrived, or one gone quiet for 5 s, starts a new trip.
- Auto Blood Camp — **cheat build only**, its own tab next to Auto Trap (split out of the Blood Camp tab on 2026-10-07; same settings, same saved values). Both its automations only ever act on blood mobs - the mobs Blood Camp saw land at the end of a trip, or whose name tag names a Watcher mob, never any other mob, however close or in view - and a click only goes when nothing but blood mobs inside the Blood Room is anywhere along its line (a beam or an arrow carries on past its target), so a shot cannot hit a mob outside the room. Both only act while you are inside the Blood Room (the room tile the Watcher is in, or a room the map calls the Blood Room - a map that took the Blood Room for some other room no longer stops them); outside it Blood Camp still draws, but nothing clicks or turns. Neither ever acts through a wall: the Aura only goes for a mob a line from your eye reaches without meeting a block, and a click only goes when your look reaches the mob before any block (Blood Camp still draws the ones behind walls). Neither clicks faster than your weapon allows a full-damage hit: on Hypixel round(10 / (1 + Attack Speed / 100)) ticks apart (10 at 0 Attack Speed, 5 at 100; a shortbow rounds up), with Attack Speed read from the tab list's Stats widget when it shows it, otherwise added up from the "Attack Speed: +N%" lines of your held item and armor (capped at 100); elsewhere the vanilla attack cooldown. **Trigger Bot** left-clicks a blood mob at its kill timer - the moment its box turns green and the Kill Sound plays - while your aim is roughly on its landing spot (the spot up through the drawn box) or on the mob - your look only has to pass within half a block of the box, or within 5 degrees of its center, on both the look the server last saw and this tick's - from any distance you can see it: an attack when a mob (player-model ones included) is in melee reach under your aim, otherwise the swing alone, which is what a real left click sends there. **Trigger Offset** (-10 to 10 ticks, -1 by default) moves that click: below 0 it clicks that many ticks after the kill timer, above 0 that many before it; the old Hit Lead does not carry over. With **Auto Detect Lag** (on) the kill timer allows for your measured ping, as the box does; off, the offset counts from the countdown's own end. If a mob stands on the spot and the hit did no damage (no hurt flash and still alive one round trip plus 4 ticks later), or the click was a swing because the mob was not in reach yet and now is, it clicks again straight away, up to 4 more times, 2-4 ticks apart; a mob that was hit is clicked once. Nothing is attacked before the Kill Popup's kill timer either: once the Watcher has greeted the room, neither automation clicks until "KILL" shows (the Watcher's predicted move less Kill Popup Lead), moved by the same Trigger Offset (-1: one tick after it); a mob whose own countdown ended earlier is clicked then. Without a greeting (joined mid-blood) there is no kill timer to wait for. After that the Trigger Bot keeps going: any blood mob in the Blood Room that is not on its way to a landing spot - landed while you looked elsewhere, walked off its spot toward you, or was held back by the kill timer - is clicked the first tick your look meets it, once (plus the same retries on a miss), at your weapon's pace, so the next mob is taken as soon as it is under your aim. In the tab, Auto Detect Lag, Trigger Offset and Never Miss sit under a small red **Attack Speed** header below the Trigger Bot and Aura switches (Freecam Mode moved up under the switches), shown whenever either is on: Auto Detect Lag and Trigger Offset time the Aura's first click on a mob the same way as the Trigger Bot's. **Never Miss** (off) does not use the timer for the first hit at all: it goes the tick a living mob is really standing on the spot, in reach under your look and in sight. **Aura** turns you toward the mob due next - its upper chest once it stands there, 1.5 blocks above the landing spot before - with a human-looking mouse movement: a reaction time first, then a turn that speeds up and slows down (longer for a bigger turn, never more than 26 degrees of yaw or 16 of pitch a tick), usually stopping a little short or a little past and correcting, a slight curve, a faint drift while resting, every step a whole number of mouse counts at your sensitivity, moved every frame rather than every tick. It also kills: once the mob it aims at is killable (the Trigger Bot's moment, or with Never Miss the tick it is there) it clicks it at your weapon's pace until it dies, at any range as long as nothing is in the way - with whatever you hold, the way your own click would: a Hypixel shortbow (its lore says "Shortbow") is a right click, anything else a left click (an attack when the mob is in melee reach under your look, otherwise the swing a mage weapon's beam fires on). The Trigger Bot clicks the same way. Between the hits that can land it also clicks the way someone spam-clicking would, at an uneven 7-10 clicks a second with the odd pause, but only well inside your weapon's cooldown (never within 2 ticks of the next hit, which still goes on the first tick it can), and only on Hypixel, where a hit inside the cooldown does nothing - and mobs already standing in the Blood Room when you come in (landed before you got there, or hit but still alive) are targets too, nearest to your aim first. Moving your mouse takes over at once: the Aura stops and waits until your mouse has been still for half a second. **Freecam Mode** (off, shown under the Aura switch) turns only your body while your camera stays where you point it; moving your mouse hands the aim straight back (body under the camera), and when nothing is left to aim at the body turns back under the camera the same way and the camera is released. Both act on Blood Camp's predictions, so Blood Camp itself must be on; the tab says so when it isn't.
- Auto Close Chest — **cheat build only** (since 2026-10-07; its own tab next to Auto Blood Camp). Instantly closes a real secret reward chest ("Chest"/"Large Chest"/"Trapped Chest") the moment it opens in a dungeon, before it's ever shown on screen. Secret Aura counts a chest closed this way as opened, so it does not click it again a second later, and Auto Secret leaves the room the tick it opens. Secret Aura, the auto puzzles' reward chests, Auto Boulder and Auto Tic Tac Toe's secret still see the chest as opened. A legit jar has no tab and no closing code, and a saved "on" from a cheat install does nothing there.
- Cheat Utilities — **cheat build only.** Wither ESP (F7 boss), Secret Aura (auto-clicks chests/levers/essence/redstone keys in reach, and puts a picked-up redstone key on its redstone block - the key is not an item since SkyBlock 0.18.4, so this is one plain right click on a side of the block open to air, with nothing in your hand that would place a block, once a key has gone within 10 blocks of you (yours or a teammate's) and the block is within the skull range; that placing is the room's key secret, and Auto Routes' awaits and Auto Secret count it, while the pickup counts for an await only; one your look misses gets your body, never your camera, turned to it for a tick first; essence and redstone key skulls within 4.7 of their center, chests and levers 4.5; nothing for 20 ticks after a world change; nothing while Auto Clear is killing with no Auto Routes route running; skips puzzle rooms where a click could fail the puzzle, and the Water Board and blaze chests until that puzzle is done; while an Auto Routes route is running it clicks nothing - no chest, trapped chest, lever, skull or key placing - unless the node you are on is waiting for secrets (an await that is not met yet; not a delay await, a crypt or bat node, a warp in flight or the walk between nodes), and with no route running it works as before; items are still picked up by walking), Auto GFS (sack refills; Hypixel Skyblock only, never p3sim; one /gfs at a time, waiting up to 5 s for the items before the next, at most 3 unanswered tries per item per world, and on a server that answers "Unknown or incomplete command" it stops until you join another server and says so once), Auto Ult (Healer/Tank at the right boss moments), and Auto Chocolate Factory (clicks the chocolate, claims strays, activates the Time Tower and buys the best-value upgrade, on the current menu layout; never clicks a menu-navigation slot) During a Shadow Assassin's leap (the world border moved off you, which makes the game refuse block clicks) Secret Aura holds its click, without spending a try, and clicks the tick the border comes back; after 5 seconds it clicks as before.
- Cheat Utilities — **cheat build only.** Wither ESP (F7 boss), Secret Aura (auto-clicks chests/levers/essence/redstone keys in reach, and puts a picked-up redstone key on its redstone block; one your look misses gets your body, never your camera, turned to it for a tick first; essence and redstone key skulls within 4.7 of their center, chests and levers 4.5; nothing for 20 ticks after a world change; nothing while Auto Clear is killing with no Auto Routes route running; skips puzzle rooms where a click could fail the puzzle, and the Water Board and blaze chests until that puzzle is done; never clicks a dummy lever - Red Blue's two wall levers beside the chest in the cage nook, only the floor lever outside the cage - at any rotation, and Secret Waypoints, Secret Triggerbot, Secret Sound and Auto Routes' secret counts ignore them too), Auto GFS (sack refills; Hypixel Skyblock only, never p3sim; one /gfs at a time, waiting up to 5 s for the items before the next, at most 3 unanswered tries per item per world, and on a server that answers "Unknown or incomplete command" it stops until you join another server and says so once), Auto Ult (Healer/Tank at the right boss moments), and Auto Chocolate Factory (clicks the chocolate, claims strays, activates the Time Tower and buys the best-value upgrade, on the current menu layout; never clicks a menu-navigation slot) During a Shadow Assassin's leap (the world border moved off you, which makes the game refuse block clicks) Secret Aura holds its click, without spending a try, and clicks the tick the border comes back; after 5 seconds it clicks as before.
- Solver highlights — each puzzle and boss solver (Boulder, Quiz, Ice Fill, Ice Path, Weirdos, Water Board, Creeper Beams, Blaze, Tic Tac Toe, Teleport Maze, Livid and the P4 Platform Highlight) has its own **Solver Highlight** switch, **Solver Highlight Through Walls** and, for those that draw waypoints, **Solver Highlight Style** (Outline or Full Block) at the bottom of its tab; the shared Solver Highlights tab was removed on 2026-10-18 and a file from before starts every solver on the old shared values. Highlights are drawn after water, so water behind them no longer hides them. Solver Highlight off: the solver still solves and its chat and HUD lines still show, only the world drawing stops.
- Boulder Solver — reads the real Boulder puzzle room's floor pattern and highlights the real stone button to press next, ported from a known 8-pattern solution database. Never clicks for you
- Quiz Solver — reads the real Oruo the Omniscient trivia question and lettered answer options in chat, looks up the correct answer in a bundled real question database, and highlights that option's answer pillar. Never answers for you. (Fixed 2026-10-01: the box was drawn one block low. Decoding the room settles what those three coordinates name - each is a smooth-stone block with air above it, ringed by four of the room's twelve wall buttons, which is all twelve - so they are the pillars Oruo's buttons hang off, not floor to stand on.) A question asked while you are out of the Quiz room is kept for when you walk back in (2026-10-17). The Quiz's progress (started, question N of 3, time until it can be answered) is one shared state every feature reads, driven only by Oruo's exact lines. **Quiz logger** (always on, log only, 2026-10-18): every line starts with `[QuizLog]` and carries the server tick and wall time - when you walked into the Quiz room and where, each of Oruo's lines, each question with the tick it opens by the shared state's estimate, each answer press (yours or Auto Quiz's, early or on time) with Oruo's reply or the lack of one, and the wrong-answer line. Written in dev and cheat builds, dropped by a release.
- Ice Fill Solver — identifies each of the real Ice Fill puzzle's 3 floor layouts and draws the real known-safe walking path across all of them. Never walks for you
- Weirdos Solver — reads the real Three Weirdos NPC dialogue lines and highlights the real correct chest (and, optionally, ruled-out ones) the moment a line gives it away
- Water Board Solver — identifies the real Water Board layout and shows a real live countdown above every remaining lever click, highlighting the soonest one. Never clicks anything for you The next lever is highlighted on the lever's own hitbox only
- Creeper Beams Solver — highlights the currently-connected Sea Lantern pairs in matching colors, live as panes are rotated. A pair turns red only when you've connected one of its lanterns to the wrong partner. Never touches anything
- Blaze Solver — ranks real blazes in the Lower/Higher Blaze puzzle by HP and highlights the correct next few kill targets in order. Never attacks anything
- Tic Tac Toe Solver — reads the Tic Tac Toe board and highlights just the button / item frame of the best move on your turn (minimax), as an outline or a fill. The optional prediction shows, on the computer's turn, the square YOU click next: the computer's best reply is worked out first, and the square is your best move after it (never the computer's own square), so you can spam it. **Prevent Misclicks** blocks clicks on every other square while one is highlighted (sneak to click anyway). Never clicks for you
- Teleport Maze Solver — tracks the pads you've used and narrows down the real exit pad from where each teleport makes you face (green = the one, gold = candidates), with a tracer from your crosshair to the best next pad. Never moves you
- Ice Path Solver — reads the silverfish Ice Path board and draws the shortest push path to the exit, outlining the silverfish's next stop. Never hits the silverfish
- Livid Solver (its own tab) — identifies the real correct Livid on Floor 5 from the wool color clue, re-checking it twice a second, and highlights it in that Livid's own color with an optional line to it, plus a countdown for its opening invulnerability window. Never attacks anything
- Architect's First Draft — when a puzzle is failed (its own fail line, whatever that puzzle's wording is - e.g. "PUZZLE FAIL! ...", or Quiz's Oruo announcing a wrong answer), posts a line in your chat you can click to get an Architect's First Draft from your sack. **Cheat build:** optionally gets it automatically, only when the fail line names you (Hypixel Skyblock only, and not on a server that answered /gfs "unknown command"; the click line is offered instead). Works in the dungeon sim too: a local world has no "PUZZLE FAIL!" broadcast and no sack, so the sim's puzzles report their own fails and the draft is handed over directly rather than fetched — the same two settings, and in the sim the cheat-build restriction does not apply because there is no server to automate against

- Class Selection Overlay — on the class-selection screen, a bar across the top showing every party member and the class they're currently on, and a faint class-name watermark in each of the five boxes with whoever is assigned to it written over the top. Click a name then a box to assign. The screen is found by its contents rather than by slot positions, because no layout for those five icons is documented anywhere. Assignments write through the same class picks the Leap Order editor sets (`/class`), so every class-colored display in the mod agrees with it; an Edit Classes chip at the end of the bar opens that editor. Its switch is in the Leap Menu tab, next to an Edit Classes button
- Auto Sell — runs by itself on any sell screen while it is on (there is no start or stop command). Sells items from a list you build, with a never-sell list that always wins; there is no "sell everything else" fallback, so an empty list sells nothing. It uses a real shift-click, so if it ever misidentifies the screen it moves the item rather than selling it, and it only ever touches your own inventory slots. Add an item to the list with the Toggle Item key (set in the tab) while hovering it in any container - pressing it again removes it. `/autosell list` or the tab's Open Sell List button opens the list as a menu where you add by identity, Add Held, or click a row to remove it. Every stack is clicked once, so three swords of the same kind are three sells in one visit. An item added to the list while the sell screen is open is sold in that screen without reopening it. After each visit the log has a "[AutoSell] visit result" line and one line for every stack left unsold with the reason: not on the sell list, on the never-sell list, no identity, refused by the server, or timed out with no answer. Between two sells it waits a random 0-10 ticks, with a min and a max slider. **Cheat build only**, off by default
- Auto Anvil — in Hypixel's Anvil, puts two identical enchanted books in and combines them into the next level, one click at a time, waiting for the server to show each step before the next. It only ever pairs the same book at the same tier: same single enchantment, same level, and every other SkyBlock attribute equal, read from the item's data, never its name. Books with more than one enchantment, recombobulated books, stacks and protected items (Item Protect) are left alone. It only combines where the anvil can make the next level for that enchantment (two Sharpness IV make V, two Sharpness V make nothing, Looting stops at III, Infinite Quiver goes to X), and an enchantment it has no rule for is never combined. Lowest levels go first, so with Combine Results Again on four III books become a V. It stops with the reason in chat on anything unexpected (the anvil closed, a book did not arrive, a preview or result that is not the expected book, something left in the anvil). Settings: Combine Results Again, Close When Done, Min/Max Delay. **Cheat build only**, off by default

- Bazaar Advisor — legit and read-only: when a Bazaar menu opens it prints one recommendation in chat (what to buy, how many, the expected profit). Turn it on in Items > Bazaar Advisor. Set the answers with `/bzflip`: `npc` or `bazaar` (where it sells), `total` or `percent` (what it optimizes), `insta` or `order` (how it buys), `instasell` or `offer` (how it sells on the Bazaar). Insta Buy is capped by your inventory space; Buy Order by the purse only. Expected profit counts only what sells within a day, and for a Buy Order only what fills in a day: a buy order fills when someone Insta Sells into it, so its day is Hypixel's 7-day instant-sell volume over seven, times the share a top order gets against the other buy orders (halved at 25 of them). A big margin on an item that instant-sells a few times a day therefore ranks low, and an item with no instant-sell volume in the API counts as filling nothing. The recommendation states the fills a day. With `percent`, a Buy Order is ranked by that day's profit as a share of the coins the order ties up, not by its margin alone. Off by default.
- Bazaar Flipper — buys an item out of the Bazaar at instant-buy price and sells it to the `/trades` NPC, picking whatever product currently has the best return. **Cheat build only**, off by default and unbound by default. Your keybind starts it and **any key or mouse button you press while it runs stops it** - in the world, in Hypixel's Bazaar menus, on the reskin and in the amount sign alike (the stop message names the key; in a menu the key is swallowed except Escape, in the sign it is always swallowed so nothing gets sent). It also stops on a target purse, on nothing being profitable, on not having the coins, on the daily NPC sell limit, and on any menu it did not expect. The tab has the switch, the Start Key, Rank By, Target Purse, Speed and a note about `/bazaarflip scan`.
  - *How it prices a flip.* Two different Hypixel endpoints: the Bazaar order books, and `npc_sell_price` from the item resources list. For each candidate it walks the real instant-buy order book cheapest level first, so the rising price as a purchase eats through the book is paid for rather than ignored, and stops at the first of three limits — a level priced at or above the NPC price, a full inventory (36 slots at the item's real stack size), or your purse running out. `quick_status.buyPrice` is deliberately not used: it is a weighted average and matched the exact top of the book on only 628 of 1833 products when this was measured, so it overstates what a purchase realizes.
  - *Ranking.* Either **Percentage Margin** or **Total Profit Per Run**, and it always takes the best one by that ranking whose profit after fees is above 0 (the Min Profit Per Run slider was removed on 2026-10-16). A third option, profit per coin invested, was dropped because it orders identically to percentage margin.
  - *Fees.* Checked on the wiki: the Bazaar's tax (1.25%, 1% with the Bazaar Flipper Community Shop upgrade) is taken on sales TO the Bazaar, not on instant buys, and NPCs and `/trades` pay their price with no fee; a Booster Cookie changes no rate. So a flip's cost is exactly what the book walk pays and its revenue exactly what the NPC pays. The fees are written as constants (both 0) so a future one is one line. What Hypixel's Instant Buy page shows is not the cost, though: it quotes the book price plus 4% and refunds what the buy did not use (wiki, Bazaar), so the flipper takes a page price as book = quote / 1.04 before comparing it with the NPC price, and makes sure your purse covers the full quote, which Hypixel requires before it runs the buy.
  - *Live price check.* Each buy is checked against the live Instant Buy page. If the page shows the flip no longer pays, it buys nothing, leaves the Bazaar, rescans and takes the next best flip; that product is not tried again this session unless a fresh scan prices it lower than before.
  - *Daily NPC sell limit.* Hypixel pays at most 500,000,000 coins a day for NPC sales, resetting at 00:00 UTC (wiki, Shops and Coins). The flipper counts every "You sold ... Coins!" line for your account today (saved, so a restart keeps it) and never sizes a buy past what the NPCs will still pay for; at the limit it refuses to start and says when it resets. A limit line from Hypixel, or a sale the NPC refuses near the limit, stops it at once with the same message, and what could not be sold is not counted as revenue. Sales made in another client or before this build ran are not seen; then the limit line or the refused sale is what stops it.
  - *Target Purse* stops it (between cycles) once your purse reaches that number. You type it: plain digits with or without commas (5,000,000) or a number with k, m or b (750k, 2.5m, 1b). Empty or 0 means no target, text that is not a number is ignored (the label says "not a number"), and anything past a trillion is clamped. The box shows the saved value with commas.
  - *Failsafe for not having the money.* Your purse is passed into the sizing, so an item you cannot fully afford is simply costed at what you can afford, and the next best item can win on its own merits. If nothing is profitable it stops and says so.
  - *Profit tracker.* Each finished cycle goes into `/profit bazaarflip` (cheat build only - a legit jar has no such card or command): coins spent from Hypixel's "[Bazaar] Bought Nx X for C coins!" lines, coins received from its "You sold" lines, the tax (0), and the net, which is exactly what your purse moved by. Units bought and not sold earn nothing there. Like the other trackers it can show this account, all accounts, or a mod profile.
  - *Scanning.* Once on start, then once a minute **while running only**. Nothing scans in the background while it is idle. Every scan is a fresh download of the whole Bazaar, asking caches not to answer, and a snapshot whose own timestamp is more than 60 seconds old is fetched again and then refused; nothing from an earlier session or a test is ever used. `/bazaarflip scan [n]` prices the market once and prints the ranking without touching a menu.
  - *Menu Speed* slider (1-10) sets the gap between two consecutive automated actions in the menus — 700-1300 ms at 1, down to 160-310 ms at 10, drawn randomly inside that band each time. It does not change how much is bought and does not shorten any safety timeout.
  - *Sell Speed* slider (1-10, same scale, much faster) sets the gap between two sell shift-clicks: 150-220 ms at 1, down to 51-58 ms at 10.
  - *Topping up.* When the planned units are in, it buys more of the same item while the purse and the free room still cover it (at the scan's price plus the quote margin). When the Bazaar does not reopen after a Custom Amount buy, it sends `/bz` again if there is still room and coins.
  - *The cycle.* `/bz <item>`, the item in Hypixel's search results, **Buy Instantly**, then on the Instant Buy page it buys the exact amount the scan found profitable, capped by your free space, the NPC sell limit and Custom Amount's own "Buy up to N x." line. When **Buy only one!**, **Buy a stack!** or **Fill my inventory!** is exactly that amount it clicks that; otherwise it clicks **Custom Amount**, types the number on the first line of Hypixel's sign ("Enter amount / to order") and presses Done. Before that it bounds the price from the preset buttons (a bigger preset's average price is never below the custom amount's), and if that bound is not under the NPC price, or your purse cannot cover it, it shrinks the amount or falls back to the largest preset that is safe. If a **Confirm Instant Buy** page follows, it clicks the one button whose lore states exactly that amount at a price under the NPC's; anything else stops it. If the sign never opens, Hypixel asks again, or nothing arrives, it stops and says which. Hypixel cuts long menu titles ("Enchanted Pufferfish ➜ Instant"), so the page is recognized by any cut of "&lt;item&gt; ➜ Instant Buy" plus its buttons, and only ever as the product it was buying. Each buy waits for the items to arrive, and "You cannot afford this!" ends the buying. Then it closes the Bazaar, opens `/trades` and shift-clicks every unit to the NPC, waiting for each stack to go, and scans again. Every page is recognized by name, never by slot number, and anything unexpected stops it with the menu's contents in chat. The Bazaar reskin keeps drawing while it runs, showing each page it reaches with "Bazaar Flipper running - any key stops it" in the bottom bar; the flipper reads and clicks Hypixel's real menu underneath. It needs an active **Booster Cookie** (remote `/bz` and selling from `/trades` both require one) and refuses to start without.
  - *Autopilot* (off by default; the Autopilot row in the tab). The start key then runs a full Bazaar flipping bot instead of the cycle above: it places buy orders and sell offers, uses Insta Buy and Insta Sell where they pay, relists its own orders when they are outbid or expire, collects what filled from Manage Orders, and sells or lists what it collected. What it does follows your `/bzflip` answers (NPC or Bazaar, total or percent, Insta Buy or Buy Order, Insta Sell or Sell Offer) and the Bazaar Advisor's pricing: a buy order bids the best order + 0.1, a sell offer undercuts the cheapest offer by 0.1, sales pay the 1.25% tax, and an order is sized by how much of the product actually trades in a day and how crowded that side of the book is. On Hypixel's "How many do you want?" page it clicks the preset whose lore (or, without an amount line, whose name) states exactly the order's amount - **Buy only one!**, **Buy 4!**, **Buy 32!** and the other **Buy N!** buttons, or the older **Get me a stack!**, **A big stack!** and **One thousand!** - and otherwise **Custom Amount**, typing the number on the sign; Confirm Buy Order must then state that amount or nothing is placed. A new buy order takes at most a third of the purse above the reserve, Insta Buy only takes the sell offers below the sale price, and it insta-sells instead of listing when that nets about as much or the listing would take more than a day. An order outbid after a minute is relisted at the new best price, and canceled instead once the new price no longer pays or after five relists (a price war). It never touches an order that was already there when it started, and never flips an item you already carry (Sell Instantly and a Sell Offer would sell yours too). **Reserve** (typed like Target Purse; empty means a tenth of your starting purse) is never spent and it stops if your purse drops under it; **Max New Orders Per Session** (5, 10, 20, 50 or 100; relists do not count) ends a session once its orders are done. Buy orders are ranked by the profit they can make in a day (the margin times what fills in a day, capped by the order size), never by margin alone, and products with no instant-sell volume in the API are logged and treated as filling nothing. How many buy orders it puts up on one product follows the same daily fills: none under one fill a day, one while a single order (a third of the purse above the reserve, at most 71,680 units) covers the day, and one more for each further order's worth, at most 5; extra orders match the session's own top bid with **Same as Top Order** instead of outbidding it, and a relist matches a sibling order rather than bidding over it. When Hypixel says "[Bazaar] Placing orders is on cooldown for up to N minute(s)!" it pauses for those N minutes from the line's arrival (1 to 5, 1 when no number is given), drops the refused attempt without counting it toward Max New Orders Per Session (a relist's second half is placed after the pause), says so once and carries on. It also stops on any other Bazaar chat line that reads like a refusal or a limit, on any menu it did not expect, and on every safety the cycle has (any key, the click cap, a world change). Target Purse works the same way. With none of its orders open it stops and says why rather than waiting: "nothing on the Bazaar is profitable right now" when it holds nothing, or, selling to the NPC, "the daily NPC sell limit is reached" (what it holds stays with you, and it says when the limit resets); a start while the limit is reached is refused. It never trades on Bazaar prices more than three minutes old by Hypixel's own timestamp: it refreshes them first, and stops naming their age if the refresh fails. Sales go into `/profit bazaarflip`, each also logged as "Sold N item: spent, received, net": spent is what Hypixel's "Bought" line charged, or, when no Bought line came, the Instant Buy page's rounded quote, and the line then says it is an estimate; received is still estimated from the sale price. `tools/bazaarflip-sim` simulates the decisions offline over recorded or synthetic order books.
- Auto Kick — Party Finder only: while you queue, each player who joins your group through Party Finder has their time on the floor you are queuing looked up, and a slower one - or none at all - is kicked (`/p kick`) with a line in chat saying why. Off by default; there is no warn-only mode. Each floor has its own slider, 30 s to 4 min (Floor 7 to 6 min, Master 7 to 7 min). **Judge By** picks which time: S+ (the default), S, or S+ else S (S only for a joiner with no S+). **Floor Source** picks the floor: Detect Queued (the default) reads the dungeon type and floor shown on the Group Builder's "Select Dungeon Type" and "Select Floor" items once Hypixel says your party has been queued - Party Finder only, `/joininstance` and `/f7`-style shortcuts are not read - and uses the last run's floor until you have queued anything; Last Run is the floor of the last run you were in; Fixed uses the Fixed Floor setting. Queuing the Entrance leaves no floor, so nobody is judged. It never judges you, never acts while you are in a dungeon, and a lookup that fails kicks nobody. `/autokick populate [player]` (or the "Populate My Times From API" button) sets each floor to that player's (or your own) S+ time on it

**AP3 - automated F7/M7 Phase 3 (cheat build only)**

AP3 walks, aligns, leaps, uses items and times you through the F7/M7 Goldor phase (P3, sections S1-S4) along a route of *nodes* you place yourself. It only ever presses real keys - W/A/S/D, sneak, sprint, jump - and turns your real view, exactly like a player would; it never writes your position or velocity. When the server corrects your position it posts a chat line and plays the Correction Alarm, and AP3 carries on from where the server put you (since 2026-10-06; the old Stop On Server Corrections switch and the two-in-ten-seconds auto-disable are gone). Use it on your own risk - it is automation and against Hypixel's rules.

*How it runs*
- Every node has a small trigger box (0.5 or 1 block by default, or any size with w<n> l<n>). Walking INTO a box fires that node; the ones you are already standing in do not fire again until you leave and come back.
- Nodes fire one per tick in a fixed priority: Stopwatch, Stop, Align/Axis Align/Fast Align, Look, Terminal, Leap Counter, Walk/Run, Jump/Edge/Block, Boom, Leap. A queued node waits for the one before it. Look, Stopwatch and Use never wait: they go off on the tick you enter their box, whatever else is running (a Use waits only while something else is aiming you - an align, a Block, a Boom, a Path, a Leap or a Terminal). Walk/Run, Jump and Edge do the same unless a Stop, an align, a Leap or a Path is running or queued ahead of them.
- AP3 knows which section you're in and only arms that section's nodes. Force Dungeon (in the tab) lets you place and test nodes anywhere, and Test Mode runs nodes without waiting for terminals or teammates.
- Freeze View (Freecam): while AP3 turns your real yaw for an align or a walk, your screen keeps the view you had and your mouse moves only the view. Your model in F5 and the server see the real angle.
- Moving yourself (WASD/space) stops AP3 at once - except inside an Align, which takes your keys over until it has landed.

*Placing nodes* - `/ap3 add <type> [modifiers]`, or a keybind for each type
- Nodes snap to the nearest half block (a block center, or the line between two or four blocks) unless you add `precise`; the height is your exact feet height, so nodes on carpet or slabs sit on top.
- The angle recorded is where you are looking (or where your free camera looks while Freeze State is on). Only the nodes that use an angle keep it: Walk and Run keep the yaw, Look, Boom, Block and Use keep yaw and pitch, and every other node is saved without one.
- `/ap3 edit <n>` opens the node editor: position, box, wait, jump/edge and the fields that node's type actually uses. The type is a dropdown; picking another type adds or removes rows on the spot (switching an Align to a Look adds Yaw and Pitch, starting at your current view). A Use node's item is a dropdown of your hotbar - click the item to use, or "Held item / none"; an item saved earlier that is no longer on the bar still shows. Nothing is applied until Save.
- `/ap3 list`, `/ap3 undo`, `/ap3 redo`, `/ap3 delete [n]`, `/ap3 replace <n>`, `/ap3 clear`, `/ap3 reload`, `/ap3 stop`, `/ap3 testmode`. Routes are saved per config file in the killer560smod-ap3 folder; Choose AP3 Config switches between them. `/ap3 undo` now takes back the last add, delete, move, re-place, editor change or clear, whatever area it was in and as many steps back as you like (until the game closes or `/ap3 reload`) - it used to remember only the last added node; with nothing left it still deletes the last node of the chain you stand in. `/ap3 redo` (and its keybind) re-applies what undo took back; any new change clears the redo history. Sliders in the AP3 tab are not undo steps.

*Node types*
- **Align** - lands you on the node's point to within 0.001 of a block, typically in 6-9 ticks from entering at speed. It plans the fastest landing each tick, mixing turning your real yaw, every key combination and sneak taps, and only counts as done once you have actually stopped.
- **Axis Align** - for a node placed against a wall: walks you into the wall (which pins that coordinate exactly), then holds one key into the wall at a small angle that steers you along it until you stop exactly on the point, without ever pulling off the wall.
- **Fast Align** (`/ap3 add fastalign`) - an align built only for speed: each tick it plans the fewest ticks until you are standing still on the point (within 0.001), combining a camera turn to any angle with any key combo and sneak, so it brakes with a sneaking tap against your momentum instead of coasting to a stop. In testing it finishes in 3 ticks about 83% of the time and never takes more than 4, where the regular Align takes 8-11 (up to 16).
- **Path** (`/ap3 add path [start|end] [<step>]`) - the route optimizer. Each Path node is a numbered step: `/ap3 add path 2` or `/ap3 add path two`, and with no number it takes the lowest one still free (place 1 and 3, the next one becomes 2). Nodes that SHARE a number are one step to be done in any order - put both levers on step 2 and it works out whether to take them one after the other or, if their boxes overlap, run between them and take both on the same tick. Place Path nodes in order and it works out the fastest way to hit every one of them, from the first to the last, without skipping any: it searches the real movement model a tick at a time (key combination, yaw and jump) and plays the answer back through the same key-writing every other node uses. Modifiers: `exact` (keep the block relationship you placed it with - the hitbox covers the same blocks, on or off an edge - rather than a meaningless decimal match), `speed:<min>-<max>` (the speed window it must cross at, blocks per tick; `speed:0.4-` at least, `speed:-0.3` at most), `dir:<deg>` with `dirtol:<deg>` (the heading it must be traveling at), and `term` (stop here, wait for a terminal to be closed, and plan the next leg from rest - a term costs you all your speed, so there is no point arriving fast). A Block node's block is understood too: the spot it will place is treated as floor for two ticks of running (a bottom slab at most), and the route never places it itself. Whether it jumps is worked out, not assumed: the air only accelerates you at a flat 0.026 no matter your speed stat, so bunny-hopping wins at vanilla speed and loses badly at 550. The world is read into a snapshot of its real collision boxes each time it plans, and every tick of the search sweeps your 0.6x1.8 hitbox against them the way the game does - one axis at a time, with the same step-up search - so stairs, slabs and ledges are planned on what actually happens rather than on a height profile. That is what lets a route run up a staircase: it climbs half a block a tick and knows that each riser takes your speed with it, so it plans around the climb instead of expecting to keep sprinting through it. Blocks Breaker Aura is going to break count as air, lava is somewhere the route may not go, and the plan is drawn through the world at the heights it will really run at. Mark the two ends of a route with `start` and `end`: one section can then hold several separate routes and each knows where it stops, instead of running on into the next one. To extend a route, delete its `end` node and place a new one further along. A chain with no `end` marked anywhere behaves as it always did and runs to the last Path node in it. Each way of arriving at a route is searched ONCE.
