The first time you run it from a given spot the search is allowed to think hard - seconds, a wide beam, several differently-shaped searches, and a longer second attempt if the first ran out of clock - and the answer goes in the config folder; arrive that way again and it loads instantly and runs exactly the same, because a saved plan is never replaced once it exists. Arrive somewhere quite different and it searches once more and remembers that approach too. What it will not do is hand back a plan built somewhere else: a plan is a fixed schedule of key presses, and replayed from the wrong place it does not go to the node, it repeats the movements that went to the node from another one. `/ap3 regenerate` forgets the plans for the route you are standing in, so the next run searches again. When a route is too tangled to plan in one piece - a gap, then a ledge, then a climb - it is cut up and planned a piece at a time, landing on each cut point, because a route that pauses is worth more than a route that does not exist. A route that never leaves the ground always wins over one that jumps, however the tick counts compare, and running into the world costs the search real ticks so it will coast, walk or take off early to keep a clean line - though a collision that gains height is how you climb a step and is charged nothing.
- **No Go** (`/ap3 add nogo`) - a GHOST BLOCK: solid to the route planner, absent from the world. It never fires as a node and nothing is ever placed or sent; it simply tells the route there is a block here even though there is not, so the route can stand on it, step up it, be stopped by it and jump off it. Use it to build the geometry a movement needs when the room does not provide it. A full block on the node's own level, sized by the node's box.
- **Term Aura** (`/ap3 add termaura`) - steps on it and clicks a terminal in range once to open it, trying again two ticks later if nothing opened. A terminal your look misses gets your body - never your camera - turned to it first (Terminal Aura's Turn To Terminal setting), and the chain carries on under the terminal it opened, so a Terminal node after it takes over. It does not turn the Terminal Aura setting on or off - it is one pulse of it.
- **Walk / Run** - keeps you walking (or sprinting) in the direction the node was placed, until another Walk/Run (which takes over), a Stop or an align (which end it), a Leap (which drops all movement) or a Path (which takes the movement over). Every other node fires with the walk still going; while a screen a node opened is up (a terminal) the walk presses nothing and carries on when it closes. A Stop or align hit while another node is still busy ends the walk at once. Your real yaw snaps so W+A / W+D (45 Degree Strafe on, the faster diagonal) or W points exactly along the walk from the first tick. On the tick a jump goes in it faces straight ahead so the sprint-jump boost follows the walk, then strafes again in the air.
- **Jump** - jumps on the next tick after you enter it (waiting up to a second for the ground). Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Edge Jump** (`edge`) - jumps as late as the game allows when you run off a block: on the one-tick "coyote" window just after your feet leave it. Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Block** - places a block (the held block, else a slab, else any block in your hotbar) where you were looking when you placed the node, then swaps back to what you held. The block shows on your screen at once, so you can run across ghost slabs. Does not end a Walk/Run. Like Use, it places on the tick you enter its box: the swap and the place are the first things sent after the movement packet that put you there, and the swap back goes out once, the tick after.
- **Look** - turns your view to the node's angle on the tick you enter its box - the next frame already shows it - client-side only (no rotation is sent). During a walk only the view turns (the walk keeps its own heading underneath, as with Freeze View); when the walk ends your real rotation glides onto the look.
- **Use** - right-clicks along the node's angle on the tick you enter its box: the click is the first thing sent after the movement packet that put you in the box, swapping first to the item you held when you placed it if it is elsewhere in your hotbar (slot change and click in the same tick, like a hotbar key and a right click). The click carries the node's angle and the next movement packet reports the same angle, as a real client's does. A block in reach is clicked (a lever, a chest), otherwise the item is used into the air. Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Boom** - uses a Superboom on the block along the node's angle, on the tick you enter its box (swap and tap sent first thing after the movement packet that put you there). Does not end a Walk/Run.
- **Leap** - opens Spirit Leap and leaps to a class, a player, or your Fast Leap target. **Leap Counter** waits until that many teammates have leapt to you.
- **Terminal** - waits for the terminal you're at to be done (a Walk/Run carries on - put a Stop before it to stand at the terminal; nothing moves while the terminal is open). **Stop** - lets go of every key and waits until you've stopped.
- **Stopwatch** - the first one starts a timer, the next one stops it and prints the time. Name the starting one (`/ap3 add stopwatch s3`) and, with Send Stopwatch to Party on, the time goes to party chat as "s3 took 12.345s". The Stopwatch HUD shows the time while it runs and for 10 seconds after it stops, then hides until the next start (the HUD editor always previews it).

*Modifiers* (any node, after the type)
- `w<n> l<n>` (also `w.5`, `.5x.5`) - the trigger box size. `precise` - no snapping. `wait:<ms>` - hold the next node that long after this one. (The old `close` modifier is gone; chains saved with it still load and the node simply fires when you walk into it.)
- `jump` / `edge` - after the node has done its part, jump (or edge-jump). `/ap3 add run edge` runs at the node's angle and jumps at the edge.
- `ss` - the node fires only once Simon Says is done this run. Walk into it before then and it waits ("waits for Simon Says"), then fires the tick the Simon Says device completes, if you are still in its box; after that it fires as soon as you walk in. Also "After SS" in the node editor.
- Two Walk/Run boxes that overlap: the one you are more in the center of is the one that goes. Entering a second Walk/Run while you are still nearer the middle of the first does nothing yet; it takes over the tick you are more central in it.

*Display*
- Node boxes with arrows (walk direction), wall markers (axis align) and look rays; labels with number, type and details; a color per node type (or one color for all); Show Nodes and Show Node Lines to hide them; Node Messages (None / Simple "Added #3 Walk" / Detailed, which also shows the queue).

*Freeze State* (its own red tab; never use it on Hypixel)
- `/freezestate` freezes your character in place (no movement, no gravity) while your camera stays free - handy for placing nodes at an exact angle.
- Rewind 1 Tick / Forward 1 Tick keys: rewind puts you exactly where you were one tick before your current position (Rewind Memory sets how many ticks are kept); forward predicts - the game runs exactly one real tick from where you are.
- AP3 keeps running while frozen: each forward step runs one AP3 tick, and your own keys and mouse never move the character - only the nodes do.
- On Hypixel the step keys refuse (it would teleport you - an instant ban); only a typed /rewind works there, behind a large warning.

**Mining**
- Shelved until after the 2.0 release: the Crystal Hollows Map (and Interactive map, structure sharing), Profit Per Hour and Nucleus Run Profit trackers are not in the jar. Their code and this section's old text are in `shelved/mining/` in the repo.

**Diagnostics**

**General**
- Auto Join Skyblock — automatically runs `/skyblock` the moment you connect to Hypixel
- Etherwarp Overlay — while holding a real Etherwarp item, highlights exactly where you'd land (green if safe, red if not by default - both colors and Outline / Filled / Filled+Outline style are configurable) using a real voxel raycast. Never moves you — the real server still handles the actual warp (in General since 2026-10-04; it used to sit in Helpers). On p3sim.net it casts from Minecraft 1.8.9's sneaking eye height (1.54 instead of 1.27), which is what that server uses, so the highlight matches where p3sim actually puts you; everywhere else nothing changes. Auto Routes' etherwarp aims follow the same rule on p3sim. In Outline style only the edges of the faces turned toward you are drawn, so the box never shows through a stair's or slab's open part. The box is the target block's own shape (a chest's, a slab's, a carpet's) unless Full Block Box is on, and it shows the same on chests, ender chests and trapped chests as on any block: until 2026-10-15 a chest's model was drawn over it on 26.1.2 and only slivers at its corners were visible
- Smooth Teleport — when an Etherwarp, Instant Transmission (AOTE/AOTV), Wither Impact (Hyperion, Astraea, Scylla, Valkyrie) or Sinseeker/Leech teleport lands you, the camera glides from where it was to where you landed over a set time (50-500 ms, default 100) instead of snapping, in first person. Purely visual: you are already at the landing, and your crosshair, clicks and every automation aim from there from the first tick. A server position change that does not answer one of your own teleport uses (a lag-back, a correction, a warp) is never smoothed and cancels a glide in progress. Each teleport type has its own toggle. Off by default; in General, under Etherwarp Overlay
- Session Recorder — while it is on, writes what the dungeon features do to one file per session in `config/killer560/diagnostics/` (`killer560smod-sessionlog-<date>_<time>.log`), so the file can be sent back for analysis: Auto Routes, Auto Puzzles, Breaker and Breaker Aura, Secret Aura and the other auras, Arrow Align, Blood Camp, the map and Auto Clear, server corrections and the automations' progress lines, plus world joins and leaves. One line per event: time, client tick, ticks since that feature's last line, feature, event and the details the feature logs (positions, blocks, items, the server's answer or the timeout). It only writes a file: it never plays, clicks, moves or logs in anything. A file stops at 20 MB with a last line saying it was cut. If the session file cannot be opened or written, the tab says so. A "Recording" tag shows in the top right while it runs. It will not start in an instance that has a `killer560smod-session-recorder-disabled` file in `config/killer560/` (the deploy script puts one in the Dungeons instance). Off by default; in Misc

**Display**
- Borderless Fullscreen — F11 toggles between windowed and borderless fullscreen, never true exclusive fullscreen
- Fullbright — see clearly in dark areas without touching your real brightness setting
- Pack Disabler — every SkyBlock item drawn the way it looked before Hypixel's 2026 resource pack: its old item or player-head skin, drawn through whatever resource packs you use, so a pack's seeds stay that pack's seeds. Items that never had an old look (90 of them, mostly Galatea, Safari and Garden items) get the mod's own original textures, which your resource packs can replace too. Hypixel's pack itself still loads, because SkyBlock requires it, and only item looks change. Off by default, in Display Options. If Noamm's PackDisabler mod is installed this one stays idle and says so once
- Custom Crosshair — replaces the vanilla crosshair with one you design in its **Custom Crosshair** section of the General tab, CS2/Valorant style, with a live preview beside the settings (click it to change the background; the strip under it previews movement spread, recoil and target colors). Styles: Cross, T-Shape, X, Dot, Circle and Cross + Circle, a center dot for any of them, and each arm switchable. Every size is a slider - length, thickness, gap (negative overlaps through the middle), dot size, circle radius and thickness, rotation and overall scale - and is rounded to whole screen pixels, so thin lines stay sharp at any GUI Scale. Size Mode is GUI Scale (follows your GUI Scale, and your monitor while Auto Scale is on) or Screen Pixels (the same pixel size everywhere). Color with transparency from the color picker, Chroma (the same flowing rainbow as Enchant Colors, at the screen center), Invert Blend (vanilla's look), its own dot color, and an outline with its own thickness and color. Optional dynamic behavior: spread when moving, sprinting or jumping, a recoil kick when you swing, and a different color while aiming at an entity or a block in reach. Vanilla's attack indicator can stay or go, and it can hide in menus (chat does not count) and show in third person; F1 hides it as usual. Built-in presets, named presets saved in the config folder, and Copy Code / Paste Code to share a crosshair as a short code. Off by default; in both builds

**Accounts**
- Account Switcher — swap between your saved Microsoft accounts from the main menu's Swap Accounts page: every account is a full-width card with its skin head, its name, the account you are playing as standing out in the theme's accent (an accent-tinted card with a bold frame and side bar, its name in the accent and a solid ACTIVE pill), the proxy it connects through (No proxy, its own, or the Universal one) and its Hypixel ban status; click a card to switch to it. Copy ID (copies the account's session ID without switching) and Set Proxy / Edit Proxy sit at the right end of each card. Each account can have its own SOCKS proxy, applied automatically every time you swap to it. With six or more accounts a search box narrows the list (or just start typing); the list scrolls smoothly and the page fits any window size and GUI scale. The footer holds Back, Direct Session Login, Set Universal Proxy and the Universal switch
- Proxy Client — optionally route your connection through a SOCKS proxy; set it with Set Universal Proxy on the main menu's Swap Accounts page (this instance uses it), then flip the Universal switch to make every instance use it no matter what. Swap Accounts and its proxy and session login pages are drawn in the mod menu's theme; Swap Accounts Theme in Display picks Theme Default (follows the menu) or any preset or your Custom colors for them

**Home**
- A HUD editor to drag and resize the mod's on-screen elements wherever you want them. You can move exactly what you could just see: an element is draggable if its own setting is on **and** it actually drew on screen within the last ten seconds, so the dungeon timers are editable in a dungeon and the RNG Meter ranking is editable for ten seconds after you close that menu. An element that is switched on but has not been on screen recently is not shown - turn on Show Unseen to move those too. Anything switched off in settings is not listed at all. Every element starts fully on screen at any GUI scale or window size, and opening chat never hides the HUD (real menus still do). Each element's box is the size of what it draws, with the same small margin all round, and its preview sits exactly where it draws in game. Resizing works like a desktop window: drag a box's corner to resize it with its shape kept (the opposite corner stays put), and a stat bar's edges and corners change its length and thickness separately; the cursor turns into the resize arrow over a handle, and scrolling over a box still scales it. While dragging or resizing, a box snaps to other elements' edges and centers, the screen's edges and center, and the gap two neighbors in a row already have, with an orange guide line while it is snapped; holding Alt places a box freely for that drag. The editor's own options are not on the editor screen (which shows only the elements and one short hint line, gone while you drag; Escape closes it) but in Home's **HUD Editor** section: Snapping on/off, what it snaps to (Element Edges, Element Centers, Screen Edges, Screen Center, Equal Spacing) and Show Unseen. The Edit HUD keybind works over any screen - an inventory, a chest, a Croesus or Chocolate Factory menu, this menu - except while you are typing: chat, an anvil rename, a sign or book, a command block, any search box (the creative and recipe book searches, this menu's, the Bazaar's, the Auction House's, the inventory search bar) or any other focused text box, and while a keybind button is waiting for a key. Opened over a container, Edit HUD draws that container behind the boxes so the overlays drawn on it (Start Croesus, Start ETable, the Croesus Chest Profit panel, the Chocolate Factory info line) are placed where they really sit; Escape goes back to the container. Those container overlays are listed only when they were drawn in the last ten seconds - over their menu, or just after you closed it - and Show Unseen does not bring them back anywhere else. It does not open with an item held on the cursor. Health and Mana Bars' bars and texts are not in Edit HUD: **Edit Bars** (Home's quick buttons, beside Edit HUD, and on the Health and Mana Bars page) opens an editor with only them, every one that is switched on, where they move, resize and scale as described there, Predefined areas included
- HUD Scale — one slider, SkyHanni-style, that resizes everything the mod draws at once, from 5% to 300% in 5% steps (100% by default). Everything follows the slider live while you drag it, the menu it sits in included. On HUD elements it multiplies each element's own size rather than replacing it, so an element you set to 200% and then a HUD Scale of 50% draws at its original size, and goes back to 200% when the slider returns to 100%. It also sizes the mod's own menus and screens (this menu, its color pickers, the editors, browsers and the rest), the Bazaar and Auction House reskins, the center-screen popups and the DVD logos. Menus follow it between 50% and 300%, never grow past what fits the window (the whole menu stays on screen, centered, on a small window), and land on a whole number of screen pixels per GUI pixel when the setting is close to one, so the text stays crisp; clicks always land on what is drawn. The menu re-sizes when you let go of the slider. Not scaled by it: the HUD editors (they show elements at exactly the size they draw), the Custom Crosshair (its own size setting), and vanilla and Hypixel screens the mod does not reskin. Saved with profiles. Auto Scale (monitor) - the button under it, ON by default - also sizes everything the mod draws (every HUD element, the center-screen popups, and all of the mod's own menus: this menu, the AP3 and Auto Routes editors, the friends list, the AH and Bazaar browsers and the rest) to your window, so it all stays the same size relative to the screen on a 1080p, 1440p or 4K monitor and at any Minecraft GUI Scale. The reference is a 2560x1440 window at GUI Scale 3, where nothing changes at all; a 1920x1080 window at GUI Scale 3 draws at 75%, a 4K one at 150%, and the button shows the factor in use. The HUD Scale slider multiplies on top. HUD positions move with it, so a layout keeps its shape on every monitor and nothing is pushed off screen. The HUD editor itself is not scaled, so it always shows elements at exactly the size and place they draw in game. Saved with profiles
- The Home page (2026-10-15): the version line reads only "Version <mod version> · <cheat|legit>" (no Minecraft version, no build); eight quick buttons in a 4x2 grid (tools on top: Edit HUD, Edit Bars, Check Updates, Sweat Mode; links below: YouTube, Discord, GitHub, Features Doc; Reboot was removed, `/reboot` stays); the Menu Theme row ends with a Main Menu: Fancy / Simple chip that flips the main menu's scene; the Mod Settings cards are in a fixed order, not alphabetical: Mod & HUD, Click Sounds, Descriptions, Discord Rich Presence, Auto Update (then the red Cheat block). Sound Effects was a Home card from 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-20 and is now in Misc.
- Discord Rich Presence's options sit directly on the Home tab under the Mod & HUD settings, with no dropdown to open
- The mod menu remembers your scroll position, selected tab, and search text between opens

**Profiles**
- The Profiles page shows the active profile at the top (with Import beside it), a New Profile card with the name box, Save Current As New and New Blank Profile, then a card per saved profile with an Active badge on the loaded one and Rename / Load / Export / Delete (Rename turns the name into a box right on the card: type, then Save or Enter; a name another profile has is refused, and renaming the active profile keeps it active; Delete asks for a second click). Loading a profile shows nothing on screen, only the log. Save your current settings as a named profile, switch between profiles, and export one to a single `.zip` file to share with a friend: Export puts the zip in the profiles' share folder and opens that folder, so it is ready to drag into Discord. New Blank Profile makes a profile with every setting off (named from the name box, or Blank), without changing your current settings until you Load it; it keeps your recorded routes, etherwarp waypoints, class overrides and HUD layout, and leaves Skyblock Only and the HUD editor's options at their defaults (`/killer560 profile blank [name]` does the same). To receive one, click Import Profile: it opens the import folder, and any `.zip` dropped in is imported within a couple of seconds as a new profile named after the file. A profile carries everything you set up, not only the settings: your GIFs, crosshair presets, the scoreboard background image, AP3 routes, Breaker Aura configs, inventory layouts, the cringe lines and the sim's speed and keys all travel with it, and loading a profile replaces them as a set (a GIF the profile does not have goes away; a GIF on the HUD switches straight away). A blank profile starts with none of them except your AP3 routes and Breaker Aura configs. Left out on purpose: credentials and keys (session login token, per-account proxies, the proxy password, the Last.fm login, the Profile Viewer API key - your own stays when you load a profile), downloads and caches (the room database and room library, island graphs, the speech model, Auction House and item caches, the video browser's browser profile, Auto Update downloads, the /reboot work folder), logs and bug reports, and history (run history, Croesus and Experimentation logs, tracked players, Fairy Souls found). Profiles move between versions: a profile made on 26.1.2 or 26.2, on the cheat, legit or testing build, or on an older version of the mod, imports and loads on any other - settings a build does not use (cheat settings on the legit build) are kept and come back when it returns to a build that uses them, settings it does not know take their defaults, and a damaged or newer-version file is reported instead of breaking the import. A profile from before profiles carried files only changes settings when loaded. While a profile is loaded, every setting and file you change is saved into that profile too, so switching away and back keeps your changes

## Requirements

- Minecraft `26.1.2`
- [Fabric Loader](https://fabricmc.net/use/) `>=0.19.3`
- [Fabric API](https://modrinth.com/mod/fabric-api)
- Java `25`+
